SetElementStyle Method

  The ApiResponse.SetElementStyle method is a command used to dynamically set or modify a single CSS style property of a specific HTML element on the client's page. This action is performed in real-time without a page refresh and targets an element by its unique id. It is one of the most versatile methods for controlling the visual presentation of the UI from the server. Common use cases include hiding or showing elements (by setting the display property), changing text or background colors, adjusting dimensions (width, height), or modifying font properties (fontWeight).

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub SetElementStyle(elementId As String, style As String, value As String)
C#
public void SetElementStyle(string elementId, string style, string value)
Parameters:
  • elementId (String): Required. The unique id of the HTML element whose style will be modified.
  • style (String): Required. The name of the CSS style property to change (e.g., "display", "color", "backgroundColor"). For consistency and to avoid typos, it is recommended to use the constants provided in the HtmlStyles structure (e.g., HtmlStyles.display).
  • value (String): Required. The new value to assign to the style property (e.g., "none", "green", "#FFFFFF").
Usage and Example:

This method is perfect for providing immediate visual feedback to the user based on server-side validation or state changes.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<input type="text" id="userInput" placeholder="Enter text...">
<button id="validateBtn" onclick="ValidateInput()">Validate</button>
<div id="statusMessage"></div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ValidateInput() {
    var text = document.getElementById('userInput').value;
    var data = [
        { key: 'inputText', vlu: text }
    ];
    // Use 'this' as the server-side function name matches.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite

Public Class myPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function ValidateInput() As ApiResponse
        Dim inputText As String = GetDataValue("inputText")

        ' 1. Instantiate the response.
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' 2. Perform validation and set styles accordingly.
        If String.IsNullOrEmpty(inputText) Then
            ' If input is empty, set error message and make it red.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("statusMessage", "Input cannot be empty!")
            _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("statusMessage", HtmlStyles.color, "red")
        Else
            ' If input is valid, set success message and make it green.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("statusMessage", "Input is valid!")
            _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("statusMessage", HtmlStyles.color, "green")
        End If

        ' 3. Return the response.
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;

public class myPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse ValidateInput()
    {
        string inputText = GetDataValue("inputText");

        // 1. Instantiate the response.
        ApiResponse _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // 2. Perform validation and set styles accordingly.
        if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(inputText))
        {
            // If input is empty, set error message and make it red.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("statusMessage", "Input cannot be empty!");
            _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("statusMessage", HtmlStyles.color, "red");
        }
        else
        {
            // If input is valid, set success message and make it green.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("statusMessage", "Input is valid!");
            _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("statusMessage", HtmlStyles.color, "green");
        }

        // 3. Return the response.
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

SetElementStyleByName Method

  The ApiResponse.SetElementStyleByName method is a command that performs a bulk styling update on the client's page. It dynamically sets or modifies a single CSS style property for all elements that share a common name attribute. This method is highly efficient for applying a uniform visual change to a group of related elements without a page refresh. It is particularly useful for tasks like highlighting all invalid fields in a form by changing their border color, changing the text color of all items in a group, or simultaneously hiding multiple related sections by setting their display property to "none".

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub SetElementStyleByName(elementName As String, style As String, value As String)
C#
public void SetElementStyleByName(string elementName, string style, string value)
Parameters:
  • elementName (String): Required. The value of the name attribute shared by the group of HTML elements whose style will be modified.
  • style (String): Required. The name of the CSS style property to change (e.g., "borderColor", "color", "display"). It is recommended to use the constants from the HtmlStyles structure.
  • value (String): Required. The new value to assign to the style property for every matched element (e.g., "red", "#FF0000", "none").
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for applying consistent styling feedback to multiple form inputs. Imagine a form where several fields are required, and a "Validate" button should highlight all empty required fields.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Username: <input type="text" id="username" name="required-field"></p>
<p>Email: <input type="text" id="email" name="required-field"></p>
<p>Optional Notes: <input type="text" id="notes"></p>
<br>
<button onclick="ValidateForm()">Validate Form</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ValidateForm() {
    // Call the server function of the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}

        ' For this example, we'll just apply the error style directly.

        ' 2. Target all elements with name="required-field" and change their border color to red.
        '    This single command will highlight both the username and email fields.

        ' 3. Return the response.

    End Function

RemoveElementStyle Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveElementStyle method is a command used to dynamically remove a specific inline CSS style property from a single HTML element on the client's page. It identifies the target element by its unique id. This action reverts the specified style property to its default value or to the value defined in an external stylesheet, effectively undoing a previously set inline style. This method is the direct counterpart to SetElementStyle and is essential for toggling visual states, such as removing a highlight color, reverting a font style, or making a hidden element visible again by removing the display: none style.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveElementStyle(elementId As String, style As String)
C#
public void RemoveElementStyle(string elementId, string style)
Parameters:
  • elementId (String): Required. The unique id of the HTML element from which the style property will be removed.
  • style (String): Required. The name of the CSS style property to remove (e.g., "color", "backgroundColor", "display"). For consistency, using the constants from the HtmlStyles structure is recommended.
Usage and Example:

This method is commonly used to reset an element's appearance after a temporary state. For instance, a validation message that is initially red can be reset to its default color once the user corrects the input.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<input type="text" id="password" placeholder="Enter new password...">
<button id="checkStrengthBtn" onclick="CheckPasswordStrength()">Check Strength</button>
<div id="strengthMessage"></div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function CheckPasswordStrength() {
    var password = document.getElementById('password').value;
    var data = [
        { key: 'pwd', vlu: password }
    ];
    // Use 'this' as the server-side function name matches.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite

Public Class myPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function CheckPasswordStrength() As ApiResponse
        Dim password As String = GetDataValue("pwd")

        ' 1. Instantiate the response.
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' 2. Perform validation and set or remove styles.
        If password.Length < 8 Then
            ' If password is weak, set the message and make it red.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("strengthMessage", "Password is too short!")
            _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("strengthMessage", HtmlStyles.color, "red")
        Else
            ' If password is strong, set a success message and remove the inline color style,
            ' allowing it to revert to its default (or stylesheet) color.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("strengthMessage", "Password strength is acceptable.")
            _ApiResponse.RemoveElementStyle("strengthMessage", HtmlStyles.color)
        End If

        ' 3. Return the response.
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)

{
    public ApiResponse CheckPasswordStrength()
    {
        string password = GetDataValue("pwd");

        // 1. Instantiate the response.
        ApiResponse _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // 2. Perform validation and set or remove styles.
        if (password.Length < 8)
        {
            // If password is weak, set the message and make it red.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("strengthMessage", "Password is too short!");
            _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("strengthMessage", HtmlStyles.color, "red");
        }
        else
        {
            // If password is strong, set a success message and remove the inline color style,
            // allowing it to revert to its default (or stylesheet) color.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("strengthMessage", "Password strength is acceptable.");
            _ApiResponse.RemoveElementStyle("strengthMessage", HtmlStyles.color);
        }

        // 3. Return the response.
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

ReplaceElement Method

  The ApiResponse.ReplaceElement method is a powerful command for performing a complete substitution of an HTML element on the client's page. It identifies a target element by its unique id and replaces the entire element—including its opening and closing tags and all of its content—with a new HTML structure provided as a string. This is a more drastic operation than SetElementContents, which only modifies the inner content of an element. ReplaceElement is essential for scenarios where the element's tag type, core attributes, or fundamental structure needs to change dynamically. A common use case is replacing a simple "div" placeholder with a fully-featured, server-generated UI control like a Grid or TabStrip.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ReplaceElement(elementId As String, contentsOuterHtml As String)
C#
public void ReplaceElement(string elementId, string contentsOuterHtml)
Parameters:
  • elementId (String): Required. The unique id of the HTML element that will be completely replaced.
  • contentsOuterHtml (String): Required. A string of well-formed HTML that will replace the target element. This string must represent a complete element structure (e.g., <div id="newDiv">...</div>).
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for dynamically loading complex components into a designated area of the page. Imagine a dashboard with a button to "Load Sales Data," which should replace a simple loading message with a data grid.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<button onclick="LoadDataGrid()">Load Sales Data</button>
<div id="data-placeholder" style="margin-top:20px;">
    <p>Click the button to load data...</p>
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function LoadDataGrid() {
    // Call the server function of the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System.Data

Public Class myPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function LoadDataGrid() As ApiResponse
        ' --- Simulate fetching data and creating a Grid control ---
        Dim dt As New DataTable()
        dt.Columns.Add("Product")
        dt.Columns.Add("Sales")
        dt.Rows.Add({"Product A", "150"})
        dt.Rows.Add({"Product B", "220"})

        Dim salesGrid As New Grid(dt)
        ' CRITICAL: The new component must also have an ID so it can be targeted in the future.
        salesGrid.Table.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "sales-grid")

        ' 1. Instantiate the response.
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' 2. Target the element with id="data-placeholder" and completely replace it
        '    with the HTML generated by the new Grid control.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement("data-placeholder", salesGrid.HtmlText())

        ' 3. Return the response.
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.Data;

public class myPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse LoadDataGrid()
    {
        // --- Simulate fetching data and creating a Grid control ---
        DataTable dt = new DataTable();
        dt.Columns.Add("Product");
        dt.Columns.Add("Sales");
        dt.Rows.Add("Product A", "150");
        dt.Rows.Add("Product B", "220");

        Grid salesGrid = new Grid(dt);
        // CRITICAL: The new component must also have an ID so it can be targeted in the future.
        salesGrid.Table.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "sales-grid");
        
        // 1. Instantiate the response.
        ApiResponse _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // 2. Target the element with id="data-placeholder" and completely replace it
        //    with the HTML generated by the new Grid control.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement("data-placeholder", salesGrid.HtmlText());

        // 3. Return the response.
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveElement Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveElement method is a command that completely removes a specific HTML element and all of its descendants from the document on the client's page. It identifies the target element by its unique id. This is a destructive operation that permanently deletes the element from the DOM for the current page view, differing from simply hiding it (e.g., via SetElementStyle("display", "none")). This method is useful for permanently dismissing notifications, removing items from a list after a successful action, or cleaning up temporary UI elements that are no longer needed.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveElement(elementId As String)
C#
public void RemoveElement(string elementId)
Parameters:
  • elementId (String): Required. The unique id of the HTML element that will be completely removed from the DOM.
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for removing elements from the UI after a user interaction. A common scenario is deleting an item from a list.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<ul id="taskList">
    <li id="task-1">Task 1 <button onclick="CompleteTask('task-1')">Complete</button></li>
    <li id="task-2">Task 2 <button onclick="CompleteTask('task-2')">Complete</button></li>
    <li id="task-3">Task 3 <button onclick="CompleteTask('task-3')">Complete</button></li>
</ul>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function CompleteTask(taskId) {
    var data = [
        { key: 'idToDelete', vlu: taskId }
    ];
    // Explicitly call the server function of the same name.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}

RemoveElementByName Method

It identifies and completely removes all elements from the DOM that share a common name attribute. This is a destructive operation that deletes the elements and all their descendants for the current page view. As a one-to-many command, it provides an efficient way to clean up multiple related UI elements with a single server-side instruction. It is ideal for scenarios like dismissing all notifications at once, clearing a list of dynamically generated error messages, or removing all items from a temporary preview list.

Syntax:

This method is perfect for clearing multiple, non-contiguous elements from the UI. A great example is a "Dismiss All" button for a series of notification alerts.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<div id="alert-1" name="notification" style="border:1px solid orange; padding:5px; margin-bottom:5px;">Warning: System maintenance soon.</div>

SetIFrameContents Method

  The ApiResponse.SetIFrameContents method is a specialized command designed to dynamically load and render a complete HTML document inside an "iframe" element on the client's page. It identifies the target "iframe" by its unique id. This method is fundamentally different from SetElementContents, as it doesn't just change the content of the iframe element itself, but rather manipulates the separate document hosted within the iframe's sandboxed environment. This makes it the ideal solution for displaying live previews, rendering rich-text editor content, embedding dynamically generated reports, or showing external content without navigating the main page.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub SetIFrameContents(iFrameId As String, contentsHtml As String)
C#
public void SetIFrameContents(string iFrameId, string contentsHtml)
Parameters:
  • iFrameId (String): Required. The unique id of the "iframe" element whose internal document will be replaced.
  • contentsHtml (String): Required. A string containing a full and well-formed HTML document (including "html", "head", and "body" tags if necessary, though a simple body content string often works) that will be rendered inside the "iframe".
Usage and Example:

A perfect use case is creating a real-time HTML editor where the user can see a preview of their markup.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<textarea id="htmlInput" style="width: 100%; height: 150px;" placeholder="Type HTML here..."></textarea>
<br>
<button onclick="UpdatePreview()">Update Preview</button>
<h3 style="margin-top:20px;">Preview:</h3>
<iframe id="previewFrame" style="width: 100%; height: 200px; border: 1px solid #ccc;"></iframe>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function UpdatePreview() {
    var htmlContent = document.getElementById('htmlInput').value;
    var data = [
        { key: 'htmlToPreview', vlu: htmlContent }
    ];
    // Use 'this' as the server-side function name matches.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite

Public Class myPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function UpdatePreview() As ApiResponse
        ' Get the HTML content to be previewed from the client.
        Dim htmlToPreview As String = GetDataValue("htmlToPreview")

        ' 1. Instantiate the response.
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' 2. Target the iframe with id="previewFrame" and set its internal document's content.
        _ApiResponse.SetIFrameContents("previewFrame", htmlToPreview)

        ' 3. Return the response.
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)

    {

ReplaceText Method

  The ApiResponse.ReplaceText method is a command that performs a global or scoped find-and-replace operation on the text content of the client's page. Unlike methods that manipulate entire elements, ReplaceText targets and replaces specific occurrences of a string within the text nodes of the HTML document. This makes it an ideal tool for mail-merge style personalization, dynamic language translation (localization), or updating placeholder text after an action is completed. The search is case-sensitive, and the replacement can be scoped to a specific container element to improve performance and prevent unintended changes.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ReplaceText(SearchText As String, ReplaceText As String, Optional RootElementId As String = "")
C#
public void ReplaceText(string SearchText, string ReplaceText, string RootElementId = "")
Parameters:
  • SearchText (String): Required. The exact, case-sensitive string to search for within the text content of the page.
  • ReplaceText (String): Required. The string that will replace every occurrence of SearchText.
  • RootElementId (Optional): Required. The id of a container element. If provided, the find-and-replace operation will be confined to the text content within this element and its descendants. If omitted or an empty string is passed, the search will be performed on the entire "body" of the document.
Usage and Example:

This method is perfect for personalizing a generic welcome message with user-specific data after they log in or perform an action.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<div id="welcome-panel" style="border: 1px solid #ccc; padding: 15px;">
    <h3>Welcome, [USERNAME]!</h3>
    <p>Your account status is: [STATUS]</p>
</div>
<br>
<button onclick="LoadUserData()">Load My Data</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function LoadUserData() {
    // Call the server function of the same name. No data is needed for this example.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite

Public Class myPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function LoadUserData() As ApiResponse
        ' --- Simulate fetching user data from a session or database ---
        Dim currentUserName As String = "JaneDoe"
        Dim currentUserStatus As String = "Active"

        ' 1. Instantiate the response.
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' 2. Perform the text replacements, scoped to the "welcome-panel" div.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceText("[USERNAME]", currentUserName, "welcome-panel")
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceText("[STATUS]", currentUserStatus, "welcome-panel")

        ' 3. Return the response.
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)

    public ApiResponse LoadUserData()
    {
        // --- Simulate fetching user data from a session or database ---
        string currentUserName = "JaneDoe";
        string currentUserStatus = "Active";

        // 1. Instantiate the response.
        ApiResponse _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // 2. Perform the text replacements, scoped to the "welcome-panel" div.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceText("[USERNAME]", currentUserName, "welcome-panel");
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceText("[STATUS]", currentUserStatus, "welcome-panel");

        // 3. Return the response.
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

TableToggleRow Method

  The ApiResponse.TableToggleRow method is a server-side command used to dynamically insert or remove a new row directly beneath a specified row in an HTML table on the client-side, without a full page refresh. It is specifically designed to create "expand/collapse" or "master-detail" functionality. When this command is received by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function is executed. This function finds the HTML element on the page that has the id matching the TableCellElementId parameter. From this anchor element, the script traverses up the DOM tree to find its parent table row ("tr"). The function then checks if a specially marked "toggle row" already exists immediately after this parent row. If one exists, it is removed (the "collapse" action). If it does not exist, a new table row, whose content is defined by the contentsOuterHtml parameter, is created and inserted directly after the parent row.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub TableToggleRow(TableCellElementId As String, contentsOuterHtml As String)
C#
public void ReplaceText(string SearchText, string ReplaceText, string RootElementId = "")
Parameters:
  • TableCellElementId (String): Required. The id attribute of the specific element within a table cell (td) that the user clicks to trigger the toggle action.
  • contentsOuterHtml (String): Required. A string containing the complete HTML for the new row to be inserted, including the opening and closing "tr" tags. This often includes "td" elements with a colspan attribute to span the width of the table.
Usage and Example:

This method is used within a server-side function to handle a client-side click event. The following example demonstrates how to show a user's email address in a new row when their name is clicked. This example uses a pure HTML file for the initial UI.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<table id="userTable" border="1" style="width:100%;">
    <thead>
        <tr>
            <th>User ID</th>
            <th>Name (Click to see details)</th>
        </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
        <tr>
            <td>101</td>
            <td>
                <span id="user-cell-101" onclick="ShowUserDetails(this.id)" style="cursor:pointer;">Alice</span>
            </td>
        </tr>
        <tr>
            <td>102</td>
            <td>
                <span id="user-cell-102" onclick="ShowUserDetails(this.id)" style="cursor:pointer;">Bob</span>
            </td>
        </tr>
    </tbody>
</table>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowUserDetails(elementId) {
    var data = [ { key: 'elementId', vlu: elementId } ];
    // Use 'this' as the server-side function name matches.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}

        Dim clickedElementId As String = GetDataValue("elementId")
        Dim userId As String = clickedElementId.Split("-")(2)
        Dim userDetails As String = "Details for User ID " & userId & ": This user is an active member."

    {

        string userId = clickedElementId.Split('-')[2];
        string userDetails = $"Details for User ID {userId}: This user is an active member.";

AppendScript Method

  The ApiResponse.AppendScript method is a server-side command that dynamically adds a new "script" tag to the "body" section of the current HTML document on the client-side. When the browser receives this command, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function creates a script element, sets its src attribute to the provided URL, and appends it to the document. The browser then asynchronously fetches and executes the JavaScript file from that URL. This allows for the "lazy-loading" of JavaScript resources, which is useful for improving initial page load times by deferring non-essential or large scripts until they are actually required by a user action.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub AppendScript(scriptUrl As String)
C#
public void AppendScript(string scriptUrl)
Parameters:
  • scriptUrl (String): Required. The full or relative URL of the JavaScript file to be added to the page (e.g., /scripts/charting.js or https://api.example.com/library.js).
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for situations where a specific JavaScript library is only needed after a user interaction, such as clicking a button. The following example demonstrates loading a hypothetical charting library and then calling a function from that library to render a chart, all triggered by a single button click.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to load the charting library and display the chart.</p>

<button onclick="LoadChart()">Load Chart Library</button>

<div id="chart-container" style="width:400px; height:300px; border:1px solid #ccc; margin-top:10px;">
    <!-- The chart will be rendered here -->
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function LoadChart() {
    // This function simply calls the server-side function. No data needs to be sent.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    ' No OnInitialized() method is needed as the UI is defined in MyPage.html

    Public Function LoadChart() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the client to append the new script file.
        _ApiResponse.AppendScript("/scripts/my-chart-library.js")

        ' Step 2: Tell the client to execute a function from the newly loaded script.
        ' This ensures the library is loaded before the function is called.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("InitializeChart('chart-container');")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    // No OnInitialized() method is needed as the UI is defined in MyPage.html

    public ApiResponse LoadChart()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Tell the client to append the new script file.
        _ApiResponse.AppendScript("/scripts/my-chart-library.js");

        // Step 2: Tell the client to execute a function from the newly loaded script.
        // This ensures the library is loaded before the function is called.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("InitializeChart('chart-container');");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveScript Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveScript method is a server-side command that dynamically removes an existing "script" tag from the HTML document on the client-side. When the browser receives this command, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function searches the DOM for a "script" element whose src attribute exactly matches the provided scriptUrl. If a matching tag is found, it is detached and removed from the DOM. This action does not "un-execute" the script if it has already been run; any functions, variables, or event listeners defined by the script will remain in memory. The primary purpose of this method is to clean up the DOM by removing script references that are no longer needed, which can be useful in complex Single Page Application (SPA)-like scenarios to prevent potential conflicts or memory bloat from repeatedly adding and removing scripts.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveScript(scriptUrl As String)
C#
public void RemoveScript(string scriptUrl)
Parameters:
  • scriptUrl (String): Required. The exact src attribute value of the "script" tag to be removed from the page (e.g., /scripts/charting.js).
Usage and Example:

This method is typically used as a cleanup action after a user has finished interacting with a feature that required a dynamically loaded script. The following example builds on the previous AppendScript scenario. After loading and using a chart library, a "Remove Chart" button will not only clear the chart content but also remove the "script" tag that was previously added.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Load and remove a script dynamically.</p>

<button onclick="LoadChart()">Load Chart Library</button>
<button onclick="RemoveChart()">Remove Chart & Script</button>

<div id="chart-container" style="width:400px; height:300px; border:1px solid #ccc; margin-top:10px;">
    <!-- The chart will be rendered here -->
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function LoadChart() {
    // Calls the server-side function to add the script and render the chart.
    $ApiRequest(this);
}

function RemoveChart() {
    // Calls the server-side function to clear content and remove the script.
    $ApiRequest(this);
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function LoadChart() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the client to append the new script file.
        _ApiResponse.AppendScript("/scripts/my-chart-library.js")

        ' Step 2: Tell the client to execute a function from the newly loaded script.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("InitializeChart('chart-container');")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function RemoveChart() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Clear the content of the chart container.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("chart-container", "Chart has been removed.")

        ' Step 2: Tell the client to remove the script file reference from the DOM.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveScript("/scripts/my-chart-library.js")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

using skylite.ToolKit;

{
    public ApiResponse LoadChart()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Tell the client to append the new script file.
        _ApiResponse.AppendScript("/scripts/my-chart-library.js");

        // Step 2: Tell the client to execute a function from the newly loaded script.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("InitializeChart('chart-container');");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse RemoveChart()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Clear the content of the chart container.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("chart-container", "Chart has been removed.");

        // Step 2: Tell the client to remove the script file reference from the DOM.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveScript("/scripts/my-chart-library.js");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

AppendLink Method

  The ApiResponse.AppendLink method is a server-side command that dynamically adds a new "link" tag to the "body" section of the current HTML document on the client-side. When the browser receives this command, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function creates a link element, sets its rel attribute to stylesheet, its type to text/css, and its href attribute to the provided URL. The element is then appended to the document's head. This prompts the browser to asynchronously fetch and apply the CSS rules from the linked stylesheet, allowing for real-time changes to the page's appearance. This method is ideal for "lazy-loading" stylesheets, such as applying themes or loading component-specific styles only when they are needed.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub AppendLink(linkUrl As String)
C#
public void AppendLink(string linkUrl)
Parameters:
  • linkUrl (String): Required. The full or relative URL of the CSS stylesheet file to be added to the page (e.g., /styles/dark-theme.css).
Usage and Example:

This method is commonly used to implement features like theme switching. The following example demonstrates how to load different stylesheets to change the theme of a content area when a user clicks a button. Note the use of RemoveLink to ensure only one theme is active at a time.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click a button to change the theme of the content area below.</p>

<button onclick="ApplyDarkTheme()">Apply Dark Theme</button>
<button onclick="ApplyLightTheme()">Apply Light Theme</button>

<div id="content-area" class="content-box">
    <h3>Themed Content</h3>
    <p>The background and text color of this box will change based on the selected theme.</p>
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ApplyDarkTheme() {
    // Calls the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}

function ApplyLightTheme() {
    // Calls the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function ApplyDarkTheme() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' First, remove the light theme if it exists to avoid conflicts.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveLink("/styles/light-theme.css")

        ' Append the dark theme stylesheet to the document's head.
        _ApiResponse.AppendLink("/styles/dark-theme.css")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function ApplyLightTheme() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' First, remove the dark theme if it exists.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveLink("/styles/dark-theme.css")

        ' Append the light theme stylesheet to the document's head.
        _ApiResponse.AppendLink("/styles/light-theme.css")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveLink Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveLink method is a server-side command that dynamically removes an existing "link" tag (typically a stylesheet) from the HTML document on the client-side. When the browser receives this command, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function searches the DOM for a "link" element whose href attribute exactly matches the provided linkUrl. If a matching tag is found, it is detached and removed from the DOM. This action causes the browser to un-apply the CSS rules contained in that stylesheet, which can result in an immediate visual change on the page. The primary purpose of this method is to manage dynamically loaded stylesheets, such as unloading a previously applied theme before applying a new one, ensuring that styles do not conflict.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveLink(linkUrl As String)
C#
public void RemoveLink(string linkUrl)
Parameters:
  • linkUrl (String): Required. The exact href attribute value of the "link" tag to be removed from the page (e.g., /styles/dark-theme.css).
Usage and Example:

This method is most effectively used in conjunction with AppendLink for features like theme switching or managing optional styles. The following example demonstrates a complete cycle: a user can apply a "print-friendly" stylesheet to the page and then remove it to return to the default view.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the buttons to add or remove a print-friendly stylesheet.</p>

<button onclick="ApplyPrintStyles()">Apply Print Styles</button>
<button onclick="RemovePrintStyles()">Remove Print Styles</button>

<div id="content-area" class="content-box">
    <h3>Article Title</h3>
    <p>This is the main content of the article. When the print-friendly style is applied, the background and other screen-specific elements might be hidden or changed.</p>
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ApplyPrintStyles() {
    // Calls the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}

function RemovePrintStyles() {
    // Calls the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}

Imports skylite.ToolKit

ExecuteScript Method

  The ApiResponse.ExecuteScript method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to execute a raw string of JavaScript code. When the ApiResponse is processed on the client, the SkyLite framework's built-in JavaScript engine takes the string provided to this method and executes it in the global scope. This command is processed sequentially after any DOM manipulation commands (like SetElementContents or ReplaceElement) within the same response, ensuring that the script can reliably interact with newly added or modified elements.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ExecuteScript(jsScripts As String)
C#
public void ExecuteScript(string jsScripts)
Parameters:
  • jsScripts (String): Required. A string containing the raw, valid JavaScript code to be executed on the client (e.g., "alert('Process Complete!');").
Usage and Example:

This method is highly effective for triggering client-side actions that require dynamic data calculated on the server, especially when interacting with third-party JavaScript libraries that may not have simple, named functions for every operation. The following example demonstrates updating a chart (from a hypothetical chart library) with new data generated on the server after a button click.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to update the chart with new data from the server.</p>
<button onclick="UpdateChartData()">Update Chart</button>

<div id="chart-container" style="width:400px; height:300px; border:1px solid #ccc; margin-top:10px;">
    <!-- Assume a chart has been initialized here -->
</div>

<!-- Placeholder for the chart library -->
<script src="/scripts/my-chart-library.js"></script>
<script>
    // Assume this function from the library initializes our chart
    var myChart = new Chart('chart-container', [20, 30, 50, 15, 45]);
</script>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function UpdateChartData() {
    // Simply calls the server-side function to get new data and the script command.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function UpdateChartData() As ApiResponse
        ' Simulate calculating new data on the server
        Dim rnd As New Random()
        Dim newData As String = $"[{rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}]"

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Construct and execute the script string on the client.
        ' This calls the chart library's update function with the new data.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("myChart.update(" & newData & ");")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse UpdateChartData()
    {
        // Simulate calculating new data on the server
        var rnd = new Random();
        string newData = $"[{rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}, {rnd.Next(1, 100)}]";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Construct and execute the script string on the client.
        // This calls the chart library's update function with the new data.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript($"myChart.update({newData});");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveFunction Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveFunction method is a server-side command that attempts to remove a globally defined JavaScript function from the client's window object. When the browser receives this command, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes a delete window[functionName] or window[functionName] = undefined; operation. This effectively unsets the function, making it no longer callable. It is important to note that this does not remove the function's source code from a loaded script file; it only removes the reference to it from the global scope. This is a specialized command intended for advanced use cases in Single Page Application (SPA)-like environments where dynamically loaded scripts might define functions with conflicting names, or for cleanup to prevent functions from being called after their associated UI has been removed.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveFunction(jsFunctionName As String)
C#
public void RemoveFunction(string jsFunctionName)
Parameters:
  • jsFunctionName (String): Required. The name of the globally-scoped JavaScript function to be removed (e.g., "temporaryValidation").
Usage and Example:

This method is useful for temporary or contextual functions. In the following example, a special validation function is dynamically added for a specific task. Once the task is complete and a "Confirm" button is clicked, the server-side logic processes the data and then sends a command to remove the temporary validation function, cleaning up the global scope.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>
    This button will add a temporary validation function and then another button will remove it.
</p>
<button onclick="AddTemporaryFunction()">Add Temp Function</button>
<button onclick="UseAndRemoveFunction()">Use and Remove Temp Function</button>
<br /><br />
<div id="status">Status: No temporary function exists.</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
// This file is initially empty or contains other functions.
// The 'temporaryValidation' function will be added dynamically.

function AddTemporaryFunction() {
    $ApiRequest();
}

function UseAndRemoveFunction() {
    // Check if the function exists before trying to call it
    if (typeof temporaryValidation === 'function') {
        temporaryValidation(); // Call the temp function
        $ApiRequest();         // Now, call the server to remove it
    } else {
        alert('The temporary function has not been added yet!');
    }
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function AddTemporaryFunction() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Use ExecuteScript to dynamically define a new function on the client
        Dim scriptToAdd As String = "function temporaryValidation() { alert('Temporary validation logic is running!'); }"
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript(scriptToAdd)

        ' Update a status element on the page
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: 'temporaryValidation' function now exists.")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function UseAndRemoveFunction() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' After the function has been used on the client, remove it.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveFunction("temporaryValidation")

        ' Update the status element on the page
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: 'temporaryValidation' function has been removed.")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse AddTemporaryFunction()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Use ExecuteScript to dynamically define a new function on the client
        string scriptToAdd = "function temporaryValidation() { alert('Temporary validation logic is running!'); }";
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript(scriptToAdd);

        // Update a status element on the page
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: 'temporaryValidation' function now exists.");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse UseAndRemoveFunction()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // After the function has been used on the client, remove it.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveFunction("temporaryValidation");

        // Update the status element on the page
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: 'temporaryValidation' function has been removed.");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

ServerMethod Method

  The ApiResponse.ServerMethod is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to immediately make a new API request back to the server after the current ApiResponse has been fully processed. When the SkyLite JavaScript engine on the client finishes executing all other commands in a response (such as DOM manipulations or popups), it checks if a ServerMethod command exists. If it does, the engine constructs and sends a new $ApiRequest, targeting the specified server-side function (func) and including the optional parameter. This creates a server-initiated callback, allowing developers to chain server-side operations that depend on each other, without requiring any additional user interaction. It effectively turns a single client action into a two-step server-client-server sequence.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ServerMethod(func As String, Optional parameter As String = "")
C#
public void ServerMethod(string func, string parameter = "")
Parameters:
  • func (String): Required. The name of the next server-side function to be called in the subsequent API request.
  • parameter (String): Optional. A string value that will be sent as a parameter with the new API request. This value can be retrieved in the second server-side function using GetDataValue.
Usage and Example:

This method is useful for orchestrating multi-step processes. The following example demonstrates a common scenario: a user clicks a button to process an order. The first server-side function updates the order status. After that update is displayed to the user, the server immediately calls a second function to refresh a dashboard summary with the latest data.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to process the order. The status and dashboard will be updated sequentially.</p>

<button onclick="ProcessOrder()">Process Order #123</button>

<div style="border:1px solid #ccc; padding:10px; margin-top:10px;">
    <strong>Order Status:</strong> <span id="order-status">Pending</span>
</div>

<div style="border:1px solid #ccc; padding:10px; margin-top:10px;">
    <strong>Dashboard Summary:</strong> <span id="dashboard-summary">Total Processed: 5</span>
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ProcessOrder() {
    // The user's action triggers the first server-side function.
    // The second server-side function will be triggered by the server itself.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    ' First function called by the user's click
    Public Function ProcessOrder() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Update the order status on the client.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("order-status", "Processed")

        ' Step 2: Instruct the client to immediately call the next server function.
        _ApiResponse.ServerMethod("RefreshDashboard")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    ' Second function, called automatically by the client after the first response is processed
    Public Function RefreshDashboard() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Simulate fetching new data (e.g., from a database)
        Dim newTotal As Integer = 6 ' New total after processing the order
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("dashboard-summary", "Total Processed: " & newTotal.ToString())

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    // First function called by the user's click
    public ApiResponse ProcessOrder()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Update the order status on the client.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("order-status", "Processed");

        // Step 2: Instruct the client to immediately call the next server function.
        _ApiResponse.ServerMethod("RefreshDashboard");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    // Second function, called automatically by the client after the first response is processed
    public ApiResponse RefreshDashboard()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Simulate fetching new data (e.g., from a database)
        int newTotal = 6; // New total after processing the order
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("dashboard-summary", $"Total Processed: {newTotal}");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

ServerPageMethod Method

  The ApiResponse.ServerPageMethod is a server-side command that provides a powerful mechanism for cross-page communication on the server. It instructs the client's browser to immediately make a new API request, but unlike ServerMethod, it targets a public function on a different WebPage class within the same application. When the client finishes processing the current ApiResponse, it sends a new $ApiRequest back to the server. The SkyLite handler then instantiates the WebPage class specified by the type parameter and invokes the function named in the func parameter. The ApiResponse returned by this second function is then sent back to the client and executed. This allows for the creation of centralized utility pages or modular components that can be called from anywhere in the application, promoting code reuse and better separation of concerns.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ServerPageMethod(type As String, func As String, Optional parameter As String = "")
C#
public void ServerPageMethod(string type, string func, string parameter = "")
Parameters:
  • type (String): Required. The class name of the target WebPage to instantiate (e.g., "UtilityPage").
  • func (String): Required. The name of the public function to call on the target page.
  • parameter (String): Optional. A string value that will be sent as a parameter with the new API request.
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for calling centralized logic. In this example, we have a main page (MyPage) and a separate utility page (UtilityPage). When a button is clicked on MyPage, it first performs a local action and then calls a function on UtilityPage to get a server timestamp, which is then displayed back on MyPage.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to perform a local action and then get the server time from a different page's method.</p>
<button onclick="PerformAction()">Get Server Time</button>

<div id="status">Status: Idle</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function PerformAction() {
    // This function calls the server-side function on the current page (MyPage).
    $ApiRequest();
}

PopOff Method

  The ApiResponse.PopOff method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to close the currently visible popup window that was previously opened by PopUpWindow or PopUpElement. When the browser receives this command, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function identifies the active modal popup elements (typically a modal overlay and the popup container itself) and removes them from the DOM. This is a straightforward action command that does not take any parameters. Its primary function is to programmatically dismiss a modal from the server after a user action within that popup has been successfully processed.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub PopOff()
C#
public void PopOff()
Parameters:
  • none
Usage and Example:

This method is essential for managing the lifecycle of modal dialogs. A common workflow is to present the user with a confirmation dialog (PopUpWindow). When the user clicks a button inside that dialog (e.g., "Confirm"), an API request is sent to the server. After the server completes the action, it sends back a PopOff command to close the dialog, often along with other commands to update the main page.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to open a confirmation dialog.</p>
<button onclick="ShowConfirmation()">Delete Item</button>

<div id="status">Status: Waiting for action.</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowConfirmation() {
    // This function calls the server to generate and show the popup.
    $ApiRequest();
}

// This function will be defined in the HTML sent by the PopUpWindow command.
function ConfirmDeletion() {
    // This function calls the server to perform the action and close the popup.
    $ApiRequest();
}

StoreLocalValue Method

  The ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to store a key-value pair in its localStorage. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native localStorage.setItem(key, value) command. Data stored in localStorage is persistent; it remains on the user's computer even after the browser window is closed and will be available during future sessions from the same origin (protocol, domain, and port). Both the key and the value are stored as strings. This method is ideal for saving non-sensitive user preferences or settings (like a chosen theme, language, or layout configuration) from the server after a specific action has been completed.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub StoreLocalValue(key As String, value As String)
C#
public void StoreLocalValue(string key, string value)
Parameters:
  • key (String): Required. The unique name (key) under which the value will be stored.
  • value (String): Required. The string data to be stored.
Usage and Example:

This method is commonly used to save user preferences. The following example demonstrates how a user can select a theme (Light or Dark). The server processes the choice, provides immediate visual feedback, and uses StoreLocalValue to command the browser to remember this choice for future visits.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Select your preferred theme. Your choice will be remembered for your next visit.</p>

<button onclick="SetTheme('light')">Set Light Theme</button>
<button onclick="SetTheme('dark')">Set Dark Theme</button>

<div id="status">Current theme preference has not been set.</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
window.onload = function() {
    const savedTheme = localStorage.getItem('userTheme');
    if (savedTheme === 'light') {
        document.body.className = 'light-theme';
        document.getElementById('status').innerText = 'Your saved theme is Light.';
    } else if (savedTheme === 'dark') {
        document.body.className = 'dark-theme';
        document.getElementById('status').innerText = 'Your saved theme is Dark.';
    }
};

function SetTheme(themeName) {
    var data = [ { key: 'selectedTheme', vlu: themeName } ];
    // Call the server function, passing the chosen theme.
    $ApiRequest('SetTheme', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function SetTheme() As ApiResponse
        Dim selectedTheme As String = GetDataValue("selectedTheme")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the browser to store this preference in localStorage.
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userTheme", selectedTheme)

        ' Step 2: Provide immediate feedback to the user.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Theme preference saved: " & selectedTheme)

        ' Step 3: Immediately apply the theme by changing the body's class.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.body.className = '" & selectedTheme & "-theme';")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse SetTheme()
    {
        string selectedTheme = GetDataValue("selectedTheme");
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Tell the browser to store this preference in localStorage.
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userTheme", selectedTheme);

        // Step 2: Provide immediate feedback to the user.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", $"Theme preference saved: {selectedTheme}");

        // Step 3: Immediately apply the theme by changing the body's class.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript($"document.body.className = '{selectedTheme}-theme';");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

ClearStorage Method

  The ApiResponse.ClearStorage method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to completely erase all key-value pairs stored in its localStorage for the current origin. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native localStorage.clear() command. This action is irreversible for the current session and permanently removes all data that was previously saved using StoreLocalValue or any client-side localStorage.setItem() calls. This method is typically used for "reset" functionality, such as restoring all user preferences to their defaults or clearing cached data as part of a logout process.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ClearStorage()
C#
public void ClearStorage()
Parameters:
  • none
Usage and Example:

This method is useful for providing a "Reset All Settings" feature. The following example builds on the previous theme-saving scenario. A new button allows the user to clear all their saved preferences, including the theme, from localStorage.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>You can set and clear your theme preference.</p>

<button onclick="SetTheme('dark')">Set Dark Theme</button>
<button onclick="ResetSettings()">Reset All Settings</button>

<div id="status">Status: Default</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
// On page load, apply any saved theme.
window.onload = function() {
    const savedTheme = localStorage.getItem('userTheme');
    if (savedTheme) {
        document.body.className = savedTheme + '-theme';
        document.getElementById('status').innerText = 'Status: Saved theme (' + savedTheme + ') applied.';
    }
};

function SetTheme(themeName) {
    var data = [ { key: 'selectedTheme', vlu: themeName } ];
    $ApiRequest('SetTheme', JSON.stringify(data));
}

function ResetSettings() {
    // This function calls the server to clear localStorage.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function SetTheme() As ApiResponse
        Dim selectedTheme As String = GetDataValue("selectedTheme")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Store the user's choice.
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userTheme", selectedTheme)
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Theme preference saved.")
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.body.className = '" & selectedTheme & "-theme';")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function ResetSettings() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the browser to clear all localStorage for this site.
        _ApiResponse.ClearStorage()

        ' Step 2: Provide feedback to the user.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: All saved settings have been reset.")

        ' Step 3: Visually revert the page to its default state.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.body.className = '';")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

    {

        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userTheme", selectedTheme);
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Theme preference saved.");
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript($"document.body.className = '{selectedTheme}-theme';");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse ResetSettings()
    {

        // Step 1: Tell the browser to clear all localStorage for this site.
        _ApiResponse.ClearStorage();

        // Step 2: Provide feedback to the user.

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveLocalValue Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveLocalValue method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to remove a single, specific key-value pair from its localStorage. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native localStorage.removeItem(key) command. This action selectively deletes the data associated with the provided key for the current origin, leaving all other stored values in localStorage untouched. This method provides a more granular way to manage persistent client-side data compared to ClearStorage, and it is ideal for scenarios where a user resets a single preference or a specific piece of cached information is no longer valid.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveLocalValue(key As String)
C#
public void RemoveLocalValue(string key)
Parameters:
  • key (String): Required. The unique name (key) of the item to be removed from localStorage.
Usage and Example:

This method is perfect for allowing users to revert a single setting to its default state without affecting their other preferences. In this example, a user has preferences for both a theme and a font size. They are given the option to reset only the theme, leaving their font size preference intact.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Set and remove individual preferences.</p>

<button onclick="SetPreferences()">Save Both Preferences</button>
<button onclick="RemoveThemePreference()">Remove Only Theme Preference</button>

<div id="status">Status: No preferences set.</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
// On page load, apply any saved preferences.
window.onload = function() {
    const savedTheme = localStorage.getItem('userTheme');
    const savedFontSize = localStorage.getItem('userFontSize');
    
    if (savedTheme) {
        document.body.className = savedTheme + '-theme';
    }
    if (savedFontSize) {
        document.body.style.fontSize = savedFontSize;
    }
    
    document.getElementById('status').innerText = 
        `Status: Theme='${savedTheme || 'default'}' | FontSize='${savedFontSize || 'default'}'`;
};

function SetPreferences() {
    $ApiRequest();
}

function RemoveThemePreference() {
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function SetPreferences() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Store two separate preferences.
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userTheme", "dark")
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userFontSize", "18px")

        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Theme and Font Size preferences have been saved.")
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();") ' Reload to see changes

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function RemoveThemePreference() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the browser to remove only the 'userTheme' key.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveLocalValue("userTheme")

        ' Step 2: Provide feedback.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Theme preference has been removed. Font Size is still saved.")

        ' Step 3: Reload the page to see the effect.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

using skylite.ToolKit;

    public ApiResponse SetPreferences()
    {

        // Store two separate preferences.
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userTheme", "dark");
        _ApiResponse.StoreLocalValue("userFontSize", "18px");

        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Theme and Font Size preferences have been saved.");
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();"); // Reload to see changes

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse RemoveThemePreference()
    {

        // Step 1: Tell the browser to remove only the 'userTheme' key.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveLocalValue("userTheme");

        // Step 2: Provide feedback.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Theme preference has been removed. Font Size is still saved.");

        // Step 3: Reload the page to see the effect.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

SetCookie Method

  The ApiResponse.SetCookie method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to create or update an HTTP cookie. When the ApiResponse is processed on the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function manipulates document.cookie to store the provided key-value pairs. Unlike localStorage, cookies are sent back to the server with every subsequent HTTP request to the same domain, making them ideal for storing small pieces of data that the server needs to be aware of, such as session identifiers, authentication tokens, or simple user preferences. The method is overloaded, allowing for setting a single cookie or a list of cookies at once. The optional duration parameter controls the cookie's lifespan; if set to 0 or omitted, it creates a session cookie that is deleted when the browser is closed. If set to a positive integer, it creates a persistent cookie that expires after that many days.

Syntax:
VB
' For a single cookie
Public Sub SetCookie(key As String, value As String, Optional duration As Integer = 0)

' For multiple cookies
Public Sub SetCookie(keyVluList As List(Of KeyVlu), Optional duration As Integer = 0)
C#
// For a single cookie
public void SetCookie(string key, string value, int duration = 0)

// For multiple cookies
public void SetCookie(List<KeyVlu> keyVluList, int duration = 0)
Parameters:
  • key (String): Required. The name of the cookie.
  • value (String): Required. The data to be stored in the cookie.
  • duration (Integer): Optional. The lifespan of the cookie in days. If 0, it's a session cookie.
  • key (String): A list of KeyVlu objects, allowing multiple cookies to be set in a single command.
Usage and Example:

This method is perfect for remembering a user's name to display a personalized welcome message on their return. In this example, the OnInitialized method first checks for an existing cookie on page load. A user can then enter their name and click a button, which calls a server function to set the cookie and reloads the page to show the new welcome message.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<div id="welcome-message">
    <!-- This will be populated by the server on page load if the cookie exists -->
</div>

<hr />

<p>Please enter your name to be remembered:</p>
<input type="text" id="userNameInput" />
<button onclick="SaveUserName()">Save My Name</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SaveUserName() {
    var userName = document.getElementById('userNameInput').value;
    if (userName) {
        var data = [ { key: 'userName', vlu: userName } ];
        $ApiRequest('SaveUserName', JSON.stringify(data));
    } else {
        alert('Please enter a name.');
    }
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' This runs on every page load (GET request)
        ' Check if the 'userName' cookie was sent by the browser
        If Request.Cookies("userName") IsNot Nothing Then
            Dim savedName As String = Request.Cookies("userName").Value
            Dim welcomeLabel As New Label("Welcome back, " & savedName & "!")
            ' Use a method that won't get overwritten by the HTML file's content
            HtmlDoc.Body.SetAttribute("data-initial-load", "true") ' Example
        End If
    End Sub

    ' This function is called by the button click (POST request)
    Public Function SaveUserName() As ApiResponse
        Dim userName As String = GetDataValue("userName")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Set a persistent cookie that lasts for 30 days.
        _ApiResponse.SetCookie("userName", userName, 30)

        ' Step 2: Reload the page so the OnInitialized logic can read the new cookie.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.Web; // Required for HttpCookie

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // This runs on every page load (GET request)
        // Check if the 'userName' cookie was sent by the browser
        HttpCookie userNameCookie = Request.Cookies["userName"];
        if (userNameCookie != null)
        {
            string savedName = userNameCookie.Value;
            var welcomeLabel = new Label($"Welcome back, {savedName}!");
            // Because the HTML file overwrites HtmlBodyText, we'll modify the welcome-message div
            // This is a common pattern when working with hybrid static/dynamic pages.
            var script = $"document.getElementById('welcome-message').innerHTML = '{welcomeLabel.HtmlText()}';";
            HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript(script);
        }
    }

    // This function is called by the button click (POST request)
    public ApiResponse SaveUserName()
    {
        string userName = GetDataValue("userName");
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Set a persistent cookie that lasts for 30 days.
        _ApiResponse.SetCookie("userName", userName, 30);

        // Step 2: Reload the page so the OnInitialized logic can read the new cookie.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveCookie Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveCookie method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to delete a specific HTTP cookie. Technically, browsers do not have a direct "delete" command for cookies. Instead, this method works by commanding the client to overwrite the existing cookie with a new one that has the same name and path, but with an expiration date set to a time in the past (e.g., Thu, 01 Jan 1970 00:00:00 UTC). When the browser receives this instruction, it discards the cookie as it is considered expired. This is an effective way to invalidate client-side tokens or preferences and is a fundamental part of implementing features like user logout or resetting "remember me" functionality.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveCookie(key As String)
C#
public void RemoveCookie(string key)
Parameters:
  • key (String): Required. The name of the cookie to be removed.
Usage and Example:

This method is the logical counterpart to SetCookie. A primary use case is providing a "Forget Me" or "Log Out" option that clears a previously saved preference. This example continues the scenario from SetCookie, where a user's name is saved. A new button is added to remove that cookie.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<div id="welcome-message">
    <!-- Server will populate this on page load if the cookie exists -->
</div>

<hr />

<p>Enter your name to be remembered:</p>
<input type="text" id="userNameInput" />
<button onclick="SaveUserName()">Save My Name</button>
<button onclick="ForgetUserName()">Forget My Name</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SaveUserName() {
    var userName = document.getElementById('userNameInput').value;
    if (userName) {
        var data = [ { key: 'userName', vlu: userName } ];
        $ApiRequest('SaveUserName', JSON.stringify(data));
    }
}

function ForgetUserName() {
    // This function calls the server to remove the cookie. No data is needed.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' On every page load, check if the cookie exists.
        If Request.Cookies("userName") IsNot Nothing Then
            Dim savedName As String = Request.Cookies("userName").Value
            Dim script As String = "document.getElementById('welcome-message').innerText = 'Welcome back, " & savedName & "!';"
            HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript(script)
        Else
            Dim script As String = "document.getElementById('welcome-message').innerText = 'Welcome, Guest!';"
            HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript(script)
        End If
    End Sub

    Public Function SaveUserName() As ApiResponse
        Dim userName As String = GetDataValue("userName")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetCookie("userName", userName, 30) ' Set cookie for 30 days
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();")
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function ForgetUserName() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the browser to remove the 'userName' cookie.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveCookie("userName")

        ' Step 2: Reload the page to see the effect.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
