using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.Web;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // On every page load, check if the cookie exists.
        HttpCookie userNameCookie = Request.Cookies["userName"];
        if (userNameCookie != null)
        {
            string savedName = userNameCookie.Value;
            string script = $"document.getElementById('welcome-message').innerText = 'Welcome back, {savedName}!';";
            HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript(script);
        }
        else
        {
            string script = "document.getElementById('welcome-message').innerText = 'Welcome, Guest!';";
            HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript(script);
        }
    }

    public ApiResponse SaveUserName()
    {
        string userName = GetDataValue("userName");
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetCookie("userName", userName, 30); // Set cookie for 30 days
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();");
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse ForgetUserName()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Tell the browser to remove the 'userName' cookie.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveCookie("userName");

        // Step 2: Reload the page to see the effect.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("window.location.reload();");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

StoreSessionValue Method

  The ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to store a key-value pair in its sessionStorage. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native sessionStorage.setItem(key, value) command. Data stored in sessionStorage is temporary and specific to a single browser tab or window session. It is automatically deleted when the tab or window is closed. Unlike cookies, this data is not sent back to the server with every request. sessionStorage is therefore ideal for storing temporary, non-critical state that is only needed on the client-side for the duration of a user's visit, such as the state of a multi-step form, temporary filter settings, or whether an introductory message has already been shown.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub StoreSessionValue(key As String, value As String)
C#
public void StoreSessionValue(string key, string value)
Parameters:
  • key (String): Required. The unique name (key) under which the value will be stored for the session.
  • value (String): Required. The string data to be stored.
Usage and Example:

A perfect use case for sessionStorage is to ensure a "Welcome" message is shown only once per session. In this example, when a user first clicks a button, a welcome message is displayed. The server then sends a command to set a flag in sessionStorage. If the user navigates away and comes back within the same session, the server can be instructed not to show the message again because the flag exists.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to get a welcome message (it will only appear once per session).</p>
<button onclick="ShowWelcomeMessage()">Show Welcome</button>

<div id="status"></div>

<!-- A link to simulate navigating away and back -->
<br /><br />
<a href="MyPage.html">Reload Page</a>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowWelcomeMessage() {
    // Check if the welcome message has already been shown in this session
    const wasShown = sessionStorage.getItem('welcomeMessageShown');

    var data = [ { key: 'wasShown', vlu: wasShown || 'false' } ];
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function ShowWelcomeMessage() As ApiResponse
        ' Get the flag from the client's sessionStorage
        Dim wasShown As String = GetDataValue("wasShown")

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        If wasShown = "true" Then
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "You have already seen the welcome message in this session.")
        Else
            ' The message has not been shown yet.
            _ApiResponse.MessageBox("Welcome to our website for the first time in this session!")
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "The welcome message was just displayed.")

            ' Step 1: Tell the browser to store a flag in sessionStorage.
            _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("welcomeMessageShown", "true")
        End If

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse ShowWelcomeMessage()
    {
        // Get the flag from the client's sessionStorage
        string wasShown = GetDataValue("wasShown");

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        if (wasShown == "true")
        {
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "You have already seen the welcome message in this session.");
        }
        else
        {
            // The message has not been shown yet.
            _ApiResponse.MessageBox("Welcome to our website for the first time in this session!");
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "The welcome message was just displayed.");

            // Step 1: Tell the browser to store a flag in sessionStorage.
            _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("welcomeMessageShown", "true");
        }

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

ClearSessionValues Method

  The ApiResponse.ClearSessionValues method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to completely erase all key-value pairs stored in its sessionStorage for the current tab/window session. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native sessionStorage.clear() command. This action removes all data that was previously saved within that specific session using StoreSessionValue or client-side sessionStorage.setItem() calls, effectively resetting the session state on the client. It is a targeted way to clear temporary data, commonly used when a user completes a multi-step process, logs out, or explicitly clicks a "start over" button within a workflow.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub ClearSessionValues()
C#
public void ClearSessionValues()
Parameters:
  • None
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for resetting a temporary user state. In this example, a user progresses through a simple multi-step form. Their progress is saved in sessionStorage at each step. A "Reset" button is available, which uses ClearSessionValues to clear their progress and allow them to start the form from the beginning.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Complete the steps. Your progress is saved for this session only.</p>

<div id="step-1">
    <p>Step 1: Enter your name.</p>
    <input type="text" id="step1_name" />
    <button onclick="CompleteStep1()">Save & Go to Step 2</button>
</div>

<div id="step-2" style="display:none;">
    <p>Step 2: Enter your email.</p>
    <input type="text" id="step2_email" />
    <button onclick="CompleteStep2()">Finish</button>
</div>

<div id="status">Status: You are at the beginning.</div>
<button onclick="ResetProgress()">Reset Progress</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function CompleteStep1() {
    var name = document.getElementById('step1_name').value;
    var data = [ { key: 'name', vlu: name } ];
    $ApiRequest('CompleteStep1', JSON.stringify(data));
}

function CompleteStep2() {
    var email = document.getElementById('step2_email').value;
    var data = [ { key: 'email', vlu: email } ];
    $ApiRequest('CompleteStep2', JSON.stringify(data));
}

function ResetProgress() {
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function CompleteStep1() As ApiResponse
        Dim name As String = GetDataValue("name")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Store progress and update UI
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("step1_complete", "true")
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("user_name", name)
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='none'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='block';")
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Step 1 complete.")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function CompleteStep2() As ApiResponse
        Dim email As String = GetDataValue("email")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Store final step progress
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("step2_complete", "true")
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("user_email", email)
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: All steps complete for this session!")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function ResetProgress() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the browser to clear all sessionStorage.
        _ApiResponse.ClearSessionValues()

        ' Step 2: Reset the UI to its initial state.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='block'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='none';")
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Progress has been reset.")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

{

    {
        string name = GetDataValue("name");
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("step1_complete", "true");
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("user_name", name);
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='none'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='block';");
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Step 1 complete.");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
    
    public ApiResponse CompleteStep2()
    {
        string email = GetDataValue("email");
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        
        // Store final step progress
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("step2_complete", "true");
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("user_email", email);
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: All steps complete for this session!");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse ResetProgress()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Tell the browser to clear all sessionStorage.
        _ApiResponse.ClearSessionValues();

        // Step 2: Reset the UI to its initial state.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='block'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='none';");
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Progress has been reset.");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

RemoveSessionValue Method

  The ApiResponse.RemoveSessionValue method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to remove a single, specific key-value pair from its sessionStorage. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native sessionStorage.removeItem(key) command. This action selectively deletes the data associated with the provided key for the current tab/window session, leaving all other stored session values untouched. This method provides a more granular way to manage temporary client-side state compared to ClearSessionValues. It is useful for scenarios where a user undoes a specific action, a temporary flag is no longer needed, or one part of a multi-step form needs to be reset without affecting the others.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub RemoveSessionValue(key As String)
C#
public void RemoveSessionValue(string key)
Parameters:
  • key (String): Required. The unique name (key) of the item to be removed from sessionStorage.
Usage and Example:

This method is ideal for allowing a user to "go back" or "edit" a step in a workflow. In this example, a user fills out a two-step form. After completing step 1, they are shown their entry and given an "Edit Name" button. Clicking this button uses RemoveSessionValue to clear only the progress flag for step 1, allowing them to re-enter their name without losing any other session data.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<div id="step-1">
    <p>Step 1: Enter your name.</p>
    <input type="text" id="step1_name" />
    <button onclick="CompleteStep1()">Save Name</button>
</div>

<div id="step-2" style="display:none;">
    <p>Step 2: Your saved name is <strong id="saved-name"></strong>.</p>
    <button onclick="EditName()">Edit Name</button>
</div>

<div id="status"></div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function CompleteStep1() {
    var name = document.getElementById('step1_name').value;
    var data = [ { key: 'name', vlu: name } ];
    $ApiRequest('CompleteStep1', JSON.stringify(data));
}

function EditName() {
    // This function calls the server to remove the specific session value.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function CompleteStep1() As ApiResponse
        Dim name As String = GetDataValue("name")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Store the name and a completion flag.
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("user_name", name)
        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("step1_complete", "true")

        ' Update the UI to show step 2.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='none'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='block';")
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("saved-name", name)
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Name saved for this session.")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function EditName() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the browser to remove only the 'step1_complete' flag.
        ' The 'user_name' value will remain in sessionStorage.
        _ApiResponse.RemoveSessionValue("step1_complete")

        ' Step 2: Reset the UI to go back to step 1.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='block'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='none';")
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "You can now edit your name.")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

        _ApiResponse.StoreSessionValue("step1_complete", "true");

        // Update the UI to show step 2.

        _ApiResponse.RemoveSessionValue("step1_complete");

        // Step 2: Reset the UI to go back to step 1.
        _ApiResponse.ExecuteScript("document.getElementById('step-1').style.display='block'; document.getElementById('step-2').style.display='none';");
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "You can now edit your name.");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

PopUpElement Method

  The ApiResponse.PopUpElement method is a server-side command that displays a non-modal, auto-dismissing notification element on the client's screen. When the ApiResponse is processed, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function creates a "div" element, injects the provided contentsHtml into it, and positions it on the page (typically in a corner or at the top). Unlike PopUpWindow, this element does not block interaction with the rest of the page. It is designed to be temporary and will automatically fade out and remove itself from the DOM after a short, predefined duration. Its main purpose is to provide quick, unobtrusive feedback to the user in response to an action, such as confirming a successful save or displaying a brief warning.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub PopUpElement(contentsHtml As String)
C#
public void PopUpElement(string contentsHtml)
Parameters:
  • contentsHtml (String): Required. A string of HTML to be rendered inside the temporary popup element. This can be simple text or more complex markup.
Usage and Example:

This method is perfect for "toast" style notifications that don't require user interaction to be dismissed. The following example shows a "Copy to Clipboard" feature. When the user clicks the button, the text is copied on the client-side, and a server call is made to trigger the PopUpElement as confirmation.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to copy the link below.</p>

<input type="text" id="shareable-link" value="https://example.com/share/12345" readonly style="width: 300px;" />
<button onclick="CopyToClipboard()">Copy Link</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function CopyToClipboard() {
    // Get the text field
    var copyText = document.getElementById("shareable-link");

    // Select the text field
    copyText.select();
    copyText.setSelectionRange(0, 99999); // For mobile devices

    // Copy the text inside the text field
    navigator.clipboard.writeText(copyText.value);
    
    // Call the server to show the confirmation popup
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function CopyToClipboard() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Command the client to display the auto-dismissing popup.
        _ApiResponse.PopUpElement("Link copied to clipboard!")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse CopyToClipboard()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Command the client to display the auto-dismissing popup.
        _ApiResponse.PopUpElement("Link copied to clipboard!");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

PopUpWindow Method

  The ApiResponse.PopUpWindow method is a server-side command that creates a modal dialog box on the client's screen, blocking interaction with the underlying page content until it is dismissed. When the command is received, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function dynamically creates several "div" elements: a semi-transparent overlay that covers the page (or a specified element) and a container for the popup content itself. The contentsHtml parameter provides the full inner HTML for this popup container, allowing for complex layouts, forms, and interactive elements (like buttons with onclick events) to be rendered inside the dialog. The optional blockElementId can be used to scope the modal overlay to a specific container element instead of the entire page. Its primary use is for critical user interactions that require a focused response, such as confirmation dialogs, data entry forms, or detailed information displays.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub PopUpWindow(contentsHtml As String, Optional blockElementId As String = "")
C#
public void PopUpWindow(string contentsHtml, string blockElementId = "")
Parameters:
  • contentsHtml (String): Required. A string of HTML to be rendered inside the popup window. This can include text, SkyLite UI controls rendered to HTML, and elements with onclick events.
  • blockElementId (String): Optional. The id of a specific element to cover with the modal overlay. If omitted, the entire page is covered.
Usage and Example:

This method is essential for creating confirmation dialogs. The following example demonstrates a "delete" action. Clicking the main button opens a modal window asking for confirmation. The buttons inside this modal then trigger subsequent server-side actions, including closing the popup itself.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Click the button to delete the item (a confirmation popup will appear).</p>
<button onclick="ShowDeleteConfirmation()">Delete Item</button>

<div id="status-message">Status: Item is active.</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowDeleteConfirmation() {
    // This function calls the server to generate and show the popup.
    $ApiRequest();
}

// NOTE: The ConfirmDelete() function is defined in the HTML string sent from the server.
// It will be called by the 'Yes, Delete' button inside the popup.
function ConfirmDelete() {
    // This function calls the server to perform the deletion and close the popup.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    ' This function is called first to open the popup.
    Public Function ShowDeleteConfirmation() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Create the content for the popup window, including confirmation buttons.
        ' Note the onclick events for 'ConfirmDelete()' and the built-in '$PopOff()'.
        Dim dialogHtml As String = "<div>" &
                                   "<p style='font-weight:bold;'>Are you sure you want to delete this item?</p>" &
                                   "<button onclick='ConfirmDelete()'>Yes, Delete</button>" &
                                   "<button onclick='$PopOff()' style='margin-left:10px;'>Cancel</button>" &
                                   "</div>"

        _ApiResponse.PopUpWindow(dialogHtml)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    ' This function is called by the 'Yes, Delete' button inside the popup.
    Public Function ConfirmDelete() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' (Perform actual deletion logic here...)

        ' Update the main page to reflect the change.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-message", "Status: Item has been deleted.")

        ' Close the popup window.
        _ApiResponse.PopOff()

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    // This function is called first to open the popup.
    public ApiResponse ShowDeleteConfirmation()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Create the content for the popup window, including confirmation buttons.
        // Note the onclick events for 'ConfirmDelete()' and the built-in '$PopOff()'.
        string dialogHtml = "<div>" +
                            "<p style='font-weight:bold;'>Are you sure you want to delete this item?</p>" +
                            "<button onclick='ConfirmDelete()'>Yes, Delete</button>" +
                            "<button onclick='$PopOff()' style='margin-left:10px;'>Cancel</button>" +
                            "</div>";

        _ApiResponse.PopUpWindow(dialogHtml);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    // This function is called by the 'Yes, Delete' button inside the popup.
    public ApiResponse ConfirmDelete()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // (Perform actual deletion logic here...)

        // Update the main page to reflect the change.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-message", "Status: Item has been deleted.");

        // Close the popup window.
        _ApiResponse.PopOff();

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

MessageBox Method

  The ApiResponse.MessageBox method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to display a native modal alert box. When the ApiResponse is processed on the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's alert(message) command. This action creates a simple, system-styled dialog box containing the provided message and an "OK" button. The alert box is synchronous and modal, meaning it halts all other script execution and user interaction with the page until the user dismisses it by clicking "OK". Due to its blocking and un-stylable nature, it is best suited for simple, critical notifications or for debugging purposes, rather than for complex user interactions which are better handled by PopUpWindow.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub MessageBox(message As String)
C#
public void MessageBox(string message)
Parameters:
  • message (String): Required. The plain text string to be displayed inside the alert box. HTML markup will not be rendered.
Usage and Example:

This method is useful for providing immediate, simple feedback to the user after a server-side validation check. In this example, a user enters a value and clicks "Validate". The server checks the value, and if it's invalid, it uses MessageBox to inform the user of the specific error before they can proceed.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<p>Enter a password (must be 'secret123').</p>
<input type="text" id="passwordInput" />
<button onclick="ValidatePassword()">Validate</button>

<div id="status"></div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ValidatePassword() {
    var password = document.getElementById('passwordInput').value;
    var data = [ { key: 'password', vlu: password } ];
    $ApiRequest('ValidatePassword', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function ValidatePassword() As ApiResponse
        Dim password As String = GetDataValue("password")
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Perform validation on the server
        If String.IsNullOrEmpty(password) Then
            ' Use MessageBox for a simple, blocking alert.
            _ApiResponse.MessageBox("Error: Password cannot be empty.")
        ElseIf password <> "secret123" Then
            _ApiResponse.MessageBox("Error: Invalid password entered.")
        Else
            ' If valid, update the main page.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Password is valid!")
        End If

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse ValidatePassword()
    {
        string password = GetDataValue("password");
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Perform validation on the server
        if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(password))
        {
            // Use MessageBox for a simple, blocking alert.
            _ApiResponse.MessageBox("Error: Password cannot be empty.");
        }
        else if (password != "secret123")
        {
            _ApiResponse.MessageBox("Error: Invalid password entered.");
        }
        else
        {
            // If valid, update the main page.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Password is valid!");
        }

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

Navigate Method

  The ApiResponse.Navigate method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to perform a full-page navigation to a new URL. When the ApiResponse is processed by the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function sets the window.location.href property to the newly constructed URL. This action triggers a standard GET request to the new page, causing the current page to be completely unloaded and replaced by the new one. The method is overloaded to accept optional parameters, which are appended to the URL as a query string (e.g., ?name=value&name2=value2). This is the primary mechanism within the SkyLite framework for redirecting users after a server-side action, such as a successful login, form submission, or cancellation.

Syntax:
VB
' Navigate without parameters
Public Sub Navigate(pagename As String)

' Navigate with a single parameter string
Public Sub Navigate(pagename As String, params As String)
C#
// Navigate without parameters
public void Navigate(string pagename)

// Navigate with a single parameter string
public void Navigate(string pagename, string params)
Parameters:
  • pagename (String): Required. The name of the target page within the application (e.g., "HomePage" or "Dashboard").
  • params (String): Optional. A URL-encoded string of query parameters to append to the URL (e.g., "userId=123&status=active").
Usage and Example:

The most common use for Navigate is to redirect a user after a successful login. In this example, a user enters their credentials. The server validates them, and if they are correct, it sends a Navigate command to redirect the user to their dashboard page.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<h3>Login</h3>
<p>User ID:</p>
<input type="text" id="userIdInput" />
<p>Password:</p>
<input type="password" id="passwordInput" />
<br /><br />
<button onclick="AttemptLogin()">Log In</button>

<div id="error-message" style="color:red; margin-top:10px;"></div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function AttemptLogin() {
    var userId = document.getElementById('userIdInput').value;
    var password = document.getElementById('passwordInput').value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'userId', vlu: userId },
        { key: 'password', vlu: password }
    ];
    $ApiRequest('AttemptLogin', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function AttemptLogin() As ApiResponse
        Dim userId As String = GetDataValue("userId")
        Dim password As String = GetDataValue("password")

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Simulate credential validation
        If userId = "admin" AndAlso password = "pass123" Then
            ' If successful, navigate the user to the HomePage.
            _ApiResponse.Navigate("HomePage")
        Else
            ' If failed, show an error on the current page.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("error-message", "Invalid credentials. Please try again.")
        End If

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public ApiResponse AttemptLogin()
    {
        string userId = GetDataValue("userId");
        string password = GetDataValue("password");

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Simulate credential validation
        if (userId == "admin" && password == "pass123")
        {
            // If successful, navigate the user to the HomePage.
            _ApiResponse.Navigate("HomePage");
        }
        else
        {
            // If failed, show an error on the current page.
            _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("error-message", "Invalid credentials. Please try again.");
        }

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

NewWindow Method

  The ApiResponse.NewWindow method is a server-side command that instructs the client's browser to open a new browser window or tab and navigate it to a specified URL. When the ApiResponse is processed on the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function executes the browser's native window.open(url, '_blank') command. This action initiates a new browsing context, separate from the current page. The URL is constructed based on the provided pagename. This method is useful for displaying supplementary information, reports, or external resources without forcing the user to leave the main application page. It is important to note that the success of this command can be affected by the user's browser settings, as many modern browsers have popup blockers that may prevent new windows from opening without direct user interaction.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub NewWindow(pagename As String)
C#
public void NewWindow(string pagename)
Parameters:
  • pagename (String): Required. The name of the target page within the application to be opened in the new window (e.g., "PrintableView").
Usage and Example:

A common use case for NewWindow is to provide a "Print Preview" or a detailed report that opens in a separate, clean window, leaving the main application interface intact. In this example, a user is viewing a summary report. Clicking a "View Details" button calls a server function that commands the client to open a more detailed, printer-friendly version of the report in a new tab.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<h3>Sales Summary - Q3</h3>
<p>Total Sales: $50,000</p>
<p>Top Product: Laptops</p>

<button onclick="ViewDetailedReport()">View Details in New Window</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ViewDetailedReport() {
    // This function simply calls the server-side function.
    // In a real scenario, you might pass a report ID.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function ViewDetailedReport() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the client to open the 'DetailedReport' page in a new window/tab.
        _ApiResponse.NewWindow("DetailedReport")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

{
    public ApiResponse ViewDetailedReport()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Step 1: Tell the client to open the 'DetailedReport' page in a new window/tab.
        _ApiResponse.NewWindow("DetailedReport");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

DownloadFile Method

  The ApiResponse.DownloadFile method is a server-side command that initiates a file download on the client's browser. Unlike other ApiResponse methods that send JSON instructions, this command is a terminal action that takes over the entire HTTP response stream. The SkyLite framework intercepts this command, stops the standard JSON response process, and instead reads the specified file from the server's file system. It then streams the file's binary data directly to the client, setting the appropriate HTTP headers: Content-Type (based on the file's extension) and Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="...". This Content-Disposition header is what instructs the browser to prompt the user with a "Save As" dialog rather than attempting to display the file content in the browser window.

Syntax:
VB
' Download using a virtual path relative to the application root
Public Sub DownloadFile(virtualFilepath As String)

' Download using a physical path and specify the "Save As" name
Public Sub DownloadFile(saveAsName As String, filepath As String)
C#
// Download using a virtual path relative to the application root
public void DownloadFile(string virtualFilepath)

// Download using a physical path and specify the "Save As" name
public void DownloadFile(string saveAsName, string filepath)
Parameters:
  • virtualFilepath (String): Required. A virtual path to the file on the server (e.g., ~/reports/Q3-Report.pdf). The framework resolves this to a physical path.
  • saveAsName (String): Required. The filename that will be suggested to the user in the download prompt.
  • filepath (String): Required. The absolute physical path to the file on the server's disk (e.g., C:\inetpub\wwwroot\myapp\reports\Q3-Report.pdf).
Usage and Example:

This method is used whenever a user needs to download a static or dynamically generated file from the server, such as a PDF report, an Excel spreadsheet, or an image. The following example provides a button that allows the user to download a pre-existing PDF report from the server.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<h3>Quarterly Reports</h3>
<p>
    The Q3 Sales Report is available for download.
</p>
<button onclick="DownloadReport()">Download Q3 Report (PDF)</button>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function DownloadReport() {
    // This function simply calls the server-side function to initiate the download.
    // No data needs to be sent.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function DownloadReport() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Specify the virtual path to the file.
        ' A file named "SampleReport.pdf" must exist in a folder named "reports"
        ' in your web application's root directory.
        Dim reportPath As String = "~/reports/SampleReport.pdf"

        ' Step 1: Call the DownloadFile method. This will override the response.
        _ApiResponse.DownloadFile(reportPath)

        ' Although the response is overridden, we still return the object.
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)

using skylite.ToolKit;

{
    public ApiResponse DownloadReport()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Specify the virtual path to the file.
        // A file named "SampleReport.pdf" must exist in a folder named "reports"
        // in your web application's root directory.
        string reportPath = "~/reports/SampleReport.pdf";

        // Step 1: Call the DownloadFile method. This will override the response.
        _ApiResponse.DownloadFile(reportPath);

        // Although the response is overridden, we still return the object.
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

CenteringElement Method

  The ApiResponse.CenteringElement method is a server-side command that dynamically positions a specified HTML element in the vertical and horizontal center of the browser's viewport. When the ApiResponse is processed on the client, a built-in SkyLite JavaScript function locates the element by its id. It then calculates the dimensions of both the element and the current viewport and applies CSS properties (such as position: fixed, top: 50%, left: 50%, and transform: translate(-50%, -50%)) to achieve the centering effect. This method is particularly useful for positioning modal dialogs, popups, or loading indicators in the middle of the screen, ensuring they are prominently displayed regardless of the user's screen size or scroll position.

Syntax:
VB
Public Sub CenteringElement(elementId As String)
C#
public void CenteringElement(string elementId)
Parameters:
  • elementId (String): Required. The id attribute of the HTML element to be centered on the screen.
Usage and Example:

This method is often used in conjunction with PopUpWindow to ensure the popup appears perfectly centered. In this example, clicking a button first opens a popup window (which by default may appear at the top of the page) and then immediately calls CenteringElement to position it in the middle of the screen.

Client-Side HTML (or generated by OnInitialized):
<div style="height: 800px; background-color: #f0f0f0;">
    <p>This is a tall section to demonstrate scrolling.</p>
</div>

<button onclick="FocusOnLogin()">Jump to Login Form</button>

<div id="login-form-container" style="padding: 20px; border: 2px solid blue; width: 400px; margin-top: 50px;">
    <h3>Login Form</h3>
    <p>User ID: <input type="text" /></p>
    <p>Password: <input type="password" /></p>
</div>

<div style="height: 800px; background-color: #f0f0f0;">
    <p>This is another tall section below the login form.</p>
</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
ffunction FocusOnLogin() {
    // Call the server-side function to perform the centering action.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Function FocusOnLogin() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()

        ' Step 1: Tell the client to center the login form container.
        _ApiResponse.CenteringElement("login-form-container")

        ' You could also add other effects, like a temporary highlight.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("login-form-container", "box-shadow", "0 0 15px 5px rgba(0,0,255,0.5)")

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

    public ApiResponse FocusOnLogin()
    {
