        // Step 1: Tell the client to center the login form container.
        _ApiResponse.CenteringElement("login-form-container");
        
        // You could also add other effects, like a temporary highlight.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("login-form-container", "box-shadow", "0 0 15px 5px rgba(0,0,255,0.5)");

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

Featured Classes

The classes that make up the framework, and what each is responsible for.

SkyLite Framework: Featured Classes

1. Core Application & Request Handling
  • skylite.WebPage: The foundational class from which all server-side page logic must inherit. It orchestrates the entire request lifecycle through a series of overridable events (OnLoad, OnInitialized, OnRequest, OnResponse). It serves as the central hub, providing access to the HtmlDocument for page construction and containing the public methods that act as endpoints for client-side API calls. Its primary role is to manage the state and logic for a single webpage.

  • skylite.WebPage.HtmlDocument: The master object for building the final HTML response during a GET request. It manages the content of both the <body> (via HtmlBodyText) and <head> sections. Crucially, it provides methods like AddJsFile and AddCSSFile for linking external resources and an InitialScripts property for queuing JavaScript to be executed immediately upon page load.

  • skylite.ApiResponse: A command-based object that serves as the return type for all API functions (POST requests). It does not return data directly, but rather a collection of commands to be executed on the client. This list of commands (e.g., SetElementContents, Navigate, PopUpWindow, TableToggleRow) is serialized to JSON, sent to the client, and processed by SkyLite's built-in JavaScript library to perform real-time DOM manipulations and other actions without a full page refresh.
2. UI Toolkit (skylite.ToolKit)
  • UI Controls (e.g., Texts, Button, Dropdown, Grid, TreeView): These are server-side classes that abstract HTML generation. Each control is a composite object that programmatically builds a specific UI component (like an input field or a data table) by generating an HTML string via its .HtmlText() method. They provide an object-oriented model for setting attributes, styles, and event handlers (like onclick) from server-side code.

  • Layout & Container Controls (e.g., HtmlElementBox, Stacker, Wrap): These controls are designed to organize and structure other UI controls on the page. They function by either accepting control objects (HtmlElementBox) or pre-rendered HTML strings (Wrap, ContentsBox) and arranging them into a cohesive layout, often using vertical stacking or horizontal (Flexbox-based) columns.

  • "Meta" Controls (UIControl, UIForm): These are high-level, metadata-driven controls that automate the creation of entire forms. They take a collection of definition objects (UIControl.Item or UIForm.Element) as input and render a complete set of input fields, labels, and sections based on the specified properties (e.g., UIType, Label, IsRequired). They represent the highest level of abstraction for form generation in the toolkit.
3. Data Handling & Utilities
  • skylite.SQLData / OraData / OleData: A suite of dedicated data access classes that provide a simplified and consistent API for interacting with different database providers (MS SQL Server, Oracle, and OLE DB sources, respectively). They encapsulate the underlying ADO.NET logic for executing queries and data manipulation commands, returning results in standard .NET data objects like DataTable and DataSet.

  • skylite.OptionValues: A crucial helper class that acts as a data provider for selection-based UI controls (Dropdown, Radio, CheckBox, etc.). Its key feature is its ability to be populated from multiple sources, including a programmatic list, a database query string, or a compact delimited string, making it a flexible tool for managing lists of choices.

  • skylite.Encrypt: A core security utility providing strong, key-based, two-way encryption and decryption. It is used to secure sensitive data for transport in URLs, cookies, or for storage.
Architectural Summary:

The SkyLite framework employs a server-centric architecture where the UI is defined and manipulated primarily through server-side C# or VB.NET code. Client-server communication is handled asynchronously via a lightweight JavaScript bridge ($ApiRequest), which invokes server methods that return a set of declarative commands (ApiResponse) to be executed on the client. This model provides the power and security of server-side logic while delivering a responsive, modern user experience.

WebCore

Application-wide services: paths, request values, files, images and XML.

skylite.WebCore

  The WebCore class serves as the fundamental base class for the entire framework, providing a rich set of low-level utilities, helper functions, and environment properties. It is not a UI control, but rather the core engine that WebPage inherits from, making its extensive functionality available to every server-side page class. Its purpose is to handle common web development tasks, from security and data manipulation to file system access and request handling, thereby abstracting away boilerplate code and providing a consistent API for the developer.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Request & Parameter Handling: This is a primary function for API calls.
    • GetDataValue(key) / GetDataValues(key): The primary methods for retrieving data sent from the client via $ApiRequest.
    • QueryValue(key) / ParamValue(key): Methods for specifically getting data from the URL's query string or other parameter sources.
    • HeaderValue(key): Retrieves values from the HTTP request headers.
  • Security & Encryption: The framework has strong, built-in security features.
    • EncryptString(string) / DecryptString(string): Core methods for two-way encryption and decryption of data.
    • RemoveXSS(string): A crucial utility to sanitize strings and prevent Cross-Site Scripting attacks.
    • ValidatePassword(...): A built-in method to enforce password complexity rules.
    • Encryptor As skylite.Encrypt: A property that provides access to the main encryption object and its settings.
  • File System & Path Management: Provides consistent access to the application's directory structure.
    • Properties like BinFolder, ScriptFolder, StyleFolder, DataFolder, LogFolder, HtmlPath, etc.: These properties return the absolute server paths to the standard SkyLite project folders, eliminating the need for manual path resolution.
    • ReadHtmlFile(path) / ReadTextFile(path): Methods to read the contents of files directly from the server's disk.
  • Serialization & Deserialization:
    • SerializeObject(...) / DeserializeObject(...): Methods for converting .NET objects to and from string representations (likely JSON or a custom format), essential for storing complex data or for transport.
  • .NET Reflection and Type Introspection:Advanced methods for working with types at runtime.
    • AssemblyTypes(), CoreTypeMethods(), TypeFields(), CreateDynamicType(...): These functions allow the developer or the framework itself to inspect assemblies, get information about classes and their members, and even create types on the fly.
  • Database Utilities:
    • SqlWithParams(...): A helper method for safely constructing parameterized SQL queries, which is the correct practice to prevent SQL injection vulnerabilities.
  • Environment & Session Information:
    • Properties like ClientIPAddress, ClientAgent, ServerTime, WebAppName, RequestPath, RequestReferrer: These provide easy access to contextual information about the user's request, their browser, and the server environment.
  • General Utility Functions: A comprehensive toolbox of helper methods.
    • ExtractNumber(string), RemoveSpecialChar(string): For string sanitization and manipulation.
    • GetGeoDistanceBetween(...): For performing geographical distance calculations.
    • RandNUM(integer): For generating random numbers.
    • CreateWorkBook(...): Appears to be a powerful utility for generating Excel workbooks from a DataTable.

  In summary, the WebCore class is the heart of the SkyLite framework, providing the essential, non-UI-related functionality that powers every page. It handles security, data access, file management, and a multitude of other common tasks, allowing the developer to focus on the business logic within their WebPage classes and UI controls.

skylite.WebCore.FileHandler

  The FileHandler class is a utility within the WebCore namespace that provides a set of convenient methods for interacting with the server's file system. It abstracts and simplifies common I/O (Input/Output) operations, making it easier and safer to manage files and directories from within a WebPage class. Its functionality ranges from reading and writing binary or serialized object data to more complex operations like directory searching and ZIP archive creation.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • File I/O:
    • WriteByteToFile(path, byte()): Writes a raw byte array to a file, which is ideal for saving uploaded files, images, or any binary data.
    • ReadByteFromFile(path): Reads an entire file into a byte array.
    • WriteObjectToFile(path, object): Serializes a .NET object and writes it to a file, perfect for saving application state or complex data structures.
    • ReadObjectFromFile(path): Reads a file and deserializes its contents back into a .NET object.
  • Directory and File Management:
    • GetDirectoryFiles(path): Retrieves a list of files within a specified directory. The overloads suggest it can return different collection types (e.g., Dictionary or List).
    • SearchFiles(path, pattern): Searches for files within a directory that match a specific pattern (e.g., "*.log").
    • DeleteFiles(path, ageInDays): A utility method for cleaning up files, likely deleting files in a directory that are older than a specified number of days.
  • Archiving:
    • CreateZipFromDir(directoryPath, zipFilePath): A powerful method that takes an entire directory on the server and compresses its contents into a single .zip file. This is extremely useful for creating downloadable archives of documents, images, or log files.

  In summary, the FileHandler class is the go-to utility in the SkyLite framework for any server-side logic that needs to read from, write to, or manage files on the server's disk. It encapsulates the underlying System.IO logic into a simple and focused API.

skylite.WebCore.ImageHandler

  The ImageHandler class is a utility within the WebCore namespace that provides a set of methods for processing and manipulating images on the server-side. It encapsulates the underlying System.Drawing logic, making it easy to perform common image operations such as resizing and rotating. This is particularly useful in web applications for tasks like creating thumbnails from user-uploaded images, standardizing image dimensions, or correcting the orientation of photos.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Image Resizing:
    • ResizeImage(Bitmap, ...): A general-purpose method to resize an image, likely accepting parameters for both width and height.
    • ResizeImageW(Bitmap, width): A convenience method to resize an image to a specific width while maintaining its aspect ratio.
    • ResizeImageH(Bitmap, height): A convenience method to resize an image to a specific height while maintaining its aspect ratio.
  • Image Rotation:
    • RotateImage(Bitmap): A method to rotate an image, likely by a predefined amount (e.g., 90 degrees).
    • ImageRotated(Bitmap): This method likely checks the EXIF data of an image to determine if it has a rotation flag set, which is common for photos taken with mobile devices.
  • Archiving:
    • ImageBase64(Bitmap, ...): A powerful method that converts an image (Bitmap object) into a Base64 encoded string. This is useful for embedding images directly into HTML (<img src="data:image/jpeg;base64,...">) or for transmitting image data within a JSON payload, avoiding the need to save a temporary file.

  In summary, the ImageHandler class is the go-to utility in the SkyLite framework for any server-side logic that needs to programmatically modify images. Its primary use case is processing user-uploaded images to create standardized thumbnails, correct orientation, and prepare them for efficient display or storage.

skylite.WebCore.XmlTool

  The XmlTool class is a utility within the WebCore namespace that provides a set of methods for parsing XML data directly from strings. It simplifies the process of extracting specific values, properties, or entire XML fragments without needing to load the XML into a full XmlDocument object. This makes it a lightweight and efficient tool for handling XML-based API responses, configuration files, or data interchange formats on the server-side. Key Features and Capabilities:

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Data Extraction:
    • GetElementValueFromXml(xmlString, ...): The primary method for extracting the inner text of a specific XML element from a larger XML string.
    • GetXmlElementProperty(xmlString, ...): A method to get the value of an attribute from a specific XML element.
    • GetXmlString(xmlString, ...): Retrieves the entire XML string of a specific element, including its tags and content.
  • Table-like XML Handling:
    • GetXmlTableBindedString(xmlString, ...): This suggests a specialized function for parsing XML that is structured like a table (e.g., a list of similar nodes like , , etc.) and possibly converting it into another format, like a delimited string.
    • GetXmlTableColumnCount(xmlString, ...): Likely used in conjunction with the above to count the number of "columns" or child elements within a table-like parent node.
  • Utility:
    • RemoveWhiteSpace(xmlString, ...): A helper function to strip insignificant whitespace from an XML string, which can be useful for cleaning or compacting the data.

  In summary, the XmlTool class is a focused utility in the SkyLite framework for performing common XML parsing tasks. It is particularly useful for server-side logic that needs to consume data from external services that provide XML responses or for reading configuration data stored in an XML format.

WebPage

The page base class, its HTML document, and the translation layer.

skylite.WebPage

  The WebPage class is the cornerstone of the SkyLite Framework's server-side logic. Every page class in an application (e.g., MyPage.vb) must inherit from WebPage. It provides the complete lifecycle event model for handling both initial page loads (GET requests) and subsequent API calls (POST requests), and it exposes the core HtmlDoc object for building the HTML response.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Page Lifecycle Events (GET Request):
    • OnLoad(): The first event, triggered by the framework. It handles the automatic loading of the corresponding .html file's content into HtmlDoc.
    • OnInitialized(): This is the primary event for developers to override. It's where all UI controls are instantiated, configured, and added to the HtmlDoc to build the page's dynamic content.
    • OnBeforeRender(): An event that fires just before the final HTML is assembled, allowing for last-minute modifications.
    • OnAfterRender(): The final event before the HTML is sent to the client, allowing for direct manipulation of the complete, rendered HTML string.
  • API Lifecycle Events (POST Request):
    • OnRequest(): Fires when a new $ApiRequest is received from the client, before the target function is called.
    • The framework then calls the specific public function requested by the client (e.g., Public Function MyFunction() As ApiResponse).
    • OnResponse(ByRef ApiResponse): Fires after the target function has executed but before the ApiResponse is sent to the client. It allows for global modification or logging of all API responses.
  • Core Properties:
    • HtmlDoc As skylite.WebPage.HtmlDocument: Provides access to the HtmlDocument object, which is used to build the final HTML page, add scripts/CSS, and set the body content. This is the central object used during a GET request.
    • Request / Response / Session / Application: Exposes the standard ASP.NET intrinsic objects for direct access to request data (cookies, headers), session state, and application state

  The WebPage class orchestrates the entire interaction model of the SkyLite framework. Developers use its lifecycle events to build the initial UI and its public functions to handle all subsequent AJAX-based interactions, making it the central hub for a page's server-side logic.

skylite.WebPage.HtmlDocument

  The HtmlDocument class, accessible within any WebPage-derived class via the HtmlDoc property, serves as the primary engine for constructing the final HTML page that is sent to the client's browser. It manages both the and <body> sections of the document, providing a rich set of methods and properties to programmatically build the entire page.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Body Content Management:
    • HtmlBodyText As String: This is the most frequently used property. It gets or sets the entire inner HTML of the "body" tag. All rendered SkyLite UI controls are ultimately assigned to this property.
    • HtmlBodyAddOn As String: Allows for appending additional HTML to the end of the body, which is useful for adding content after the main body text has been set.
  • Head Content Management:
    • AddJsFile(String): Automatically adds a "script" tag for a JavaScript file. The framework intelligently looks in the /scripts folder for a file matching the page name.
    • AddCSSFile(String): Automatically adds a [link rel="stylesheet"] tag for a CSS file, looking in the /styles folder.
    • AddHeaderScript(String) / AddBodyScript(String): Methods to add script tags to either the head or body.
    • AddMetaTag(...) / AddMetaElement(...): Methods for programmatically adding tags to the document head for SEO or configuration.
    • SetTitle(String): Sets the content of the [title] tag.
  • Initial Script Execution:
    • InitialScripts As skylite.ApiResponse: This unique property allows you to queue ApiResponse commands that should be executed immediately after the page has finished loading on the client. It is primarily used to call initialization functions for certain complex controls (e.g., HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$InitSideBar()")).
  • HTML Generation and Finalization:
    • The class contains internal methods like BuildHeadElements() and GenerateHtmlElements() which are called by the framework during the page rendering lifecycle (OnAfterRender). These methods assemble all the added scripts, styles, meta tags, and body content into a complete, valid HTML5 document string.

  In summary, the HtmlDocument object is the master controller for the final HTML output. Developers interact with it throughout the OnInitialized method to define the page's content (HtmlBodyText), link necessary resources (AddJsFile, AddCSSFile), and schedule initial client-side actions (InitialScripts).

skylite.WebPage.Translator

  The Translator class is a powerful, built-in utility within the WebPage class for handling multi-language text translation. Its purpose is to replace hardcoded text labels, titles, and messages with dictionary keys, which are then translated into the appropriate language at runtime. This allows a single codebase to support multiple languages.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Dictionary Management:
    • The Translator manages a collection of Dictionary objects in its TDictionary property.
    • Each Dictionary object represents a single translatable term, containing a DicKey (the unique identifier, e.g., "LBL_USERNAME"), a DicWord (the translated text, e.g., "Nom d'utilisateur"), and an IsoCode (the language code, e.g., "fr-FR").
    • Translation dictionaries can be populated programmatically using the Add methods. A common pattern would be to load these from a database table containing all application translations.
  • Core Functionality:
    • Translate(String): This is the primary method. It takes a dictionary key as input and returns the corresponding DicWord for the currently active language (IsoCode). If no translation is found, it likely returns the key itself.
    • Format(String): This method likely works in conjunction with Translate to handle formatted strings with placeholders (e.g., translating "Welcome, {0}!" and then inserting a user's name).
  • Integration:
    • The Translator object is accessible within the WebPage class, allowing UI controls to be populated with translated text during the OnInitialized phase. For example: nameInput.Label.InnerText = HtmlTranslator.Translate("LBL_USERNAME").

  In summary, the Translator class provides a complete, server-side system for internationalization. By abstracting text into a key-based dictionary, it enables developers to build multilingual applications that can be easily updated with new languages by simply adding new entries to the translation data source, without changing the application's C# or VB.NET code.

Utility Classes

Data access, mail, encryption, serialization and the supporting helpers.

skylite.DynamicModel

  The DynamicModel class is a specialized object that inherits from System.Dynamic.DynamicObject, allowing it to behave like an expando object. Its primary purpose is to enable the creation of objects whose properties are not defined at compile time but are instead added dynamically at runtime. Internally, it uses a Dictionary(Of String, Object) to store its properties and values. By overriding the TryGetMember and TrySetMember methods, it intercepts property access, allowing a developer to get and set properties using standard dot notation (e.g., myModel.FirstName = "John") as if they were pre-defined. Key Features and Capabilities:

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Dynamic Property Creation: Allows for adding properties to an object on the fly, without needing to pre-define a class structure. This is extremely useful for working with data whose schema is not known until runtime, such as results from a dynamic database query or a JSON payload.

  • Dictionary-Backed Storage: Uses a standard Dictionary as its internal data store, making it easy to inspect, iterate over, or serialize the collection of properties.

  • Simplified Data Handling: It provides a cleaner and more readable syntax for handling unstructured or semi-structured data compared to directly manipulating a Dictionary.
Usage Scenario:

A developer would use DynamicModel to represent a single data record where the columns might change. For instance, when retrieving data from a database, instead of creating a rigid class for each query result, one could populate a DynamicModel for each row.

Example (Conceptual): 
' Create a new dynamic model instance
Dim user As dynamic = New DynamicModel()

' Set properties on the fly
user.Id = 101
user.FirstName = "Alice"
user.IsActive = True

' Get properties
Dim name As String = user.FirstName ' Returns "Alice"

' The internal 'data' dictionary would now contain:
' { "Id": 101, "FirstName": "Alice", "IsActive": True }

  In summary, the DynamicModel class is an advanced tool within the SkyLite framework for handling dynamic data structures, providing a convenient and intuitive way to work with objects whose members are determined at runtime.

skylite.Encryptl

  The Encrypt class is a fundamental security utility within the SkyLite framework that provides methods for strong, two-way encryption and decryption. It encapsulates a specific cryptographic algorithm (likely AES, given modern security standards) and simplifies the process of securing and un-securing sensitive data. The encryption process is keyed, with the key being provided to the constructor, ensuring that only instances with the correct key can decrypt the data.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Keyed Encryption:
    • New(key): The constructor requires a secret key as a string. This key is used for both the encryption and decryption processes.
  • Core Functions:
    • EncryptData(plainText): Takes a plain text string as input and returns a cryptographically secure, encrypted string (likely in a Base64 format for easy transport).
    • DecryptData(encryptedText): Takes an encrypted string (that was encrypted with the same key) as input and returns the original plain text string.
Usage Scenario:

A developer would use the Encrypt class whenever they need to handle sensitive data that must be protected. Common use cases include:

  • Encrypting query string parameters before sending them in a URL with ApiResponse.Navigate.
  • Securing sensitive data before storing it in a cookie or localStorage.
  • Protecting data that is stored in a database column which requires application-level encryption.
Example (Conceptual): 
' Assume 'secretKey' is retrieved from a secure configuration
Dim secretKey As String = "MySuperSecretApplicationKey"
Dim crypto As New Encrypt(secretKey)

' --- Encryption ---
Dim userId As String = "12345"
Dim encryptedUserId As String = crypto.EncryptData(userId)
' 'encryptedUserId' would be a long, unreadable string like "aXbYcZ..."

' --- Decryption ---
' Later, when the encrypted value is received...
Dim receivedEncryptedId As String = "aXbYcZ..."
Dim decryptedUserId As String = crypto.DecryptData(receivedEncryptedId)
' 'decryptedUserId' would now be "12345"

  In summary, the Encrypt class is an essential and easy-to-use tool for implementing strong encryption within a SkyLite application, crucial for protecting sensitive user and application data.

skylite.Mail

  The Mail and MailInfo classes provide a complete, object-oriented system for sending emails via SMTP from within the SkyLite framework. They encapsulate the underlying .NET mail functionality into a simplified and reusable API.

skylite.MailInfo

  The MailInfo class is a data object used to configure the SMTP server settings. An instance of this class holds all the necessary information to connect and authenticate with an email server.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Server As String: The address of the SMTP server (e.g., "smtp.gmail.com").
  • Port As Integer: The port number for the SMTP server (e.g., 587).
  • SenderId As String: The username for authenticating with the SMTP server.
  • SenderPwd As String: The password for the SMTP user.
  • SecurityProtocolType As Integer: An integer likely representing the security protocol (e.g., SSL/TLS).
  • SenderAddr As String: The "From" email address.
  • Display As String: The "From" display name.
skylite.Mail

  The Mail class is the main worker class that constructs and sends the email message. It is initialized with a configured MailInfo object.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • ToAddr As String(): An array of recipient email addresses.
  • Subject As String: The subject line of the email.
  • Body As String: The HTML or plain text content of the email body.
Key Methods:
  • SendMail() As String: This is the primary action method. It uses the configured MailInfo and the message properties (ToAddr, Subject, Body) to connect to the SMTP server and send the email. It likely returns a status message indicating success or failure.
Usage Scenario:

  A developer would first create and configure a single MailInfo object, often in a global or configuration file, with their application's email server details. Then, in any server-side function where an email needs to be sent (e.g., after a user registers or a form is submitted), they would instantiate a Mail object, provide the MailInfo, set the ToAddr, Subject, and Body properties, and finally call the SendMail() method.

In summary, this system provides a clean separation of concerns: MailInfo handles the server configuration, while Mail handles the construction and sending of individual email messages.

skylite.MathEval

  The MathEval class is a specialized utility within the SkyLite framework designed to evaluate mathematical expressions provided as a string. It contains a single primary method, DoMath, which takes a string representing a mathematical formula (e.g., "(10 + 5) * 2 / 3") as input and computes the result, returning it as a string.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • String-Based Calculation: Its core function is to parse and calculate a numeric result from a string-based mathematical expression. This is useful in scenarios where formulas are stored dynamically in a database or configured by a user, and need to be evaluated on the server at runtime.
Usage Scenario:

  A developer would use MathEval in a server-side function where a calculation needs to be performed based on a dynamic or user-defined formula. For example, in a financial application, a user might define a custom formula for a report. The application would store this formula as a string and then use MathEval to compute the result when the report is generated.

Example (Conceptual):
' Assume 'formulaString' is retrieved from a database or user input
Dim formulaString As String = "(100 * 0.05) + 25"

' Instantiate the MathEval class
Dim evaluator As New MathEval()

' Call the DoMath method to get the result
Dim result As String = evaluator.DoMath(formulaString) ' result would be "30"

In summary, the MathEval class is a powerful tool for adding dynamic calculation capabilities to an application, allowing it to process mathematical formulas that are not hardcoded at compile time.

skylite.OleData

  The OleData class is a data access utility within the SkyLite framework specifically designed to interact with data sources via the OLE DB (Object Linking and Embedding, Database) provider. This makes it the primary tool for connecting to and manipulating data in non-SQL Server databases, such as Microsoft Access, Excel spreadsheets, or Oracle databases for which an OLE DB provider is installed. It encapsulates the underlying System.Data.OleDb logic into a simplified API for performing common database operations.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Connection Management:
    • New(connectionString): The constructor takes a full OLE DB connection string as its parameter, which specifies the provider, data source, and any other necessary connection details.
  • Data Retrieval:
    • DataGetSet(query, ByRef DataSet, ByRef returnMsg): Executes a SELECT query and populates a DataSet object with the results. It returns a boolean indicating success and an error message via a ByRef parameter.
    • GetOleDataTable(query, ByRef returnMsg): A convenience method that executes a SELECT query and returns the results directly as a single DataTable.
  • Data Manipulation:
    • DataPut(commands, ByRef returnMsg): Executes one or more data manipulation commands (e.g., INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, or stored procedure calls). It takes a list of command strings and returns a boolean for success and any error message.
Usage Scenario:

  A developer would use the OleData class when the application needs to read from or write to a Microsoft Access database or an Excel file on the server. They would first construct the appropriate OLE DB connection string, instantiate the OleData class with it, and then use the DataGetSet or DataPut methods to interact with the data source.

Example (Conceptual):
' Connection string for an Excel file
Dim connStr As String = "Provider=Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0;Data Source=C:\data\mysheet.xlsx;Extended Properties=""Excel 12.0 Xml;HDR=YES"";"

' Instantiate the OleData class
Dim excelData As New OleData(connStr)
Dim returnMsg As String = ""

' Get data from a sheet
Dim dt As DataTable = excelData.GetOleDataTable("SELECT * FROM [Sheet1$]", returnMsg)

' Check for errors
If Not String.IsNullOrEmpty(returnMsg) Then
    ' Handle error
End If

In summary, the OleData class is the counterpart to the SQLData class, providing a standardized and simplified interface for working with a wide range of OLE DB-compliant data sources.

skylite.OraData

  The OraData class is a data access utility within the SkyLite framework specifically designed to interact with Oracle databases. It likely encapsulates the Oracle.ManagedDataAccess.Client or a similar provider, abstracting the underlying ADO.NET logic into a simplified API for performing common database operations against an Oracle data source.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Connection Management:
    • New(connectionString) / New(OraInfo): The class can be instantiated with either a full Oracle connection string or a pre-configured OraInfo data object (which is not pictured but can be inferred to exist).
  • Data Retrieval:
    • DataGetSet(query, ByRef DataSet, ByRef returnMsg): Executes a SELECT query and populates a standard DataSet object with the results.
    • OraDataTable(query): A convenience method that executes a SELECT query and returns the results directly as a single DataTable.
    • OraFieldValue(query): A specialized method to execute a query that is expected to return a single row and a single column, retrieving just that single value as a string. This is highly efficient for lookup queries.
  • Data Manipulation:
    • DataPut(commands, ByRef returnMsg): Executes one or more data manipulation commands (e.g., INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, or PL/SQL block executions). It takes a list of command strings and returns a boolean for success and any error message.
Usage Scenario:

  A developer would use the OraData class when their application's backend database is Oracle. They would configure the connection details (either in a string or an OraInfo object), instantiate the OraData class, and then use its methods to query and manipulate data within their server-side WebPage functions.

In summary, the OraData class completes the data access trio (SQLData, OleData, OraData), providing a consistent and simplified interface for developers working specifically with Oracle databases within the SkyLite framework.

skylite.SQLData

  The SQLData class is a core data access utility within the SkyLite framework, specifically optimized for interacting with Microsoft SQL Server databases. It encapsulates the underlying System.Data.SqlClient logic, providing a simplified and consistent API for performing a wide range of database operations. This is the main class developers will use for any MS SQL database communication.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • Connection Management:
    • New(): A parameterless constructor that likely reads the main database connection string from a central configuration file (e.g., application.cfg).
    • New(connectionString) / New(SQLInfo): Overloaded constructors that allow for specifying a direct connection string or using a pre-configured SQLInfo data object.
  • Data Retrieval:
    • DataGetSet(query, ByRef DataSet, ByRef returnMsg): Executes a SELECT query and populates a standard DataSet object with one or more result tables.
    • SQLDataSet(query): A convenience method that executes a SELECT query and returns a DataSet.
    • SQLDataTable(query): The most common retrieval method, which executes a query and returns the results as a single, ready-to-use DataTable.
    • SQLFieldValue(query): A highly optimized method for queries that return a single row and single column, retrieving just that one value as a string.
    • SQLNameValues(query): Executes a query that is expected to return two columns and formats the result as a List(Of NameValue), which is perfect for populating OptionValues for dropdowns.
  • Data Manipulation:
    • DataPut(commands, ByRef returnMsg): Executes one or more data manipulation commands (e.g., INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, or stored procedure calls). It takes a list of command strings and includes built-in transaction management.
    • DataBulkInsert(tableName, DataTable): A high-performance method for efficiently inserting a large number of rows from a DataTable into a specified database table using SQL Server's bulk copy capabilities.
  • Schema Information:
    • SQLTables(): Retrieves a list of all tables in the database.
    • SQLTableSchema(tableName): Retrieves the schema information (column names, data types, etc.) for a specific table.

In summary, the SQLData class is the robust and full-featured gateway to Microsoft SQL Server in the SkyLite framework. It simplifies all common data operations, from simple lookups to high-performance bulk inserts, into an easy-to-use set of methods.

skylite.XmlHandler

  The XmlHandler class is a low-level, server-side utility designed for parsing and manipulating raw XML strings. Its primary function is to deconstruct an XML string into a navigable, object-oriented structure. This is in contrast to the XmlTool which is for simple, one-off value extraction. The XmlHandler is for more complex, stateful XML manipulation.

Key Features and Capabilities:
  • XML Parsing:
    • New(xmlString): The constructor takes a raw XML string as input and immediately parses it into a collection of XmlElement objects.
    • XmlElements As List(Of XmlHandler.XmlElement): This collection holds the entire parsed structure of the XML, representing each element as an object.
  • Object-Oriented XML Representation:
    • XmlElement: Represents a single node in the XML tree. It contains an Id, ParentId, its OuterXml, and an XmlTag object. This structure allows for representing a hierarchical document.
    • XmlTag: Represents the core of an XML element—its tag name, attributes, and inner text.
      • Tag As String: The element's name (e.g., "product").
      • InnerText As String: The text content between the opening and closing tags.
      • Attributes As List(Of NameValue): A collection of the element's attributes.
    • NameValue: A simple helper class to store a single attribute as a name/value pair.
  • XML Manipulation and Reconstruction:
    • The XmlTag class provides methods to SetAttribute, GetAttribute, and RemoveAttribute, allowing for programmatic modification of the parsed XML structure.
    • XmlText(): This method reconstructs and returns the complete, potentially modified, XML string from the internal XmlElements collection.
Usage Scenario:

  A developer would use the XmlHandler when they need to perform complex modifications on an XML document received from an external source before using its data. For example, they might receive an XML feed, parse it with XmlHandler, iterate through the XmlElements to find specific nodes, add or modify attributes on those nodes (using the XmlTag's methods), and then get the final, modified XML string using XmlText() to be saved or processed further.

In summary, the XmlHandler is an advanced, object-oriented XML parser and manipulator within the SkyLite framework. It provides the tools necessary to deconstruct, programmatically alter, and reconstruct XML documents entirely on the server-side.

ToolKit

The server-side UI control library. Build an interface in VB.NET or C# without writing markup by hand.

SkyLite ToolKit

  The skylite.toolkit namespace is a core component of the SkyLite Framework, providing a comprehensive suite of server-side UI controls for rapid web page construction. These controls act as object-oriented wrappers that abstract away the complexity of writing raw HTML and client-side scripts. Each control, such as Texts, Dropdown, Grid, or Button, is a class that programmatically generates its corresponding HTML structure when its HtmlText() method is called. The toolkit is built upon a foundational HtmlTag class, which allows for granular manipulation of attributes, styles, and content for every element within a control. This architecture enables developers to build complex, data-driven user interfaces entirely within a server-side language like VB.NET or C#, defining not only the structure and content but also client-side event bindings (onclick, onchange, etc.) before the page is ever rendered. The toolkit is specifically designed to integrate seamlessly with the framework's AJAX-based communication model, allowing the server to generate and send back new UI components as HTML strings within an ApiResponse, enabling dynamic, real-time page updates without a full postback.

SkyLite Toolkit Control Categories

  1. Basic Input Controls
    • Texts: The primary control for single-line text inputs (input). Configured using the TextTypes enum (e.g., text, password, email, date).
    • TextArea: For multi-line text input (textarea).
    • Dropdown: For creating dropdown lists (select). Uses OptionValues to populate its items.
    • CheckBox: For creating checkboxes, allowing for multiple selections. Also uses OptionValues.
    • Radio: For creating radio buttons, allowing for a single selection from a group. Also uses OptionValues.
    • Switch: A modern UI toggle switch, representing a boolean on/off state.
    • FileUpload: A control for selecting and uploading files.
    • Hidden: For creating hidden input fields to store data on the page.
  2. Buttons & Interaction
    • Button: A standard clickable button (input type="button"). Configured using the Button.ButtonTypes enum (Button, Reset, Submit)
    • Buttons: A container for creating a group of multiple buttons.
    • ImageButton: A button that uses an image.
  3. Container & Layout Controls
    • Wrap: The most basic container, inheriting from HtmlTag and acting as a simple "div" wrapper.
    • HtmlElementBox: A powerful container that arranges child controls and allows for proportional spacing (e.g., AddItem(control, 50)).
    • ContentsBox: A container that can hold the raw HTML of other controls and has a convenient Border property for default styling.
    • Stacker: Creates a multi-column (side-by-side) layout. Composed of Stacker.Column objects.
    • ItemPanel: A panel designed to arrange multiple HtmlTag elements with alignment options.
    • HtmlContentsBox / HtmlWrapper: Additional container types for flexible content arrangement.
  4. Data Display Controls
    • Grid: A flexible table for displaying data. Can be populated manually (AddColumn, AddRow) or bound to a DataTable. Composed of Grid.Column and Grid.Row objects.
    • DataGrid: A more powerful, data-bound grid control that includes advanced features for cell manipulation and styling. Composed of DataGrid.TableColumn, DataGrid.TableRow, etc.
    • SQLGridSection: A high-level, all-in-one control for displaying data directly from a SQL database. It includes built-in paging, sorting, and data download functionality. It is configured using the SQLGridInfo and SQLQuery helper classes.
  5. Navigation & Menu ControlsNavigation & Menu Controls
    • TabStrip: Creates a tabbed interface for organizing content. Requires TabStripScript.
    • MenuList: Creates a vertical or horizontal list of clickable menu items.
    • MenuPanel: A multi-column menu, suitable for complex "mega-menu" style navigation. Composed of MenuPanel.Column objects.
    • MenuSection / MultiMenuSection: Sectional controls that utilize MenuList to create structured page navigation.
    • SideBarSection: Creates a layout with a main content area and a side navigation menu. Requires SideBarScript.
    • ItemList: A simple list of clickable items, often used for navigation or selection.
  6. Hierarchical Data Controls
    • TreeView / TreeView2: Controls for displaying hierarchical data in a tree structure. Composed of TreeView.TreeItem objects. Requires TreeScript.
  7. Specialized & Display Controls
    • Label: A simple control for displaying non-interactive text.
    • Title / TitleBox / TitleSection / TitleSection2: Various controls for creating styled page titles and headers.
    • DialogBox: For creating modal dialogs or popups.
    • Paging: A control used to add page navigation to data grids.
    • Progress: A visual indicator to show progress through a series of steps. Composed of Progress.Item objects.
    • Timer: A control to perform actions on a timer. Requires TimerScript.
    • UserIcon: A control for displaying a user avatar or icon, often with a dropdown menu. Requires UserIconScript and UserIconStyle.
    • iFrame: A wrapper for the "iframe" HTML element.
    • LinePitch: A simple control to add vertical spacing.
    • Parallax: A container for creating parallax scrolling effects. Requires ParallaxStyle.
  8. Advanced Meta-Controls (Form Generation)
    • UIForm: A powerful, high-level control that can automatically generate an entire form based on a collection of UIForm.Element objects.
    • UIControl: A related control for dynamically generating UI elements based on a list of UIControl.Item objects, with different modes (Edit, New, View).
  9. Helper Classes & Enums
    • Alignments, HorizontalAligns, VerticalAligns: Enums for positioning.
    • Button.ButtonTypes, TextTypes, UIControl.Modes, UIForm.Modes: Enums for configuring control behavior.
    • OptionValues: A helper class used by Dropdown, CheckBox, and Radio to manage their list of options.

skylite.ToolKit.Button

  The skylite.ToolKit.Button control is a server-side object that programmatically generates an HTML "input" element for user interaction. It inherits directly from skylite.HtmlTag, giving it the full capabilities of setting any style or attribute. The type attribute of the generated input element is determined by the Button.ButtonTypes enum (Button, Reset, Submit). Within the SkyLite framework, its primary purpose is to use type="Button" to initiate client-server communication. This is achieved by setting an onclick attribute on the server, which calls a custom JavaScript function. This function, in turn, typically uses the built-in $ApiRequest function to invoke a corresponding public method on the server-side WebPage class, allowing for dynamic, AJAX-based actions without a full page postback.

Constructor:
VB
Public Sub New(value As String, Optional buttonType As Button.ButtonTypes = Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
C#
public Button(string value, Button.ButtonTypes buttonType = Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
Parameters:
  • value (String): The text that will appear on the button (rendered as the value attribute of the "input" tag).
  • buttonType (Button.ButtonTypes): An enum that specifies the button's type. Defaults to Button.ButtonTypes.Button.
    • Button: A standard clickable button, typically used with an onclick event for custom scripts.
    • Reset: Resets the fields within its parent form to their default values.
    • Submit: Submits its parent form (less common in the AJAX-driven SkyLite pattern).
Usage and Example:

The most common use of the Button control is to trigger a server-side action and update a part of the page in response. This example demonstrates a button that, when clicked, calls a server function to get the current time and displays it on the page. This example uses the OnInitialized method to dynamically build the UI.

JavaScript (myPage.js):
function GetServerTime() {
    // No data needs to be sent to the server, so a simple call is sufficient.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Create a label to display the result.
        Dim resultLabel As New Label("Click the button to see the server time.")
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "time-display")
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "10px")

        ' Create the Button control.
        Dim timeButton As New Button("Get Server Time", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        timeButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "GetServerTime()")

        ' Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = timeButton.HtmlText() & resultLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function GetServerTime() As ApiResponse
        Dim serverTime As String = DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss")
        Dim message As String = "The time on the server is: " & serverTime

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Update the content of the resultLabel's wrapper div.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("time-display", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Create a label to display the result.
        var resultLabel = new Label("Click the button to see the server time.");
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "time-display");
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "10px");

        // Create the Button control.
        var timeButton = new Button("Get Server Time", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        timeButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "GetServerTime()");

        // Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = timeButton.HtmlText() + resultLabel.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse GetServerTime()
    {
        string serverTime = DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss");
        string message = $"The time on the server is: {serverTime}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Update the content of the resultLabel's wrapper div.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("time-display", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Buttons

  The skylite.ToolKit.Buttons control is a specialized container designed to simplify the creation and layout of a group of multiple buttons. Unlike the singular Button control, Buttons does not inherit from HtmlTag itself, but rather acts as a manager that generates a parent "div" wrapper and populates it with one or more [input type="button"] elements. Buttons are added programmatically via the AddButton method, which allows for setting the label, inline styles, and HTML attributes for each button individually. This control is particularly useful for standard UI patterns like "OK/Cancel" or "Save/Delete/Close" dialogs, ensuring consistent structure and simplifying the process of generating a horizontal row of interactive buttons.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a button to the group
Public Sub AddButton(label As String, Optional inlineStyles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public Buttons()

// Method to add a button to the group
public void AddButton(string label, string inlineStyles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • label (String): The text that will appear on the button (rendered as the value attribute).
  • inlineStyles (String): A string of CSS rules to be applied to the button's style attribute (e.g., "width:100px; background-color:red;").
  • attributes (String): A string of HTML attributes to be added to the button, often including the onclick event (e.g., "class:my-btn; onclick:HandleSave();").
Usage and Example:

The Buttons control is ideal for the action bar of a form or dialog. This example demonstrates creating a simple form with "Save" and "Cancel" buttons. Each button triggers a different server-side function to update a status message on the page.

JavaScript (myPage.js):
function HandleSave() {
    // Call the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}

function HandleCancel() {
    // Call the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to show the status.
        Dim statusLabel As New Label("Status: Waiting for action.")
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label")

        ' Create the Buttons container.
        Dim actionButtons As New Buttons()

        ' Add a "Save" button with an onclick event.
        actionButtons.AddButton("Save", "background-color:green; color:white;", "onclick:HandleSave();")

        ' Add a "Cancel" button with a different style and onclick event.
        actionButtons.AddButton("Cancel", "background-color:gray; color:white;", "onclick:HandleCancel();")

        ' Set a style on the wrapper div for the buttons.
        actionButtons.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "20px")

        ' Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = statusLabel.HtmlText() & actionButtons.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function HandleSave() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", "Status: Save action was triggered.")
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function HandleCancel() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", "Status: Cancel action was triggered.")
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to show the status.
        var statusLabel = new Label("Status: Waiting for action.");
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label");

        // Create the Buttons container.
        var actionButtons = new Buttons();

        // Add a "Save" button with an onclick event.
        actionButtons.AddButton("Save", "background-color:green; color:white;", "onclick:HandleSave();");

        // Add a "Cancel" button with a different style and onclick event.
        actionButtons.AddButton("Cancel", "background-color:gray; color:white;", "onclick:HandleCancel();");

        // Set a style on the wrapper div for the buttons.
        actionButtons.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "20px");

        // Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = statusLabel.HtmlText() + actionButtons.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse HandleSave()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", "Status: Save action was triggered.");
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse HandleCancel()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", "Status: Cancel action was triggered.");
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.CheckBox

  The skylite.ToolKit.CheckBox control is a server-side object designed to generate a group of one or more HTML [input type="checkbox"] elements, each with an associated [label]. This control allows users to make multiple selections from a list of options. The options themselves are defined and managed using a separate OptionValues object, which is then assigned to the CheckBox control's Checks property. The control provides properties for layout (Alignment) and styling of the checkboxes and their labels (CheckStyles, CheckLabelStyles, etc.). When a form is submitted, the value of each checked box is sent to the server. Because multiple checkboxes can share the same name attribute, the developer must be prepared to handle multiple values for a single key on the server-side.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New(labelText As String, Optional toolAlignment As Alignments = Alignments.Vertical)
C#
// Main constructor
public CheckBox(string labelText, Alignments toolAlignment = Alignments.Vertical)
Parameters:
  • labelText (String): The text for the main label of the checkbox group.
  • toolAlignment (Alignments): An enum that specifies if the individual checkboxes should be arranged vertically or horizontally. Defaults to Vertical.
Usage and Example:

The CheckBox control is ideal for forms where the user needs to select multiple preferences or options. This example demonstrates a "Notification Preferences" form where a user can select multiple ways to be contacted.

JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SavePreferences() {
    // Find all checkboxes with the name 'contactMethod'
    var checkBoxes = document.getElementsByName('contactMethod');
    var selectedMethods = [];
    
    // Loop through them to find which ones are checked
    for (var i = 0; i < checkBoxes.length; i++) {
        if (checkBoxes[i].checked) {
            selectedMethods.push(checkBoxes[i].value);
        }
    }

    // Join the array into a comma-separated string to send to the server
    var data = [
        { key: 'methods', vlu: selectedMethods.join(',') }
    ];
    
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to show the submitted results.
        Dim resultLabel As New Label("Your preferences will be shown here.")
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "result-label")

        ' Create the CheckBox control.
        Dim prefsCheckBox As New CheckBox("Notification Preferences:", Alignments.Vertical)

        ' Create the options for the checkboxes.
        Dim contactOptions As New OptionValues()
        ' AddItem(value, text, name)
        contactOptions.AddItem("Email", "Receive email updates", "contactMethod")
        contactOptions.AddItem("SMS", "Receive SMS text messages", "contactMethod")
        contactOptions.AddItem("Phone", "Receive phone calls", "contactMethod")

        ' Assign the options to the CheckBox control.
        prefsCheckBox.Checks = contactOptions

        ' A button to submit the preferences.
        Dim saveButton As New Button("Save Preferences", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        saveButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SavePreferences()")

        ' Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = prefsCheckBox.HtmlText() & saveButton.HtmlText() & resultLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function SavePreferences() As ApiResponse
        ' The 'methods' value will be a comma-separated string from the client.
        Dim selectedMethods As String = GetDataValue("methods")

        Dim message As String = "Your selected preferences are: " & selectedMethods

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("result-label", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

        contactOptions.AddItem("SMS", "Receive SMS text messages", "contactMethod");

        prefsCheckBox.Checks = contactOptions;

skylite.ToolKit.ContentsBox

  The skylite.ToolKit.ContentsBox is a server-side container control designed to group and encapsulate other UI controls or raw HTML markup within a styled [div] wrapper. Unlike layout containers such as HtmlElementBox which accept control objects, ContentsBox works by accepting the rendered HTML strings of its child elements via its Add(String) method. This makes it a straightforward container for combining various pieces of pre-rendered content. A notable feature is its boolean Border property, which, when set to True, conveniently applies a default set of CSS styles for borders, padding, and shadows, making it ideal for creating visually distinct panels or sections on a page with minimal effort.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add content
Public Sub Add(contentsHtml As String)
C#
// Main constructor
public ContentsBox()

// Method to add content
public void Add(string contentsHtml)
Parameters:
  • contentsHtml (String): The rendered HTML string from another UI control (e.g., myButton.HtmlText()) or any raw HTML markup.
Usage and Example:

The ContentsBox is excellent for creating simple input forms or grouped content areas. This example demonstrates creating a search box that contains a text input and a submit button, all enclosed within the default styled border.

JavaScript (myPage.js):
function Search() {
    // Get the search term from the input box.
    var searchTerm = document.getElementById('search-input').value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'searchTerm', vlu: searchTerm }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)

skylite.ToolKit.DataGrid

  The skylite.ToolKit.DataGrid is a powerful, server-side UI control designed exclusively for data binding to generate a feature-rich HTML [table]. Unlike the simpler Grid control, DataGrid operates only in a data-bound mode, requiring a System.Data.DataTable to be passed to its constructor. It automatically generates table headers ([th]) based on the DataTable's column names and table rows ([tr]) for each DataRow. The DataGrid provides an object-oriented model for deep customization, allowing developers to programmatically access and manipulate individual TableColumns and TableRows after data binding. This enables advanced features such as setting styles and attributes on specific cells, embedding complex HTML or even other controls within cells, and attaching client-side events. It is the preferred control for displaying and interacting with structured, tabular data retrieved from a database.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New(dataSource As System.Data.DataTable)
C#
// Main constructor
public DataGrid(System.Data.DataTable dataSource)
Parameters:
  • dataSource (DataTable): A System.Data.DataTable object containing the data to be displayed. The grid's structure is inferred directly from this DataTable.
Usage and Example:

The DataGrid is ideal for displaying database query results. This example demonstrates populating a DataTable (simulating a database call), binding it to a DataGrid, and then iterating through the grid's rows and columns to add custom styling and an onclick event to a specific column.

JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ViewProductDetails(productId) {
    // Package the product ID to send to the server.
    var data = [
        { key: 'productId', vlu: productId }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function to handle the click.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System.Data

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create a DataTable (simulating data from a database).
        Dim dt As New DataTable()
        dt.Columns.Add("ProductID", GetType(Integer))
        dt.Columns.Add("ProductName", GetType(String))
        dt.Columns.Add("Price", GetType(Decimal))

        dt.Rows.Add(101, "Laptop", 1200.50)
        dt.Rows.Add(102, "Mouse", 25.00)
        dt.Rows.Add(103, "Keyboard", 75.99)

        ' 2. Create the DataGrid instance, binding it to the DataTable.
        Dim productGrid As New DataGrid(dt)

        ' 3. Customize the grid after binding.
        ' Apply styling to the entire table.
        productGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "100%")
        productGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderCollapse, "collapse")

        ' Loop through columns to style headers.
        For i As Integer = 0 To productGrid.TableColumns.Count - 1
            productGrid.TableColumns(i).SetHeaderStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, "#f2f2f2")
            productGrid.TableColumns(i).SetHeaderStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "8px")
        Next

        ' Loop through rows to style cells and add an onclick event.
        For i As Integer = 0 To productGrid.TableRows.Count - 1
            ' Style every cell in the row
            productGrid.TableRows(i).SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ddd")
            productGrid.TableRows(i).SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "8px")
            
            ' Make the 'ProductName' cell (column index 1) clickable.
            Dim productId As String = productGrid.TableRows(i).Columns(0).Value
            Dim productNameCell As DataGrid.TD = productGrid.TableRows(i).Columns(1)
            productNameCell.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.cursor, "pointer")
            productNameCell.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.color, "blue")
            productNameCell.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, $"ViewProductDetails('{productId}')")
        Next

        ' 4. Add the final grid to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = productGrid.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ViewProductDetails() As ApiResponse
        Dim productId As String = GetDataValue("productId")
        Dim message As String = "You clicked on Product ID: " & productId

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.Data;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create a DataTable (simulating data from a database).
        var dt = new DataTable();
        dt.Columns.Add("ProductID", typeof(int));
        dt.Columns.Add("ProductName", typeof(string));
        dt.Columns.Add("Price", typeof(decimal));

        dt.Rows.Add(101, "Laptop", 1200.50);
        dt.Rows.Add(102, "Mouse", 25.00);
        dt.Rows.Add(103, "Keyboard", 75.99);

        // 2. Create the DataGrid instance, binding it to the DataTable.
        var productGrid = new DataGrid(dt);

        // 3. Customize the grid after binding.
        // Apply styling to the entire table.
        productGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "100%");
        productGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderCollapse, "collapse");

        // Loop through columns to style headers.
        for (int i = 0; i < productGrid.TableColumns.Count; i++)
        {
            productGrid.TableColumns[i].SetHeaderStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, "#f2f2f2");
            productGrid.TableColumns[i].SetHeaderStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "8px");
        }

        // Loop through rows to style cells and add an onclick event.
        for (int i = 0; i < productGrid.TableRows.Count; i++)
        {
            // Style every cell in the row
            productGrid.TableRows[i].SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ddd");
            productGrid.TableRows[i].SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "8px");

            // Make the 'ProductName' cell (column index 1) clickable.
            string productId = productGrid.TableRows[i].Columns[0].Value;
            var productNameCell = productGrid.TableRows[i].Columns[1];
            productNameCell.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.cursor, "pointer");
            productNameCell.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.color, "blue");
            productNameCell.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, $"ViewProductDetails('{productId}')");
        }
        
        // 4. Add the final grid to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = productGrid.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse ViewProductDetails()
    {
        string productId = GetDataValue("productId");
        string message = $"You clicked on Product ID: {productId}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.DataList

  The skylite.ToolKit.DataList control is a server-side object that generates an HTML [datalist] element. The [datalist] element itself is not visible on the page; instead, it provides a set of pre-defined autocomplete options for an associated [input type="text"] field. This association is created by giving the [datalist] an id and then setting the list attribute of the text input to that same id. The options for the datalist are populated using an OptionValues object, which can be filled manually, from a database, or from a delimited string. This control enhances user experience by offering suggestions as the user types, without restricting them to only the provided options, as a standard [select] dropdown would.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New(id As String, dataOptions As OptionValues)
C#
// Main constructor
public DataList(string id, OptionValues dataOptions)
Parameters:
  • id (String): The unique id for the element. This id is crucial for linking the datalist to a text input field.
  • dataOptions (OptionValues): An OptionValues object containing the list of suggestion items (
Usage and Example:

The DataList control is perfect for providing suggestions on a search field or form input. This example demonstrates a "Country Search" input field where users get a list of suggested countries as they type. Note that this requires two controls: a Texts control for the input field and a DataList control for the suggestions.

JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowSelection() {
    // Get the final value from the input box.
    var selectedCountry = document.getElementById('country-input').value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'country', vlu: selectedCountry }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function with the selected value.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the OptionValues for the datalist using the delimited string format.
        Dim countryOptions As New OptionValues("{US|United States}{CA|Canada}{MX|Mexico}{GB|United Kingdom}{DE|Germany}")

        ' 2. Create the DataList control, giving it an ID and the options.
        Dim countryDataList As New DataList("country-suggestions", countryOptions)

        ' 3. Create the text input field.
        Dim searchInput As New Texts("Search for a Country:")
        ' Crucially, link the input to the datalist using the 'list' attribute.
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.list, "country-suggestions")
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "country-input")

        ' 4. Add a button to submit the selection.
        Dim submitButton As New Button("Submit", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        submitButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ShowSelection()")

        ' 5. Add the rendered HTML of BOTH controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = searchInput.HtmlText() & countryDataList.HtmlText() & submitButton.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ShowSelection() As ApiResponse
        Dim selectedCountry As String = GetDataValue("country")
        Dim message As String = "You selected or entered: " & selectedCountry

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

        // 3. Create the text input field.
        var searchInput = new Texts("Search for a Country:");
        // Crucially, link the input to the datalist using the 'list' attribute.
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.list, "country-suggestions");

    }

skylite.ToolKit.DialogBox

  The skylite.ToolKit.DialogBox is a server-side UI control that generates the HTML content for a modal dialog, which is then typically displayed on the client-side using the ApiResponse.PopUpWindow method. The DialogBox is a container control that can hold both simple text content and a collection of Button objects. Content is added via the constructor or the AddContents method, and buttons are added via the AddButton method. When rendered, it produces a structured [div] with separate sections for the main content and a button panel. This control simplifies the creation of standard confirmation prompts ("Are you sure?"), informational messages, and simple interactive dialogs by providing a consistent structure and separating the content from the action buttons.

Constructor:
VB
' Constructors
Public Sub New()
Public Sub New(contents As String)

' Methods
Public Sub AddContents(contents As String)
Public Sub AddButton(label As String, Optional inlineStyles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Constructors
public DialogBox()
public DialogBox(string contents)

// Methods
public void AddContents(string contents)
public void AddButton(string label, string inlineStyles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • contents (String): The HTML or plain text content to be displayed in the main body of the dialog.
  • label (String): The text to appear on a button within the dialog.
  • inlineStyles (String): CSS styles for a button.
  • attributes (String): HTML attributes for a button, including the onclick event.
Usage and Example:

The most common use for DialogBox is to create a confirmation prompt that is shown to the user with PopUpWindow. This example demonstrates a "Save Changes" button that, when clicked, calls a server function to create and display a confirmation dialog. The "Yes, Save" button inside the dialog then calls a different server function to perform the save and close the popup.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
<p>Click the button to save your changes.</p>
<button onclick="ShowSaveConfirmation()">Save Changes</button>
<div id="status">Status: No changes saved.</div>
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowSaveConfirmation() {
    // Calls the server to generate and show the popup.
    $ApiRequest();
}

// NOTE: The PerformSave() function will be called by the 'Yes, Save' button
// which is defined in the DialogBox HTML generated by the server.
function PerformSave() {
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    ' This function is called first to build and show the dialog.
    Public Function ShowSaveConfirmation() As ApiResponse
        ' 1. Create the DialogBox instance.
        Dim confirmDialog As New DialogBox("Are you sure you want to save the changes?")
        
        ' 2. Add buttons with onclick events.
        confirmDialog.AddButton("Yes, Save", "", "onclick:PerformSave();")
        confirmDialog.AddButton("Cancel", "", "onclick:$PopOff();") ' Use built-in function to close

        ' 3. Create the response and show the dialog using PopUpWindow.
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.PopUpWindow(confirmDialog.HtmlText())
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    ' This function is called by the 'Yes, Save' button inside the dialog.
    Public Function PerformSave() As ApiResponse
        ' (Perform actual save logic here...)
        
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Update the main page.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Changes have been saved successfully.")
        ' Close the dialog.
        _ApiResponse.PopOff()
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)

using skylite.ToolKit;
