{
    // This function is called first to build and show the dialog.
    public ApiResponse ShowSaveConfirmation()
    {
        // 1. Create the DialogBox instance.
        var confirmDialog = new DialogBox("Are you sure you want to save the changes?");

        // 2. Add buttons with onclick events.
        confirmDialog.AddButton("Yes, Save", "", "onclick:PerformSave();");
        confirmDialog.AddButton("Cancel", "", "onclick:$PopOff();"); // Use built-in function to close

        // 3. Create the response and show the dialog using PopUpWindow.
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.PopUpWindow(confirmDialog.HtmlText());
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    // This function is called by the 'Yes, Save' button inside the dialog.
    public ApiResponse PerformSave()
    {
        // (Perform actual save logic here...)

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Update the main page.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status", "Status: Changes have been saved successfully.");
        // Close the dialog.
        _ApiResponse.PopOff();
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Dropdown

  The skylite.ToolKit.Dropdown is a server-side UI control that generates an HTML [select] element, commonly known as a dropdown list, allowing a user to choose a single option from a predefined set. The control is a composite object that includes an HtmlTag for the main [select] element (SelBox) as well as HtmlTags for its label and wrappers. The list of choices ([option] tags) is managed by a separate OptionValues helper class, which is then assigned to the Dropdown control's SelOptions property. This separation allows the options to be populated from various sources, including a hardcoded delimited string, a database query, or programmatic AddItem calls. The control supports standard form element features like a main label, required field validation, and event handling via attributes like onchange.

Constructor:
VB
' Constructors
Public Sub New()
Public Sub New(labelText As String, Optional toolAlignment As Alignments = Alignments.Vertical)
Public Sub New(labelText As String, name As String, Optional toolAlignment As Alignments = Alignments.Vertical)
C#
// Constructors
public Dropdown()
public Dropdown(string labelText, Alignments toolAlignment = Alignments.Vertical)
public Dropdown(string labelText, string name, Alignments toolAlignment = Alignments.Vertical)
Parameters:
  • labelText (String): The text for the
  • toolAlignment (Alignments): Specifies if the label and dropdown are arranged vertically or horizontally.
  • name (String): The name attribute for the "select" element.
Usage and Example:

The Dropdown control is a fundamental part of most forms. This example demonstrates creating a dropdown to select a department. When the user changes the selection, an onchange event is fired, calling a server-side function to display the chosen department.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function DepartmentChanged(dropdownElement) {
    // Get the selected value from the dropdown.
    var selectedDept = dropdownElement.value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'department', vlu: selectedDept }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-sde function, passing the selected value.
    $ApiRequest('DepartmentChanged', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to display the result.
        Dim resultLabel As New Label("You have not selected a department.")
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "result-label")

        ' 1. Create the Dropdown control.
        Dim deptDropdown As New Dropdown("Select Department:", Alignments.Horizontal)
        
        ' Set the ID so JavaScript can find it, and the onchange event.
        deptDropdown.SelBox.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "dept-select")
        deptDropdown.SelBox.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onchange, "DepartmentChanged(this)")

        ' 2. Create the OptionValues to populate the dropdown.
        Dim deptOptions As New OptionValues()
        deptOptions.AddItem("", "-- Please Select --") ' Value, Text
        deptOptions.AddItem("HR", "Human Resources")
        deptOptions.AddItem("IT", "Information Technology")
        deptOptions.AddItem("SALES", "Sales and Marketing")

        ' 3. Assign the options to the Dropdown.
        deptDropdown.SelOptions = deptOptions

        ' 4. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = deptDropdown.HtmlText() & resultLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function DepartmentChanged() As ApiResponse
        Dim selectedDept As String = GetDataValue("department")
        Dim message As String = ""
        If String.IsNullOrEmpty(selectedDept) Then
            message = "You have not selected a department."
        Else
            message = "You selected the " & selectedDept & " department."
        End If

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("result-label", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to display the result.
        var resultLabel = new Label("You have not selected a department.");
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "result-label");

        // 1. Create the Dropdown control.
        var deptDropdown = new Dropdown("Select Department:", Alignments.Horizontal);

        // Set the ID so JavaScript can find it, and the onchange event.
        deptDropdown.SelBox.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "dept-select");
        deptDropdown.SelBox.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onchange, "DepartmentChanged(this)");

        // 2. Create the OptionValues to populate the dropdown.
        var deptOptions = new OptionValues();
        deptOptions.AddItem("", "-- Please Select --"); // Value, Text
        deptOptions.AddItem("HR", "Human Resources");
        deptOptions.AddItem("IT", "Information Technology");
        deptOptions.AddItem("SALES", "Sales and Marketing");

        // 3. Assign the options to the Dropdown.
        deptDropdown.SelOptions = deptOptions;

        // 4. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = deptDropdown.HtmlText() + resultLabel.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse DepartmentChanged()
    {
        string selectedDept = GetDataValue("department");
        string message = "";
        if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(selectedDept))
        {
            message = "You have not selected a department.";
        }
        else
        {
            message = $"You selected the {selectedDept} department.";
        }

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("result-label", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.FileUpload

  The skilite.ToolKit.FileUpload is a server-side UI control that generates the necessary HTML and provides the server-side mechanism to handle file uploads from a client's browser. It renders as a composite element, typically including a visible "Browse" or "Attach" button and a text area to display the selected filename, while the underlying [input type="file"] element is often visually hidden but programmatically triggered. When a user selects a file and an associated "Upload" button is clicked, the file is sent to the server as part of a multipart/form-data POST request. On the server, within the target API function, the uploaded file can be accessed via the WebPage's built-in Request.Files collection. The framework provides helper properties to easily access the file's name, size, and content type, and a method to save the file to the server's disk.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
C#
// Main constructor
public FileUpload()
Parameters:
  • None
Usage and Example:

The FileUpload control is used in any scenario where a user needs to upload a file to the server. This example demonstrates a simple page where a user can select a file and click "Upload". The server-side code will then "receive" the file, check its name and size, and save it to a designated folder on the server.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function UploadFile() {
    // The file is automatically included in the POST request by the browser.
    // We only need to trigger the server-side function.
    // No data needs to be manually packaged for the file itself.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System.IO

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to show the status of the upload.
        Dim statusLabel As New Label("Please select a file to upload.")
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label")

        ' 1. Create the FileUpload control.
        ' Set the ID on the actual file input element for server-side access.
        Dim fileUploader As New FileUpload()
        fileUploader.File.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "my-file-uploader")
        fileUploader.File.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.name, "my-file-uploader") ' Name is important

        ' 2. Create the Upload button.
        Dim uploadButton As New Button("Upload", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        uploadButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "UploadFile()")

        ' 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = fileUploader.HtmlText() & uploadButton.HtmlText() & statusLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function UploadFile() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        Dim message As String = ""

        ' 4. Check if any files were uploaded in the request.
        If Request.Files.Count > 0 Then
            ' Access the uploaded file (by its 'name' attribute).
            Dim uploadedFile = Request.Files("my-file-uploader")

            If uploadedFile IsNot Nothing AndAlso uploadedFile.ContentLength > 0 Then
                ' Get file details.
                Dim fileName As String = Path.GetFileName(uploadedFile.FileName)
                Dim fileSize As Integer = uploadedFile.ContentLength
                Dim fileType As String = uploadedFile.ContentType

                ' 5. Save the file to a folder on the server.
                ' The 'uploads' folder must exist in your project root.
                Dim savePath As String = Server.MapPath("~/uploads/" & fileName)
                uploadedFile.SaveAs(savePath)

                message = "File uploaded successfully! Name: " & fileName & ", Size: " & fileSize & " bytes."
            Else
                message = "Error: No file was selected or the file is empty."
            End If
        Else
            message = "Error: No files were included in the request."
        End If

        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.IO;
using System.Web; // Required for HttpPostedFile

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to show the status of the upload.
        var statusLabel = new Label("Please select a file to upload.");
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label");

        // 1. Create the FileUpload control.
        // Set the ID and Name on the actual file input element for server-side access.
        var fileUploader = new FileUpload();
        fileUploader.File.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "my-file-uploader");
        fileUploader.File.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.name, "my-file-uploader"); // Name is important

        // 2. Create the Upload button.
        var uploadButton = new Button("Upload", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        uploadButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "UploadFile()");

        // 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = fileUploader.HtmlText() + uploadButton.HtmlText() + statusLabel.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse UploadFile()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        string message = "";

        // 4. Check if any files were uploaded in the request.
        if (Request.Files.Count > 0)
        {
            // Access the uploaded file (by its 'name' attribute).
            HttpPostedFile uploadedFile = Request.Files["my-file-uploader"];

            if (uploadedFile != null && uploadedFile.ContentLength > 0)
            {
                // Get file details.
                string fileName = Path.GetFileName(uploadedFile.FileName);
                int fileSize = uploadedFile.ContentLength;
                string fileType = uploadedFile.ContentType;

                // 5. Save the file to a folder on the server.
                // The 'uploads' folder must exist in your project root.
                string savePath = Server.MapPath($"~/uploads/{fileName}");
                uploadedFile.SaveAs(savePath);
                
                message = $"File uploaded successfully! Name: {fileName}, Size: {fileSize} bytes.";
            }
            else
            {
                message = "Error: No file was selected or the file is empty.";
            }
        }
        else
        {
            message = "Error: No files were included in the request.";
        }

        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.FilterSection

  The skilite.ToolKit.FilterSection is a specialized server-side container control designed to create a common UI pattern: a horizontal section containing a navigation or category menu on the left and a set of filter/search controls on the right. The control is a composite object, exposing a Menu property (of type MenuList) to manage the left-hand menu items, and a FilterHtml string property to hold the rendered HTML of the right-hand filter controls. By populating these two properties, developers can easily construct a structured and visually distinct filter bar at the top of a data-driven page, separating the main navigation/categorization from the more granular search and filter inputs.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
C#
// Main constructor
public FilterSection()
Parameters:
  • None
Usage and Example:

The FilterSection is perfect for the top of a page that displays a large data grid. This example demonstrates creating a filter bar for a product list. The left side has menu items to filter by category, and the right side has a text box for searching and a button.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function FilterByCategory(category) {
    var data = [ { key: 'category', vlu: category } ];
    $ApiRequest('ApplyFilters', JSON.stringify(data));
}

function Search() {
    var searchTerm = document.getElementById('search-term').value;
    var data = [ { key: 'searchTerm', vlu: searchTerm } ];
    $ApiRequest('ApplyFilters', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to show the current filter status.
        Dim statusLabel As New Label("Showing all products.")
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label")

        ' 1. Create the child controls for the filter area.
        Dim searchInput As New Texts()
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "search-term")
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.placeholder, "Search by name...")

        Dim searchButton As New Button("Search", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        searchButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "Search()")

        ' 2. Create the FilterSection instance.
        Dim productFilters As New FilterSection()
        productFilters.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "20px")

        ' 3. Populate the left-side menu.
        productFilters.Menu.Add("All", "", "onclick:FilterByCategory('All');")
        productFilters.Menu.Add("Laptops", "", "onclick:FilterByCategory('Laptops');")
        productFilters.Menu.Add("Keyboards", "", "onclick:FilterByCategory('Keyboards');")

        ' 4. Populate the right-side filter area with the rendered HTML of child controls.
        productFilters.FilterHtml = searchInput.HtmlText() & searchButton.HtmlText()
        
        ' 5. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = productFilters.HtmlText() & statusLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ApplyFilters() As ApiResponse
        Dim category As String = GetDataValue("category")
        Dim searchTerm As String = GetDataValue("searchTerm")

        Dim message As String = "Filtering by: "
        If Not String.IsNullOrEmpty(category) Then
            message &= "Category='" & category & "' "
        End If
        If Not String.IsNullOrEmpty(searchTerm) Then
            message &= "Search='" & searchTerm & "' "
        End If

        ' (In a real app, you would now re-query the database and update a grid)

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to show the current filter status.
        var statusLabel = new Label("Showing all products.");
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label");

        // 1. Create the child controls for the filter area.
        var searchInput = new Texts();
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "search-term");
        searchInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.placeholder, "Search by name...");

        var searchButton = new Button("Search", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        searchButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "Search()");
        
        // 2. Create the FilterSection instance.
        var productFilters = new FilterSection();
        productFilters.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "20px");

        // 3. Populate the left-side menu.
        productFilters.Menu.Add("All", "", "onclick:FilterByCategory('All');");
        productFilters.Menu.Add("Laptops", "", "onclick:FilterByCategory('Laptops');");
        productFilters.Menu.Add("Keyboards", "", "onclick:FilterByCategory('Keyboards');");

        // 4. Populate the right-side filter area with the rendered HTML of child controls.
        productFilters.FilterHtml = searchInput.HtmlText() + searchButton.HtmlText();

        // 5. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = productFilters.HtmlText() + statusLabel.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse ApplyFilters()
    {
        string category = GetDataValue("category");
        string searchTerm = GetDataValue("searchTerm");

        string message = "Filtering by: ";
        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(category))
        {
            message += $"Category='{category}' ";
        }
        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(searchTerm))
        {
            message += $"Search='{searchTerm}' ";
        }

        // (In a real app, you would now re-query the database and update a grid)

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.FooterSection

  The skilite.ToolKit.FooterSection is a specialized server-side UI control designed to simplify the creation of a standard webpage footer. It is a composite control that encapsulates a main title (e.g., "Quick Links") and a list of menu items or links. The footer's title can be set via the Title property, and links are added programmatically using the AddMenu method. This method allows for setting the link's text, optional inline styles, and optional HTML attributes, which are typically used to define the link's destination (href) or an onclick JavaScript event. When rendered, it generates a structured "div" containing the title and an unordered list (ul) of links, providing a consistent and organized footer section with minimal code.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a menu link
Public Sub AddMenu(text As String, Optional styles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public FooterSection()

// Method to add a menu link
public void AddMenu(string text, string styles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • text (String): The visible text of the link.
  • styles (String) (Optional): A string of CSS rules to be applied to the link's style attribute.
  • attributes (String) (Optional): A string of HTML attributes, most commonly used to set the link's destination (e.g., "href:about.html") or an onclick event (e.g., "onclick:ShowPopup();").
Usage and Example:

The FooterSection control is used to quickly build the footer area of a webpage, complete with navigation links and interactive elements. This example demonstrates creating a simple footer with both standard navigation links and a "Help" link that triggers a server-side action.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowHelp() {
    // This function calls the server to display a help message.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the FooterSection instance.
        Dim myFooter As New FooterSection()
        myFooter.Title.InnerText = "Company Info"
        myFooter.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "50px")
        myFooter.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "20px")
        myFooter.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, "#f8f9fa")

        ' 2. Add menu items. Some are standard links, one is interactive.
        myFooter.AddMenu("Privacy Policy", "", "href:privacy.html")
        myFooter.AddMenu("Terms of Service", "", "href:terms.html")
        myFooter.AddMenu("Help", "", "onclick:ShowHelp();") ' This one calls a script.

        ' 3. Add the rendered HTML to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = myFooter.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    ' This function is called by the "Help" link's onclick event.
    Public Function ShowHelp() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox("For assistance, please contact support@example.com.")
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class

        myFooter.AddMenu("Terms of Service", "", "href:terms.html");

        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = myFooter.HtmlText();

skylite.ToolKit.Grid

  The skilite.ToolKit.Grid is a versatile server-side UI control that generates an HTML "table" for displaying structured data. It supports two primary modes of operation: "unbound" and "data-bound". In unbound mode, the developer has full manual control, programmatically creating the table structure by calling AddColumn(String) to define headers and AddRow(String()) to populate the data row by row. In data-bound mode, the grid is initialized with a System.Data.DataTable, and it automatically generates the columns and rows based on the DataTable's structure and content. In both modes, the Grid provides a rich object model, allowing for post-creation customization of headers (Headers), rows (Rows), and columns (Columns) to set styles and attributes on a granular level.

Constructor:
VB
' Unbound constructor
Public Sub New()
' Data-bound constructor
Public Sub New(dataSource As System.Data.DataTable, Optional autoGenerateColumns As Boolean = True)

' Methods for unbound mode
Public Sub AddColumn(columnName As String)
Public Sub AddRow(rowData As String())
C#
// Unbound constructor
public Grid()
// Data-bound constructor
public Grid(System.Data.DataTable dataSource, bool autoGenerateColumns = true)

// Methods for unbound mode
public void AddColumn(string columnName)
public void AddRow(string[] rowData)
Parameters:
  • dataSource (DataTable): A System.Data.DataTable object to populate the grid.
  • autoGenerateColumns (Boolean) (Optional): If True, columns are created automatically from the DataTable.
  • columnName (String): The text for a table header (th).
  • rowData (String()): An array of strings representing the cell data for a single row (tr).
Usage and Example:

The Grid control is a fundamental tool for displaying lists of data. This example demonstrates the "unbound" mode, where a simple user list is created manually. It also shows how to customize a specific cell after the rows have been added.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ViewUser(userId) {
    var data = [ { key: 'userId', vlu: userId } ];
    $ApiRequest('ViewUser', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create an empty Grid instance (unbound mode).
        Dim userGrid As New Grid()
        userGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "100%") ' Style the main table tag
        userGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderCollapse, "collapse")

        ' 2. Define the columns. This creates the <thead> section.
        userGrid.AddColumn("ID")
        userGrid.AddColumn("Name")
        userGrid.AddColumn("Role")
        userGrid.AddColumn("Action")

        ' 3. Add rows of data. This creates the <tbody> section.
        userGrid.AddRow({"101", "Alice", "Admin", ""})
        userGrid.AddRow({"102", "Bob", "User", ""})
        userGrid.AddRow({"103", "Charlie", "User", ""})

        ' 4. Customize the grid after creation.
        ' Loop through rows to add a "View" button to the last column.
        For i As Integer = 0 To userGrid.Rows.Count - 1
            Dim userId As String = userGrid.Rows(i).Columns(0).Value
            Dim actionCell As Grid.TD = userGrid.Rows(i).Columns(3) ' Get the 4th column (index 3)

            ' Create a button and place its HTML into the cell.
            Dim viewButton As New Button("View", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
            viewButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, $"ViewUser('{userId}')")
            actionCell.InnerText = viewButton.HtmlText()
            
            ' Add some basic styling to all rows
            userGrid.Rows(i).SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ddd")
        Next

        ' 5. Add the final grid to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userGrid.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ViewUser() As ApiResponse
        Dim userId As String = GetDataValue("userId")
        Dim message As String = "Viewing details for User ID: " & userId

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create an empty Grid instance (unbound mode).
        var userGrid = new Grid();
        userGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "100%"); // Style the main table tag
        userGrid.Table.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderCollapse, "collapse");

        // 2. Define the columns. This creates the <thead> section.
        userGrid.AddColumn("ID");
        userGrid.AddColumn("Name");
        userGrid.AddColumn("Role");
        userGrid.AddColumn("Action");

        // 3. Add rows of data. This creates the <tbody> section.
        userGrid.AddRow(new string[] { "101", "Alice", "Admin", "" });
        userGrid.AddRow(new string[] { "102", "Bob", "User", "" });
        userGrid.AddRow(new string[] { "103", "Charlie", "User", "" });
        
        // 4. Customize the grid after creation.
        // Loop through rows to add a "View" button to the last column.
        for (int i = 0; i < userGrid.Rows.Count; i++)
        {
            string userId = userGrid.Rows[i].Columns[0].Value;
            var actionCell = userGrid.Rows[i].Columns[3]; // Get the 4th column (index 3)

            // Create a button and place its HTML into the cell.
            var viewButton = new Button("View", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
            viewButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, $"ViewUser('{userId}')");
            actionCell.InnerText = viewButton.HtmlText();

            // Add some basic styling to all rows
            userGrid.Rows[i].SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ddd");
        }
        
        // 5. Add the final grid to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userGrid.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse ViewUser()
    {
        string userId = GetDataValue("userId");
        string message = $"Viewing details for User ID: {userId}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Hidden

  The skilite.ToolKit.Hidden is a server-side UI control that generates an HTML [input type="hidden"] element. This control inherits from skylite.HtmlTag, allowing for standard attribute manipulation. Its primary purpose is to store non-visible data on the client-side that needs to be sent back to the server with an API request. This is useful for maintaining state, such as storing a record's unique ID, a security token, or other contextual data that the user does not need to see but is required for server-side processing. The value of the hidden input can be accessed and submitted by client-side JavaScript along with other form data.

Constructor:
VB
' Constructors
Public Sub New()
Public Sub New(id As String, Optional name As String = "")
C#
// Constructors
public Hidden()
public Hidden(string id, string name = "")
Parameters:
  • id (String): The id attribute for the hidden input, used for DOM manipulation via JavaScript.
  • name (String) (Optional): The name attribute for the hidden input.
Usage and Example:

The Hidden control is essential for tracking the ID of a record that is being edited. In this example, when the page loads, it simulates fetching a user record and stores the user's ID in a hidden field. When the user clicks "Save", the JavaScript function reads both the visible data (the user's name) and the invisible data (the user's ID) and sends them to the server.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SaveUser() {
    // Get the value from the visible text input.
    var userName = document.getElementById('user-name-input').value;
    // Get the value from the hidden input.
    var userId = document.getElementById('user-id-hidden').value;

    var data = [
        { key: 'userId', vlu: userId },
        { key: 'userName', vlu: userName }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function with both pieces of data.
    $ApiRequest('SaveUser', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' --- Simulate loading a user record ---
        Dim currentUserId As String = "101"
        Dim currentUserName As String = "Alice"

        ' 1. Create the Hidden control to store the user's ID.
        Dim userIdHidden As New Hidden("user-id-hidden")
        userIdHidden.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.value, currentUserId)

        ' 2. Create the visible text input for the user's name.
        Dim userNameInput As New Texts("User Name:")
        userNameInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "user-name-input")
        userNameInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.value, currentUserName)

        ' 3. Create a save button.
        Dim saveButton As New Button("Save", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        saveButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SaveUser()")
        
        ' 4. Add the controls to the page. The hidden input will not be visible.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userIdHidden.HtmlText() & userNameInput.HtmlText() & saveButton.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function SaveUser() As ApiResponse
        ' Get both the visible and hidden values.
        Dim userId As String = GetDataValue("userId")
        Dim userName As String = GetDataValue("userName")

        ' (In a real app, you would execute an UPDATE query using the userId)
        Dim message As String = "Saved user data. ID: " & userId & ", New Name: " & userName

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // --- Simulate loading a user record ---
        string currentUserId = "101";
        string currentUserName = "Alice";

        // 1. Create the Hidden control to store the user's ID.
        var userIdHidden = new Hidden("user-id-hidden");
        userIdHidden.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.value, currentUserId);

        // 2. Create the visible text input for the user's name.
        var userNameInput = new Texts("User Name:");
        userNameInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "user-name-input");
        userNameInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.value, currentUserName);

        // 3. Create a save button.
        var saveButton = new Button("Save", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        saveButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SaveUser()");
        
        // 4. Add the controls to the page. The hidden input will not be visible.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userIdHidden.HtmlText() + userNameInput.HtmlText() + saveButton.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse SaveUser()
    {
        // Get both the visible and hidden values.
        string userId = GetDataValue("userId");
        string userName = GetDataValue("userName");

        // (In a real app, you would execute an UPDATE query using the userId)
        string message = $"Saved user data. ID: {userId}, New Name: {userName}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.HtmlContentsBox

  The skilite.ToolKit.HtmlContentsBox is a server-side container control that generates a [div] element designed to hold a group of HTML elements. It inherits from skylite.HtmlTag, granting it full access to standard SetStyle and SetAttribute methods. Its primary purpose is to group multiple, pre-rendered HTML strings into a single, cohesive block. Unlike more complex layout containers, its Add method is specifically for combining HTML content, allowing developers to set individual styles and attributes for each piece of content added. This makes it a lightweight and flexible choice for creating custom components or sections where the layout is managed by a combination of the order of added elements and CSS.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add an HTML content string
Public Sub Add(contents As String, Optional styles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public HtmlContentsBox()

// Method to add an HTML content string
public void Add(string contents, string styles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • contents (String): The HTML string to add to the container. This is often the output of another control's .HtmlText() method.
  • styles (String) (Optional): A string of CSS rules to be applied to a [span] wrapper around the added content.
  • attributes (String) (Optional): A string of HTML attributes to be applied to the [span] wrapper.
Usage and Example:

The HtmlContentsBox is useful for creating custom info-cards or composite display elements. This example demonstrates building a simple user profile "card" that combines an image, a name, and a department, each styled differently within the same container.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
This example does not require any JavaScript as it is for display purposes only.
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the individual HTML elements that will go inside the box.
        Dim userImage As New HtmlTag("img")
        userImage.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/avatar.png") ' Placeholder image
        userImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "80px")
        userImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "50%") ' Make it circular

        Dim userName As New Label("Alice")
        userName.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "18px")
        userName.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold")

        Dim userDept As New Label("Administrator")
        userDept.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.color, "gray")

        ' 2. Create the HtmlContentsBox container.
        Dim profileCard As New HtmlContentsBox()
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "250px")
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "20px")
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.textAlign, "center")
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.boxShadow, "0 2px 5px rgba(0,0,0,0.1)")
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "8px")

        ' 3. Add the rendered HTML of the child elements to the container.
        ' Each Add call can have its own wrapper style.
        profileCard.Add(userImage.HtmlText(), "margin-bottom:15px;")
        profileCard.Add(userName.HtmlText(), "margin-bottom:5px;")
        profileCard.Add(userDept.HtmlText())

        ' 4. Add the final container to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = profileCard.HtmlText()
    End Sub
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create the individual HTML elements that will go inside the box.
        var userImage = new HtmlTag("img");
        userImage.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/avatar.png"); // Placeholder image
        userImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "80px");
        userImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "50%"); // Make it circular

        var userName = new Label("Alice");
        userName.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "18px");
        userName.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold");

        var userDept = new Label("Administrator");
        userDept.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.color, "gray");

        // 2. Create the HtmlContentsBox container.
        var profileCard = new HtmlContentsBox();
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "250px");
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "20px");
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.textAlign, "center");
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.boxShadow, "0 2px 5px rgba(0,0,0,0.1)");
        profileCard.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "8px");

        // 3. Add the rendered HTML of the child elements to the container.
        // Each Add call can have its own wrapper style.
        profileCard.Add(userImage.HtmlText(), "margin-bottom:15px;");
        profileCard.Add(userName.HtmlText(), "margin-bottom:5px;");
        profileCard.Add(userDept.HtmlText());
        
        // 4. Add the final container to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = profileCard.HtmlText();
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.HtmlElementBox

  The skilite.ToolKit.HtmlElementBox is a powerful server-side layout container designed to arrange other UI controls vertically. It inherits from skylite.HtmlTag, giving it the standard SetStyle and SetAttribute capabilities. Its key feature is the AddItem(Object, [Integer]) method, which accepts SkyLite UI control objects directly, rather than their rendered HTML strings. This allows the HtmlElementBox to manage the layout internally. The optional integer parameter in AddItem specifies a fixed vertical spacing (in pixels) to be inserted after the control, providing a simple mechanism for controlling the vertical rhythm of a form or content block. It is one of the most fundamental layout tools in the framework for building structured, top-to-bottom page designs.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a control object
Public Sub AddItem(control As Object, Optional spacing As Integer = 0)
C#
// Main constructor
public HtmlElementBox()

// Method to add a control object
public void AddItem(object control, int spacing = 0)
Parameters:
  • control (Object): An instance of a SkyLite UI control (e.g., Texts, Button, Grid).
  • spacing (Integer) (Optional): The height in pixels of a "div" spacer to be inserted after the control.
Usage and Example:

The HtmlElementBox is the ideal choice for building the main container of a standard data-entry form. This example demonstrates creating a simple contact form by adding Label, Texts, TextArea, and Button controls to an HtmlElementBox, using the spacing parameter to ensure a clean vertical layout.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SubmitForm() {
    var name = document.getElementById('contact-name').value;
    var message = document.getElementById('contact-message').value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'name', vlu: name },
        { key: 'message', vlu: message }
    ];
    
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the individual UI controls for the form.
        Dim formTitle As New Label("Contact Us")
        formTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "22px")
        formTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold")

        Dim nameInput As New Texts("Your Name:", "contact-name", TextTypes.text)
        nameInput.Alignment = Alignments.Vertical ' Label above input

        Dim messageInput As New TextArea("Message:", Alignments.Vertical)
        messageInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "contact-message")
        messageInput.Text.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "100px")
        
        Dim submitButton As New Button("Send Message", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        submitButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SubmitForm()")

        ' 2. Create the HtmlElementBox to act as the form container.
        Dim formContainer As New HtmlElementBox()
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "400px")
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.margin, "20px auto") ' Center the box
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "20px")
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc")
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "5px")

        ' 3. Add the control objects to the container with vertical spacing.
        formContainer.AddItem(formTitle, 20) ' Add title with 20px space below
        formContainer.AddItem(nameInput, 15) ' Add name input with 15px space below
        formContainer.AddItem(messageInput, 20) ' Add message input with 20px space below
        formContainer.AddItem(submitButton) ' Add button with default (0px) spacing

        ' 4. Add the final container to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = formContainer.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function SubmitForm() As ApiResponse
        Dim name As String = GetDataValue("name")
        Dim message As String = GetDataValue("message")

        Dim responseMessage As String = "Thank you, " & name & "! Your message has been received."
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(responseMessage)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create the individual UI controls for the form.
        var formTitle = new Label("Contact Us");
        formTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "22px");
        formTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold");

        var nameInput = new Texts("Your Name:", "contact-name", TextTypes.text);
        nameInput.Alignment = Alignments.Vertical; // Label above input

        var messageInput = new TextArea("Message:", Alignments.Vertical);
        messageInput.Text.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "contact-message");
        messageInput.Text.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "100px");

        var submitButton = new Button("Send Message", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        submitButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SubmitForm()");

        // 2. Create the HtmlElementBox to act as the form container.
        var formContainer = new HtmlElementBox();
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "400px");
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.margin, "20px auto"); // Center the box
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "20px");
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc");
        formContainer.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "5px");

        // 3. Add the control objects to the container with vertical spacing.
        formContainer.AddItem(formTitle, 20); // Add title with 20px space below
        formContainer.AddItem(nameInput, 15); // Add name input with 15px space below
        formContainer.AddItem(messageInput, 20); // Add message input with 20px space below
        formContainer.AddItem(submitButton); // Add button with default (0px) spacing

        // 4. Add the final container to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = formContainer.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse SubmitForm()
    {
        string name = GetDataValue("name");
        string message = GetDataValue("message");

        string responseMessage = $"Thank you, {name}! Your message has been received.";
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(responseMessage);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.HtmlWrapper

  The skilite.ToolKit.HtmlWrapper is a server-side container control that generates a [div] element designed to encapsulate and group other SkyLite UI control objects. It inherits from skylite.HtmlTag, which provides it with the standard SetStyle and SetAttribute methods for direct customization of the wrapper [div]. Its primary method, AddContents(Object, [Integer]), accepts UI control instances directly, similar to HtmlElementBox. However, HtmlWrapper is a simpler container; its main purpose is to bundle a set of related controls into a single, logical unit. This encapsulation is particularly useful for creating components or panels that can be manipulated as a whole—for instance, showing, hiding, or styling an entire group of controls with a single ApiResponse command that targets the wrapper's ID.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a control object
Public Sub AddContents(control As Object, Optional spacing As Integer = 0)
C#
// Main constructor
public HtmlWrapper()

// Method to add a control object
public void AddContents(object control, int spacing = 0)
Parameters:
  • control (Object): An instance of a SkyLite UI control (e.g., Texts, Button, Grid).
  • spacing (Integer) (Optional): The height in pixels of a
    spacer to be inserted after the control.
Usage and Example:

The HtmlWrapper is ideal for creating collapsible sections or tool panels. This example demonstrates building a "Search Panel" that can be shown or hidden by clicking buttons. The HtmlWrapper groups the search input and a dropdown, allowing us to control their visibility with a single command targeting the wrapper.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ShowPanel() {
    // Calls the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}

function HidePanel() {
    // Calls the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the controls that will toggle the wrapper's visibility.
        Dim showButton As New Button("Show Search Panel", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        showButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ShowPanel()")

        Dim hideButton As New Button("Hide Search Panel", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        hideButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "HidePanel()")

        ' 2. Create the HtmlWrapper to encapsulate the search controls.
        Dim searchWrapper As New HtmlWrapper()
        searchWrapper.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "search-panel")
        ' Start with the panel hidden.
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.display, "none")
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc")
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "15px")
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "10px")

        ' 3. Create the controls that go INSIDE the wrapper.
        Dim categoryDropdown As New Dropdown("Category:")
        Dim keywordInput As New Texts("Keyword:")

        ' 4. Add the control OBJECTS to the wrapper.
        searchWrapper.AddContents(categoryDropdown)
        searchWrapper.AddContents(keywordInput)

        ' 5. Add all elements to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = showButton.HtmlText() & hideButton.HtmlText() & searchWrapper.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ShowPanel() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Target the wrapper's ID to show it.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("search-panel", "display", "block")
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    Public Function HidePanel() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Target the wrapper's ID to hide it.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("search-panel", "display", "none")
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create the controls that will toggle the wrapper's visibility.
        var showButton = new Button("Show Search Panel", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        showButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ShowPanel()");

        var hideButton = new Button("Hide Search Panel", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        hideButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "HidePanel()");

        // 2. Create the HtmlWrapper to encapsulate the search controls.
        var searchWrapper = new HtmlWrapper();
        searchWrapper.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "search-panel");
        // Start with the panel hidden.
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.display, "none");
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc");
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "15px");
        searchWrapper.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "10px");

        // 3. Create the controls that go INSIDE the wrapper.
        var categoryDropdown = new Dropdown("Category:");
        var keywordInput = new Texts("Keyword:");

        // 4. Add the control OBJECTS to the wrapper.
        searchWrapper.AddContents(categoryDropdown);
        searchWrapper.AddContents(keywordInput);
        
        // 5. Add all elements to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = showButton.HtmlText() + hideButton.HtmlText() + searchWrapper.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse ShowPanel()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Target the wrapper's ID to show it.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("search-panel", "display", "block");
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    public ApiResponse HidePanel()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Target the wrapper's ID to hide it.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementStyle("search-panel", "display", "none");
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.iFrame

  The skilite.ToolKit.iFrame control is a server-side object that generates an HTML [iframe] element. An [iframe] is used to embed another HTML document within the current page, effectively creating a nested browsing context. This control inherits from skilite.HtmlTag, providing it with the standard methods to set any HTML attribute, such as src (to specify the URL of the document to embed), width, height, and id. The iFrame control is particularly useful for displaying content from an external source, showing a separate page from the same application without a full redirect, or isolating third-party content. Its content can be dynamically changed from the server using the ApiResponse.SetIFrameContents method, which allows for updating the embedded document in real-time.

Constructor:
VB
' Constructors
Public Sub New()
Public Sub New(srcUrl As String)
C#
// Constructors
public iFrame()
public iFrame(string srcUrl)
Parameters:
  • srcUrl (String) (Optional): The URL of the page or resource to be embedded in the iframe.
Usage and Example:

The iFrame control is ideal for displaying content from other pages dynamically. This example demonstrates a set of buttons that, when clicked, load different pages from the same application into an iFrame on the current page.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function LoadPage(pageName) {
    var data = [ { key: 'pageName', vlu: pageName } ];
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create buttons to control the iFrame's content.
        Dim page1Button As New Button("Load Page 1", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        page1Button.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "LoadPage('Page1')")

        Dim page2Button As New Button("Load Page 2", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        page2Button.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "LoadPage('Page2')")

        ' 2. Create the iFrame control.
        Dim contentFrame As New iFrame()
        contentFrame.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "content-iframe")
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "100%")
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "400px")
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc")
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "10px")

        ' 3. Add all controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = page1Button.HtmlText() & page2Button.HtmlText() & contentFrame.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    ' This function changes the src of the iFrame.
    Public Function LoadPage() As ApiResponse
        Dim pageName As String = GetDataValue("pageName")

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        
        ' Note: This reloads the iFrame to a new URL. For setting inner HTML directly,
        ' you would use SetIFrameContents.
        ' Here, we change the 'src' attribute.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementAttribute("content-iframe", "src", pageName)

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create buttons to control the iFrame's content.
        var page1Button = new Button("Load Page 1", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        page1Button.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "LoadPage('Page1')");

        var page2Button = new Button("Load Page 2", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        page2Button.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "LoadPage('Page2')");

        // 2. Create the iFrame control.
        var contentFrame = new iFrame();
        contentFrame.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "content-iframe");
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "100%");
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "400px");
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc");
        contentFrame.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "10px");
        
        // 3. Add all controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = page1Button.HtmlText() + page2Button.HtmlText() + contentFrame.HtmlText();
    }

    // This function changes the src of the iFrame.
    public ApiResponse LoadPage()
    {
        string pageName = GetDataValue("pageName");

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();

        // Note: This reloads the iFrame to a new URL. For setting inner HTML directly,
        // you would use SetIFrameContents.
        // Here, we change the 'src' attribute.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementAttribute("content-iframe", "src", pageName);

        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.ImageBox

  The skylite.ToolKit.ImageBox is a server-side UI control designed to display an image. It is a composite control that generates an HTML [img] element enclosed within a [div] wrapper. This structure provides distinct advantages for customization: the inner [img] tag is accessible via the Image property for setting attributes like src and alt, while the outer [div] is accessible via the Wrap property for applying layout styles, borders, or padding. The control also includes a convenience method, SetCenterAlignView(), which applies default CSS to horizontally center the image within its container. The ImageBox is the standard control for displaying static or dynamic images such as logos, product photos, or user avatars.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
C#
// Main constructor
public ImageBox()
Parameters:
  • None
Usage and Example:

The ImageBox is ideal for displaying an image that can be dynamically updated based on user actions. This example demonstrates a simple image viewer with two buttons that change the source of the image displayed in the ImageBox control.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ChangeImage(imageName) {
    // Package the name of the new image file to send to the server.
    var data = [
        { key: 'imageName', vlu: imageName }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function with the same name.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the ImageBox control.
        Dim myImageBox As New ImageBox()
        myImageBox.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc")
        myImageBox.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "10px")
        myImageBox.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.display, "inline-block")

        ' Set the ID and initial source on the inner <img> tag.
        myImageBox.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "display-image")
        myImageBox.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/photo1.jpg") ' Default image
        myImageBox.Image.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "300px")

        ' 2. Create buttons to change the image.
        Dim button1 As New Button("Show Photo 1", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        button1.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ChangeImage('photo1.jpg')")

        Dim button2 As New Button("Show Photo 2", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        button2.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ChangeImage('photo2.jpg')")

        ' 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = button1.HtmlText() & button2.HtmlText() & "<br/>" & myImageBox.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ChangeImage() As ApiResponse
        Dim imageName As String = GetDataValue("imageName")
        Dim imagePath As String = "/images/" & imageName

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Update the 'src' attribute of the <img> tag.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementAttribute("display-image", "src", imagePath)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create the ImageBox control.
        var myImageBox = new ImageBox();
        myImageBox.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ccc");
        myImageBox.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "10px");
        myImageBox.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.display, "inline-block");

        // Set the ID and initial source on the inner <img> tag.
        myImageBox.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "display-image");
        myImageBox.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/photo1.jpg"); // Default image
        myImageBox.Image.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "300px");

        // 2. Create buttons to change the image.
        var button1 = new Button("Show Photo 1", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        button1.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ChangeImage('photo1.jpg')");

        var button2 = new Button("Show Photo 2", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        button2.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ChangeImage('photo2.jpg')");

        // 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = button1.HtmlText() + button2.HtmlText() + "<br/>" + myImageBox.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse ChangeImage()
    {
        string imageName = GetDataValue("imageName");
        string imagePath = $"/images/{imageName}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Update the 'src' attribute of the <img> tag.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementAttribute("display-image", "src", imagePath);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.ImageButton

  The skilite.ToolKit.ImageButton is a server-side control that programmatically generates an HTML [input type="image"] element. This control acts as a graphical submit button, using an image for its appearance instead of text. It inherits directly from skylite.HtmlTag, which allows a developer to set all necessary attributes such as src (to define the image path), alt (for accessibility), and id. In the context of the SkyLite framework, its primary function is to trigger a client-side JavaScript function via the onclick attribute. This JavaScript function then typically uses the built-in $ApiRequest to invoke a corresponding server-side method, enabling AJAX-based actions initiated by a visual, clickable icon.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
C#
// Main constructor
public ImageButton()
Parameters:
  • None. All configuration is done after instantiation using SetAttribute and SetStyle.
Usage and Example:

The ImageButton is perfect for iconic actions like "refresh", "delete", or "add". This example demonstrates using an ImageButton as a refresh icon. When clicked, it calls a server function to get the latest server time and updates a label on the page.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function RefreshData() {
    // No data needs to be sent, just trigger the server function.
    $ApiRequest();
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to display the data that will be refreshed.
        Dim dataLabel As New Label("Data loaded at: " & DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss"))
        dataLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "data-display")
        dataLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginRight, "10px")

        ' 1. Create the ImageButton instance.
        Dim refreshButton As New ImageButton()

        ' 2. Configure the ImageButton using SetAttribute and SetStyle.
        refreshButton.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/refresh-icon.png") ' Placeholder path
        refreshButton.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.alt, "Refresh Data")
        refreshButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "RefreshData()")
        refreshButton.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "24px") ' Set the size of the icon
        refreshButton.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.verticalAlign, "middle")
        refreshButton.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.cursor, "pointer")

        ' 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = dataLabel.HtmlText() & refreshButton.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function RefreshData() As ApiResponse
        Dim serverTime As String = DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss")
        Dim message As String = "Data loaded at: " & serverTime

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("data-display", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to display the data that will be refreshed.
        var dataLabel = new Label($"Data loaded at: {DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss}");
        dataLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "data-display");
        dataLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginRight, "10px");

        // 1. Create the ImageButton instance.
        var refreshButton = new ImageButton();

        // 2. Configure the ImageButton using SetAttribute and SetStyle.
        refreshButton.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/refresh-icon.png"); // Placeholder path
        refreshButton.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.alt, "Refresh Data");
        refreshButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "RefreshData()");
        refreshButton.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "24px"); // Set the size of the icon
        refreshButton.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.verticalAlign, "middle");
        refreshButton.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.cursor, "pointer");

        // 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = dataLabel.HtmlText() + refreshButton.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse RefreshData()
    {
        string serverTime = DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss");
        string message = $"Data loaded at: {serverTime}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("data-display", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.ImageSection

  The skilite.ToolKit.ImageSection is a server-side UI control that generates a [div] wrapper containing a single [img] element. It is a composite control designed specifically for displaying an image as a distinct section on a page. The control provides direct access to the inner [img] tag via its Image property, allowing for the setting of src, alt, and style attributes. It also provides access to the outer [div] via its Wrap property for applying layout and container-level styles like borders or padding. The ImageSection simplifies the common task of adding a styled, standalone image to a webpage by bundling the image and its container into a single, manageable object.

The ImageSection is ideal for displaying a main banner image or a featured product photo on a page. This example demonstrates creating a product display section that includes a title and an ImageSection to show the product.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.

This example is for display purposes only and does not require JavaScript.

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

        ' A title for the section.

        titleLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "24px")
        titleLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold")
        titleLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "10px")

        ' 1. Create the ImageSection instance.
        Dim productImageSection As New ImageSection()
        
        ' 2. Configure the outer wrapper div.
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ddd")
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "15px")
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "5px")
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "fit-content") ' Make wrapper fit image
        productImage.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.margin, "0 auto") ' Center the section

        ' 3. Configure the inner image tag.

        productImageSection.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.alt, "Our featured product")
        productImageSection.Image.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.maxWidth, "400px") ' Make image responsive
        productImageSection.Image.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.display, "block")

        ' 4. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = titleLabel.HtmlText() & productImageSection.HtmlText()
    End Sub

using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A title for the section.
        var titleLabel = new Label("Featured Product");
        titleLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "24px");
        titleLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold");
        titleLabel.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "10px");

        // 1. Create the ImageSection instance.
        var productImageSection = new ImageSection();

        // 2. Configure the outer wrapper div.
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.border, "1px solid #ddd");
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "15px");
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderRadius, "5px");
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "fit-content"); // Make wrapper fit image
        productImageSection.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.margin, "0 auto"); // Center the section

        // 3. Configure the inner image tag.
        productImageSection.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/featured-product.jpg");
        productImageSection.Image.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.alt, "Our featured product");
        productImageSection.Image.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.maxWidth, "400px"); // Make image responsive
        productImageSection.Image.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.display, "block");
