skylite.ToolKit.Paging

  The skilite.ToolKit.Paging is a server-side UI control that generates the HTML and CSS for a pagination interface, typically used in conjunction with a data grid. It does not handle data slicing itself; rather, it calculates the number of page links to display based on parameters provided to its constructor: TotalRecords, CurrentPage, and LinePerPage. The control renders a series of clickable [span] elements, each representing a page number. When a user clicks a page number, an onclick event fires a specified JavaScript function, passing the clicked page number as an argument. This triggers an API request back to the server, which is then responsible for re-querying the data source for the requested page and updating the associated grid.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New(totalRecords As Integer, currentPage As Integer, linePerPage As Integer, Optional jsEvents As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public Paging(int totalRecords, int currentPage, int linePerPage, string jsEvents = "")
Parameters:
  • totalRecords (Integer): The total number of records in the entire dataset.
  • currentPage (Integer): The currently active page number.
  • linePerPage (Integer): The number of records to be displayed on each page.
  • jsEvents (String) (Optional): The name of the JavaScript function to be called when a page number is clicked. The page number will be passed as the first argument to this function.
Usage and Example:

The Paging control is almost always used with a data grid to navigate large datasets. This example demonstrates a page with a grid and a paging control. When a page number is clicked, a server function simulates fetching the data for that page and replaces the content of the grid.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function GoToPage(pageNumber) {
    var data = [ { key: 'page', vlu: pageNumber } ];
    // Call the server-side function, passing the requested page number.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System.Data

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    ' Store these as class-level variables to be accessible by both methods
    Private totalRecords As Integer = 125 ' Total items in our "database"
    Private linesPerPage As Integer = 10

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Initial load is always page 1
        RenderPage(1)
    End Sub

    ' This function handles the logic for displaying a specific page
    Private Sub RenderPage(currentPage As Integer)
        ' 1. Create the data grid.
        Dim dataGridWrap As New Wrap()
        dataGridWrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "grid-container")
        ' Fetch and display the data for the current page.
        dataGridWrap.InnerText = GenerateGridForPage(currentPage).HtmlText()

        ' 2. Create the Paging control.
        Dim pager As New Paging(totalRecords, currentPage, linesPerPage, "GoToPage")

        ' 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = dataGridWrap.HtmlText() & pager.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    ' This function is called by the paging control's onclick event.
    Public Function GoToPage() As ApiResponse
        Dim requestedPage As Integer = Integer.Parse(GetDataValue("page"))
        
        ' Generate the new grid HTML for the requested page.
        Dim newGridHtml As String = GenerateGridForPage(requestedPage).HtmlText()
        
        ' Generate the new pager HTML, now with the new active page.
        Dim newPagerHtml As String = New Paging(totalRecords, requestedPage, linesPerPage, "GoToPage").HtmlText()

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Replace the content of the grid and the pager itself.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement("grid-container", newGridHtml)
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement(newPagerHtml.Split("""")(1), newPagerHtml) ' A bit of a hack to get the ID, but it works

        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    ' Helper function to simulate fetching paged data
    Private Function GenerateGridForPage(pageNumber As Integer) As Grid
        Dim grid As New Grid()
        grid.AddColumn("Record ID")

        Dim startRecord As Integer = ((pageNumber - 1) * linesPerPage) + 1
        Dim endRecord As Integer = startRecord + linesPerPage - 1

        For i As Integer = startRecord To endRecord
            If i <= totalRecords Then
                grid.AddRow({ "Record #" & i.ToString() })
            End If
        Next

        Return grid
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.Data;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    // Store these as class-level variables to be accessible by both methods
    private int totalRecords = 125; // Total items in our "database"
    private int linesPerPage = 10;

    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Initial load is always page 1
        RenderPage(1);
    }

    // This function handles the logic for displaying a specific page
    private void RenderPage(int currentPage)
    {
        // 1. Create the data grid.
        var dataGridWrap = new Wrap();
        dataGridWrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "grid-container");
        // Fetch and display the data for the current page.
        dataGridWrap.InnerText = GenerateGridForPage(currentPage).HtmlText();

        // 2. Create the Paging control.
        var pager = new Paging(totalRecords, currentPage, linesPerPage, "GoToPage");

        // 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = dataGridWrap.HtmlText() + pager.HtmlText();
    }

    // This function is called by the paging control's onclick event.
    public ApiResponse GoToPage()
    {
        int requestedPage = int.Parse(GetDataValue("page"));

        // Generate the new grid HTML for the requested page.
        string newGridHtml = GenerateGridForPage(requestedPage).HtmlText();
        
        // Generate the new pager HTML, now with the new active page.
        var newPager = new Paging(totalRecords, requestedPage, linesPerPage, "GoToPage");
        string newPagerHtml = newPager.HtmlText();

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Replace the content of the grid and the pager itself.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement("grid-container", newGridHtml);
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement(newPager.ID, newPagerHtml);

        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    // Helper function to simulate fetching paged data
    private Grid GenerateGridForPage(int pageNumber)
    {
        var grid = new Grid();
        grid.AddColumn("Record ID");

        int startRecord = ((pageNumber - 1) * linesPerPage) + 1;
        int endRecord = startRecord + linesPerPage - 1;

        for (int i = startRecord; i <= endRecord; i++)
        {
            if (i <= totalRecords)
            {
                grid.AddRow(new string[] { $"Record #{i}" });
            }
        }
        return grid;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Parallax

  The skilite.ToolKit.Parallax is a server-side UI control that generates the HTML structure for a parallax scrolling effect. This effect is a web design technique where background content (typically an image) moves at a different speed than foreground content as the user scrolls, creating an illusion of depth and a dynamic visual experience. The control functions as a container that holds a collection of Parallax.Section objects. Each section represents a distinct content block with its own background image and foreground content. To function correctly, this control requires a built-in CSS file to be added to the page using HtmlDoc.AddCSSFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.ParallaxStyle). The control programmatically generates the necessary nested "div" structure and class names that the corresponding CSS uses to achieve the parallax effect.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a section
Public Sub AddSection(backgroundImageUrl As String, contentsHtml As String, Optional styles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public Parallax()

// Method to add a section
public void AddSection(string backgroundImageUrl, string contentsHtml, string styles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • backgroundImageUrl (String): The URL of the image to be used as the parallax background for the section.
  • contentsHtml (String): The HTML content that will appear in the foreground of the section.
  • styles (String) (Optional): CSS styles for the section's wrapper.
  • attributes (String) (Optional): HTML attributes for the section's wrapper.
Usage and Example:

The Parallax control is used to create visually engaging landing pages or feature sections. This example demonstrates creating a page with two parallax sections, each with a different background image and foreground content, separated by a standard content block.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
This example is for display purposes only and does not require JavaScript.
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Crucial Step: Add the required built-in CSS for the parallax effect.
        HtmlDoc.AddCSSFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.ParallaxStyle)

        ' 1. Create the Parallax container instance.
        Dim parallaxContainer As New Parallax()

        ' 2. Define the foreground content for the sections.
        Dim section1Content As String = "<h1>Welcome to Our Website</h1><p>Discover our amazing products.</p>"
        Dim section2Content As String = "<h2>Our Mission</h2><p>To deliver excellence and innovation.</p>"
        
        ' A standard (non-parallax) content block to place between sections.
        Dim standardContent As String = "<div style='height:200px; padding:20px; background-color:white; text-align:center;'><h3>A Standard Content Section</h3></div>"

        ' 3. Add the sections to the parallax container.
        parallaxContainer.AddSection("/images/background1.jpg", section1Content)
        ' Add the standard content directly to the page's body text
        parallaxContainer.AddSection("/images/background2.jpg", section2Content)

        ' 4. Add the final container to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = parallaxContainer.HtmlText() & standardContent & parallaxContainer.HtmlText()
    End Sub
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Crucial Step: Add the required built-in CSS for the parallax effect.
        HtmlDoc.AddCSSFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.ParallaxStyle);

        // 1. Create the Parallax container instance.
        var parallaxContainer = new Parallax();

        // 2. Define the foreground content for the sections.
        string section1Content = "<h1>Welcome to Our Website</h1><p>Discover our amazing products.</p>";
        string section2Content = "<h2>Our Mission</h2><p>To deliver excellence and innovation.</p>";

        // A standard (non-parallax) content block to place between sections.
        string standardContent = "<div style='height:200px; padding:20px; background-color:white; text-align:center;'><h3>A Standard Content Section</h3></div>";
        
        // 3. Add the sections to the parallax container.
        parallaxContainer.AddSection("/images/background1.jpg", section1Content);
        parallaxContainer.AddSection("/images/background2.jpg", section2Content);

        // 4. Add the final container and standard content to the page.
        // NOTE: The example is conceptual. A real implementation would likely structure this differently.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = parallaxContainer.HtmlText() + standardContent + parallaxContainer.HtmlText();
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Progress

  The skilite.Tool-Kit.Progress is a server-side UI control that generates a visual, step-based progress indicator. It is designed to guide users through a multi-step process, such as a wizard, a checkout flow, or a registration form, by clearly showing which steps are complete, which is current, and which are upcoming. The control renders a horizontal sequence of "mark points" (circles) connected by lines. Each step is added programmatically using the AddItem method, which accepts the text label for that step. The control's appearance is highly customizable through its properties, allowing a developer to define the colors and styles for the mark points and text content in their different states (e.g., current, completed, upcoming).

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a progress step
Public Sub AddItem(HtmlContent As String, Optional HtmlContentStyles As String = "", Optional MarkPointStyles As String = "", Optional HtmlContentAttributes As String = "", Optional MarkPointAttributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public Progress()

// Method to add a progress step
public void AddItem(string HtmlContent, string HtmlContentStyles = "", string MarkPointStyles = "", string HtmlContentAttributes = "", string MarkPointAttributes = "")
Parameters:
  • HtmlContent (String): The text label to display for the progress step.
  • HtmlContentStyles (String) (Optional): CSS styles for the text label.
  • MarkPointStyles (String) (Optional): CSS styles for the step's circular mark point.
  • HtmlContentAttributes (String) (Optional): HTML attributes for the text label's wrapper.
  • MarkPointAttributes (String) (Optional): HTML attributes for the mark point's wrapper.
Usage and Example:

The Progress control is ideal for visually representing the user's location in a workflow. This example demonstrates a simple 3-step process. Buttons allow the user to "navigate" between steps, and with each step, the Progress control is re-rendered to reflect the current state.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SetCurrentStep(stepNumber) {
    var data = [ { key: 'step', vlu: stepNumber } ];
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Initial load is always step 1
        RenderPageForStep(1)
    End Sub

    ' This function is called to show a specific step
    Public Function SetCurrentStep() As ApiResponse
        Dim requestedStep As Integer = Integer.Parse(GetDataValue("step"))
        
        ' Generate the new progress bar HTML for the requested step.
        Dim newProgressBarHtml As String = CreateProgressBar(requestedStep).HtmlText()

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Replace the old progress bar with the new one.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement("progress-bar-container", newProgressBarHtml)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    ' Helper function to render the whole page for a given step
    Private Sub RenderPageForStep(currentStep As Integer)
        ' Create a wrapper so we can easily replace the progress bar.
        Dim progressWrap As New Wrap()
        progressWrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "progress-bar-container")
        progressWrap.InnerText = CreateProgressBar(currentStep).HtmlText()

        ' Create navigation buttons
        Dim btn1 As New Button("Go to Step 1")
        btn1.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SetCurrentStep(1)")
        Dim btn2 As New Button("Go to Step 2")
        btn2.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SetCurrentStep(2)")
        Dim btn3 As New Button("Go to Step 3")
        btn3.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SetCurrentStep(3)")

        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = progressWrap.HtmlText() & "<br/>" & btn1.HtmlText() & btn2.HtmlText() & btn3.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    ' Helper function to create the Progress control for a specific step
    Private Function CreateProgressBar(currentStep As Integer) As Progress
        Dim progressBar As New Progress()
        progressBar.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "80%")
        progressBar.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.margin, "20px auto")

        ' Define styles for different states
        Dim completedStyle As String = "background-color:green;"
        Dim currentStyle As String = "background-color:blue;"
        Dim upcomingStyle As String = "background-color:gray;"

        ' Add items, styling them based on the current step
        progressBar.AddItem("Step 1: Account Info", "", If(currentStep >= 1, completedStyle, upcomingStyle))
        progressBar.Items(0).MarkPoint.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, If(currentStep = 1, currentStyle, If(currentStep > 1, completedStyle, upcomingStyle)))
        
        progressBar.AddItem("Step 2: Shipping", "", If(currentStep >= 2, completedStyle, upcomingStyle))
        progressBar.Items(1).MarkPoint.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, If(currentStep = 2, currentStyle, If(currentStep > 2, completedStyle, upcomingStyle)))
        
        progressBar.AddItem("Step 3: Payment", "", If(currentStep >= 3, completedStyle, upcomingStyle))
        progressBar.Items(2).MarkPoint.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, If(currentStep = 3, currentStyle, If(currentStep > 3, completedStyle, upcomingStyle)))

        Return progressBar
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Initial load is always step 1
        RenderPageForStep(1);
    }

    // This function is called to show a specific step
    public ApiResponse SetCurrentStep()
    {
        int requestedStep = int.Parse(GetDataValue("step"));

        // Generate the new progress bar HTML for the requested step.
        string newProgressBarHtml = CreateProgressBar(requestedStep).HtmlText();

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Replace the old progress bar with the new one.
        _ApiResponse.ReplaceElement("progress-bar-container", newProgressBarHtml);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    // Helper function to render the whole page for a given step
    private void RenderPageForStep(int currentStep)
    {
        // Create a wrapper so we can easily replace the progress bar.
        var progressWrap = new Wrap();
        progressWrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "progress-bar-container");
        progressWrap.InnerText = CreateProgressBar(currentStep).HtmlText();
        
        // Create navigation buttons
        var btn1 = new Button("Go to Step 1");
        btn1.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SetCurrentStep(1)");
        var btn2 = new Button("Go to Step 2");
        btn2.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SetCurrentStep(2)");
        var btn3 = new Button("Go to Step 3");
        btn3.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SetCurrentStep(3)");
        
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = progressWrap.HtmlText() + "<br/>" + btn1.HtmlText() + btn2.HtmlText() + btn3.HtmlText();
    }

    // Helper function to create the Progress control for a specific step
    private Progress CreateProgressBar(int currentStep)
    {
        var progressBar = new Progress();
        progressBar.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.width, "80%");
        progressBar.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.margin, "20px auto");
        
        // Define styles for different states
        string completedStyle = "background-color:green;";
        string currentStyle = "background-color:blue;";
        string upcomingStyle = "background-color:gray;";
        
        // Add items, styling them based on the current step
        progressBar.AddItem("Step 1: Account Info", "", (currentStep >= 1) ? completedStyle : upcomingStyle);
        progressBar.Items[0].MarkPoint.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, (currentStep == 1) ? currentStyle : (currentStep > 1) ? completedStyle : upcomingStyle);

        progressBar.AddItem("Step 2: Shipping", "", (currentStep >= 2) ? completedStyle : upcomingStyle);
        progressBar.Items[1].MarkPoint.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, (currentStep == 2) ? currentStyle : (currentStep > 2) ? completedStyle : upcomingStyle);
        
        progressBar.AddItem("Step 3: Payment", "", (currentStep >= 3) ? completedStyle : upcomingStyle);
        progressBar.Items[2].MarkPoint.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.backgroundColor, (currentStep == 3) ? currentStyle : (currentStep > 3) ? completedStyle : upcomingStyle);

        return progressBar;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Radio

  The skilite.Tool-Kit.Radio control is a server-side object that generates a group of HTML [input type="radio"] elements, each with an associated [label]. It is designed for scenarios where a user must select only one option from a mutually exclusive set. The available choices are defined and managed by a separate OptionValues helper object, which is then assigned to the Radio control's Radios property. Critically, all radio buttons generated by a single Radio control share the same name attribute (specified during the AddItem call in OptionValues), which is the HTML mechanism that ensures only one can be selected at a time. The control provides properties for layout (Alignment) and styling of both the radio inputs and their labels (RadioStyles, RadioLabelStyles, etc.).

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New(labelText As String, Optional toolAlignment As Alignments = Alignments.Vertical)
C#
// Main constructor
public Radio(string labelText, Alignments toolAlignment = Alignments.Vertical)
Parameters:
  • labelText (String): The text for the main label of the radio button group (e.g., "Select an Option:").
  • toolAlignment (Alignments) (Optional): An enum that specifies if the individual radio buttons should be arranged vertically or horizontally. Defaults to Vertical.
Usage and Example:

The Radio control is a staple of web forms, used for single-choice questions. This example demonstrates a simple survey question where a user selects their experience level. Clicking a "Submit" button sends the single selected value to the server.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function SubmitChoice() {
    // Find all radio buttons with the name 'experienceLevel'
    var radios = document.getElementsByName('experienceLevel');
    var selectedValue = '';
    
    // Loop through them to find the one that is checked
    for (var i = 0; i < radios.length; i++) {
        if (radios[i].checked) {
            selectedValue = radios[i].value;
            break; // Exit the loop once the checked one is found
        }
    }

    if (selectedValue) {
        var data = [ { key: 'level', vlu: selectedValue } ];
        $ApiRequest('SubmitChoice', JSON.stringify(data));
    } else {
        alert('Please select an option.');
    }
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to display the result.
        Dim resultLabel As New Label("No selection has been submitted.")
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "result-label")

        ' 1. Create the Radio control.
        Dim experienceRadio As New Radio("What is your experience level?", Alignments.Vertical)

        ' 2. Create the options for the radio buttons.
        Dim levelOptions As New OptionValues()
        ' AddItem(value, text, name) - 'name' must be the same for the group.
        levelOptions.AddItem("Beginner", "Beginner (Less than 1 year)", "experienceLevel")
        levelOptions.AddItem("Intermediate", "Intermediate (1-3 years)", "experienceLevel")
        levelOptions.AddItem("Advanced", "Advanced (3+ years)", "experienceLevel")

        ' 3. Assign the options to the Radio control.
        experienceRadio.Radios = levelOptions

        ' A button to submit the choice.
        Dim submitButton As New Button("Submit", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        submitButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SubmitChoice()")

        ' Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = experienceRadio.HtmlText() & submitButton.HtmlText() & resultLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function SubmitChoice() As ApiResponse
        ' The 'level' value will be the single selected radio button's value.
        Dim selectedLevel As String = GetDataValue("level")

        Dim message As String = "You submitted the experience level: " & selectedLevel

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("result-label", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to display the result.
        var resultLabel = new Label("No selection has been submitted.");
        resultLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "result-label");

        // 1. Create the Radio control.
        var experienceRadio = new Radio("What is your experience level?", Alignments.Vertical);

        // 2. Create the options for the radio buttons.
        var levelOptions = new OptionValues();
        // AddItem(value, text, name) - 'name' must be the same for the group.
        levelOptions.AddItem("Beginner", "Beginner (Less than 1 year)", "experienceLevel");
        levelOptions.AddItem("Intermediate", "Intermediate (1-3 years)", "experienceLevel");
        levelOptions.AddItem("Advanced", "Advanced (3+ years)", "experienceLevel");

        // 3. Assign the options to the Radio control.
        experienceRadio.Radios = levelOptions;

        // A button to submit the choice.
        var submitButton = new Button("Submit", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        submitButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "SubmitChoice()");

        // Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = experienceRadio.HtmlText() + submitButton.HtmlText() + resultLabel.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse SubmitChoice()
    {
        // The 'level' value will be the single selected radio button's value.
        string selectedLevel = GetDataValue("level");

        string message = $"You submitted the experience level: {selectedLevel}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("result-label", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.SideBarSection

  The skilite.ToolKit.SideBarSection is a high-level, server-side layout control that generates the complete HTML and JavaScript-driven structure for a common application interface: a fixed sidebar menu on the left and a main content area on the right. It is a composite control that encapsulates objects for the TitleWrap, the MenuBox, and the ContentsBox. Menu items are added programmatically to the sidebar using the AddMenuItem method. This control is dependent on a built-in JavaScript library, which must be included via HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.SideBarScript) and initialized with HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$InitSideBar()"). This script handles the interactive behaviors, such as toggling the sidebar's visibility. The primary purpose of this control is to provide the foundational layout for an entire application dashboard or a main page with persistent navigation.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a menu item
Public Sub AddMenuItem(text As String, onclick As String)
' Overloaded method to add a pre-configured Menu object
Public Sub AddMenuItem(menuItem As SideBarSection.Menu)
C#
// Main constructor
public SideBarSection()

// Method to add a menu item
public void AddMenuItem(string text, string onclick)
// Overloaded method to add a pre-configured Menu object
public void AddMenuItem(SideBarSection.Menu menuItem)
Parameters:
  • text (String): The visible text for the menu item.
  • onclick (String): The JavaScript function to call when the menu item is clicked.
  • menuItem (SideBarSection.Menu): An instance of the SideBarSection.Menu class, which allows for more detailed configuration (e.g., adding an image).
Usage and Example:

The SideBarSection is the ideal control for building the main interface of an application. This example demonstrates creating a simple dashboard where clicking items in the sidebar loads different content into the main area.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function LoadContent(sectionName) {
    var data = [ { key: 'section', vlu: sectionName } ];
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Crucial Step 1: Add the required built-in JavaScript file.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.SideBarScript)

        ' 1. Create the SideBarSection instance.
        Dim mainLayout As New SideBarSection()

        ' 2. Configure the sidebar.
        mainLayout.TitleWrap.InnerText = "My Application"
        mainLayout.AddMenuItem("Dashboard", "LoadContent('dashboard')")
        mainLayout.AddMenuItem("Profile", "LoadContent('profile')")
        mainLayout.AddMenuItem("Settings", "LoadContent('settings')")
        
        ' 3. Configure the main content area.
        ' Set an ID so we can easily update it.
        mainLayout.ContentsBox.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "main-content")
        mainLayout.ContentsBox.InnerText = "<h2>Welcome to the Dashboard!</h2>"

        ' 4. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = mainLayout.HtmlText()

        ' Crucial Step 2: Initialize the sidebar script after the page loads.
        HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$InitSideBar()")
    End Sub

    ' This function is called by the menu item clicks.
    Public Function LoadContent() As ApiResponse
        Dim section As String = GetDataValue("section")
        Dim contentHtml As String = ""

        Select Case section
            Case "dashboard"
                contentHtml = "<h2>Dashboard</h2><p>Here are your latest stats.</p>"
            Case "profile"
                contentHtml = "<h2>My Profile</h2><p>Update your personal information here.</p>"
            Case "settings"
                contentHtml = "<h2>Settings</h2><p>Manage your application settings.</p>"
        End Select

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("main-content", contentHtml)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Crucial Step 1: Add the required built-in JavaScript file.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.SideBarScript);

        // 1. Create the SideBarSection instance.
        var mainLayout = new SideBarSection();

        // 2. Configure the sidebar.
        mainLayout.TitleWrap.InnerText = "My Application";
        mainLayout.AddMenuItem("Dashboard", "LoadContent('dashboard')");
        mainLayout.AddMenuItem("Profile", "LoadContent('profile')");
        mainLayout.AddMenuItem("Settings", "LoadContent('settings')");
        
        // 3. Configure the main content area.
        // Set an ID so we can easily update it.
        mainLayout.ContentsBox.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "main-content");
        mainLayout.ContentsBox.InnerText = "<h2>Welcome to the Dashboard!</h2>";
        
        // 4. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = mainLayout.HtmlText();

        // Crucial Step 2: Initialize the sidebar script after the page loads.
        HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$InitSideBar()");
    }

    // This function is called by the menu item clicks.
    public ApiResponse LoadContent()
    {
        string section = GetDataValue("section");
        string contentHtml = "";

        switch (section)
        {
            case "dashboard":
                contentHtml = "<h2>Dashboard</h2><p>Here are your latest stats.</p>";
                break;
            case "profile":
                contentHtml = "<h2>My Profile</h2><p>Update your personal information here.</p>";
                break;
            case "settings":
                contentHtml = "<h2>Settings</h2><p>Manage your application settings.</p>";
                break;
        }

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("main-content", contentHtml);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.SQLGridSection

  The skilite.ToolKit.SQLGridSection is a high-level, all-in-one, server-side UI control that automates the display of data from a SQL database. It is one of the most powerful controls in the toolkit, encapsulating a DataGrid, a Paging control, a title bar with record counts, and built-in functionality for server-side sorting and data downloading. Instead of being bound to a DataTable, it is configured with a SQLGridInfo object. This object defines the database connection (TableSource), the SQL query details (tables, columns, order by via the SQLQuery object), pagination settings, and column display properties. The control handles the entire data retrieval, paging, and rendering lifecycle internally. When a user interacts with the control (e.g., clicks a page number or a sortable header), the SQLGridSection automatically re-queries the database with the appropriate parameters and refreshes itself on the client-side.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New(gridInfo As SQLGridInfo)
C#
// Main constructor
public SQLGridSection(SQLGridInfo gridInfo)
Parameters:
  • gridInfo (SQLGridInfo): A comprehensive configuration object that specifies everything the grid needs to fetch and display its data.
Usage and Example:

The SQLGridSection is the ideal solution for quickly creating a fully functional, paged, and sortable data display with minimal code. This example demonstrates how to configure a SQLGridInfo object to display a list of employees from a database table.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
No custom JavaScript is required. The control's built-in scripts handle all user interactions like paging and sorting automatically.
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create and configure the SQLGridInfo object.
        Dim gridConfig As New SQLGridSection.SQLGridInfo() With {
            .Id = "employeeGrid",
            .Name = "Employee List",
            .LinesPerPage = 10, ' Show 10 records per page
            .TableSource = SQLGridSection.SQLGridInfo.DBsource.MSSQL, ' Specify the DB type
            .Query = New SQLGridSection.SQLQuery() With {
                .Tables = "Employees", ' The database table to query
                .Columns = {"EmployeeID", "FirstName", "LastName", "Title"}, ' Columns to display
                .ColumnAlias = {"ID", "First Name", "Last Name", "Job Title"}, ' Headers for the grid
                .OrderBy = {"LastName"}, ' Initial sort order
                .SortHeaders = {"LastName", "FirstName"} ' Make these headers clickable for sorting
            }
        }

        ' 2. Create the SQLGridSection instance with the configuration.
        Dim employeeGrid As New SQLGridSection(gridConfig)

        ' 3. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = employeeGrid.HtmlText()
    End Sub

End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create and configure the SQLGridInfo object.
        var gridConfig = new SQLGridSection.SQLGridInfo
        {
            Id = "employeeGrid",
            Name = "Employee List",
            LinesPerPage = 10, // Show 10 records per page
            TableSource = SQLGridSection.SQLGridInfo.DBsource.MSSQL, // Specify the DB type
            Query = new SQLGridSection.SQLQuery
            {
                Tables = "Employees", // The database table to query
                Columns = new string[] { "EmployeeID", "FirstName", "LastName", "Title" }, // Columns to display
                ColumnAlias = new string[] { "ID", "First Name", "Last Name", "Job Title" }, // Headers for the grid
                OrderBy = new string[] { "LastName" }, // Initial sort order
                SortHeaders = new string[] { "LastName", "FirstName" } // Make these headers clickable for sorting
            }
        };

        // 2. Create the SQLGridSection instance with the configuration.
        var employeeGrid = new SQLGridSection(gridConfig);

        // 3. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = employeeGrid.HtmlText();
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Stacker

  The skilite.ToolKit.Stacker is a server-side layout control that generates a multi-column, side-by-side content structure using a Flexbox-based [div] container. It is designed to create primary page layouts, such as a navigation pane next to a content area. The control manages a collection of Stacker.Column objects. Each column is added programmatically via the AddColumn method and can contain any raw HTML content, including the rendered HTML of other complex SkyLite controls. The AddColumn method allows for precise control over each column's width and other CSS styles. Additionally, a vertical AddDivider can be inserted between columns, which can also be styled to act as a visual separator or a resizable handle.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a column
Public Sub AddColumn(contents As String, Optional styles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
' Method to add a divider
Public Sub AddDivider(Optional styles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public Stacker()

// Method to add a column
public void AddColumn(string contents, string styles = "", string attributes = "")
// Method to add a divider
public void AddDivider(string styles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • contents (String): The HTML string to be placed inside the column.
  • styles (String) (Optional): CSS styles for the column
    . Width is typically defined here (e.g., "width:250px;").
  • attributes (String) (Optional): HTML attributes for the column
    , such as id.
Usage and Example:

The Stacker is the ideal control for building the main layout of a page that requires a sidebar and a content area. This example demonstrates creating a two-column layout where the left column contains a TreeView for navigation and the right column contains a Wrap to act as the main content display area.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function TreeItemSelected(itemId) {
    var data = [ { key: 'itemId', vlu: itemId } ];
    $ApiRequest('TreeItemSelected', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System.Collections.Generic

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Crucial Step: Add the TreeView script.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TreeScript)

        ' 1. Create the content for the left column (a TreeView).
        Dim navTree As New TreeView("nav-tree")
        Dim treeItems As New List(Of TreeView.TreeItem)
        treeItems.Add(New TreeView.TreeItem With {.Id = "dash", .Name = "Dashboard"})
        treeItems.Add(New TreeView.TreeItem With {.Id = "users", .Name = "Users"})
        navTree.TreeItems = treeItems
        navTree.TreeItemClick = "TreeItemSelected(this.id)"

        ' 2. Create the content for the right column (a simple wrapper).
        Dim contentArea As New Wrap()
        contentArea.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "content-area")
        contentArea.InnerText = "<h2>Welcome!</h2><p>Select an item from the menu on the left.</p>"

        ' 3. Create the Stacker instance.
        Dim mainLayout As New Stacker()
        mainLayout.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "500px") ' Give the stacker a height

        ' 4. Add the columns to the Stacker.
        mainLayout.AddColumn(navTree.HtmlText(), "width:250px; border-right:1px solid #ccc; padding:10px;")
        mainLayout.AddColumn(contentArea.HtmlText(), "width:calc(100% - 250px); padding:10px;", "id:main-content-column")
        
        ' 5. Add the final layout to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = mainLayout.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function TreeItemSelected() As ApiResponse
        Dim itemId As String = GetDataValue("itemId")
        Dim content As String = "<h2>Content for " & itemId & "</h2><p>This is the content loaded for the selected menu item.</p>"
        
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Update the contents of the right-hand column's inner wrapper.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("content-area", content)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System.Collections.Generic;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Crucial Step: Add the TreeView script.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TreeScript);
        
        // 1. Create the content for the left column (a TreeView).
        var navTree = new TreeView("nav-tree");
        var treeItems = new List<TreeView.TreeItem>
        {
            new TreeView.TreeItem { Id = "dash", Name = "Dashboard" },
            new TreeView.TreeItem { Id = "users", Name = "Users" }
        };
        navTree.TreeItems = treeItems;
        navTree.TreeItemClick = "TreeItemSelected(this.id)";

        // 2. Create the content for the right column (a simple wrapper).
        var contentArea = new Wrap();
        contentArea.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "content-area");
        contentArea.InnerText = "<h2>Welcome!</h2><p>Select an item from the menu on the left.</p>";

        // 3. Create the Stacker instance.
        var mainLayout = new Stacker();
        mainLayout.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "500px"); // Give the stacker a height
        
        // 4. Add the columns to the Stacker.
        mainLayout.AddColumn(navTree.HtmlText(), "width:250px; border-right:1px solid #ccc; padding:10px;");
        mainLayout.AddColumn(contentArea.HtmlText(), "width:calc(100% - 250px); padding:10px;", "id:main-content-column");

        // 5. Add the final layout to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = mainLayout.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse TreeItemSelected()
    {
        string itemId = GetDataValue("itemId");
        string content = $"<h2>Content for {itemId}</h2><p>This is the content loaded for the selected menu item.</p>";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Update the contents of the right-hand column's inner wrapper.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("content-area", content);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Switch

  The skilite.ToolKit.Switch is a server-side UI control that generates a modern, CSS-driven toggle switch. It renders as an HTML [label] element containing a visually hidden [input type="checkbox"] and a [span] element that is styled to create the sliding toggle appearance. This provides a user-friendly, boolean (on/off) input that is functionally equivalent to a single checkbox but with a more contemporary aesthetic. The control's state can be set on the server via the Checked property. On the client-side, JavaScript can determine its state by inspecting the checked property of the underlying checkbox input. This control is ideal for settings, preferences, or any binary choice in a user interface.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
' Constructor with text, onclick, and styles
Public Sub New(text As String, Optional onclick As String = "", Optional styles As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public Switch()
// Constructor with text, onclick, and styles
public Switch(string text, string onclick = "", string styles = "")
Parameters:
  • text (String): The text for the
  • onclick (String) (Optional): The JavaScript function to call when the switch is clicked.
  • styles (String) (Optional): CSS styles for the switch's main wrapper.
Usage and Example:

The Switch control is perfect for enabling or disabling a feature. This example demonstrates a "Notifications" switch. When the user toggles the switch, an onclick event immediately calls a server function, which then confirms the new state and updates a status label.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function ToggleNotifications(switchElement) {
    // Determine the new state (true or false) of the switch.
    var isEnabled = switchElement.checked;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'isEnabled', vlu: isEnabled }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function with the new state.
    $ApiRequest('ToggleNotifications', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' A label to display the current status.
        Dim statusLabel As New Label("Status: Notifications are currently ON.")
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label")

        ' 1. Create the Switch control.
        Dim notificationSwitch As New Switch()
        notificationSwitch.Label.InnerText = "Enable Notifications"
        ' Set the ID on the underlying checkbox for JavaScript access.
        notificationSwitch.Id = "notification-switch"
        ' Set the initial state.
        notificationSwitch.Checked = True
        ' Add the onclick event to the underlying checkbox.
        notificationSwitch.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ToggleNotifications(this)")

        ' 2. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = notificationSwitch.HtmlText() & statusLabel.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function ToggleNotifications() As ApiResponse
        ' GetDataValue will return "true" or "false" as a string.
        Dim isEnabled As Boolean = Boolean.Parse(GetDataValue("isEnabled"))
        Dim message As String = ""

        If isEnabled Then
            message = "Status: Notifications have been turned ON."
            ' (Perform logic to enable notifications...)
        Else
            message = "Status: Notifications have been turned OFF."
            ' (Perform logic to disable notifications...)
        End If

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // A label to display the current status.
        var statusLabel = new Label("Status: Notifications are currently ON.");
        statusLabel.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "status-label");

        // 1. Create the Switch control.
        var notificationSwitch = new Switch();
        notificationSwitch.Label.InnerText = "Enable Notifications";
        // Set the ID on the underlying checkbox for JavaScript access.
        notificationSwitch.Id = "notification-switch";
        // Set the initial state.
        notificationSwitch.Checked = true;
        // Add the onclick event to the underlying checkbox.
        notificationSwitch.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "ToggleNotifications(this)");

        // 2. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = notificationSwitch.HtmlText() + statusLabel.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse ToggleNotifications()
    {
        // GetDataValue will return "true" or "false" as a string.
        bool isEnabled = bool.Parse(GetDataValue("isEnabled"));
        string message = "";

        if (isEnabled)
        {
            message = "Status: Notifications have been turned ON.";
            // (Perform logic to enable notifications...)
        }
        else
        {
            message = "Status: Notifications have been turned OFF.";
            // (Perform logic to disable notifications...)
        }

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("status-label", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.TabStrip

  The skilite.ToolKit.TabStrip is a server-side UI control that generates the HTML and CSS for a tabbed content interface. It is a composite control that manages a set of clickable tab headers and a single content area. Each tab is added programmatically using the AddTab method. The control relies on a built-in JavaScript library to function, which must be included in the page via HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TabStripScript). This script handles the client-side logic of showing and hiding content when a tab is clicked. The content for the currently active tab is displayed in the main TabContent area. This content can be set initially using the InitialContent property, and it can be updated dynamically by calling a server-side function (specified in the TabStripClick property) that returns an ApiResponse with a command to update the TabContent's inner HTML.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()

' Method to add a tab
Public Sub AddTab(text As String, Optional styles As String = "", Optional attributes As String = "")
C#
// Main constructor
public TabStrip()

// Method to add a tab
public void AddTab(string text, string styles = "", string attributes = "")
Parameters:
  • text (String): The visible text label for the tab header.
  • styles (String) (Optional): CSS styles for the tab header.
  • attributes (String) (Optional): HTML attributes for the tab header.
Usage and Example:

The TabStrip is perfect for organizing different categories of information on a single page without clutter. This example demonstrates a simple "User Profile" page with three tabs: "Profile", "Account", and "Settings". Clicking a tab calls a server function to load the appropriate content into the content area.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
// This function is called by the TabStrip's built-in script.
// The clicked tab element is passed as 'tab'.
function TabClicked(tab) {
    // The tab's innerText (its label) is used to identify which tab was clicked.
    var tabName = tab.innerText;
    var data = [ { key: 'tabName', vlu: tabName } ];
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Crucial Step: Add the required built-in JavaScript file.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TabStripScript)

        ' 1. Create the TabStrip instance.
        Dim userProfileTabs As New TabStrip()
        ' Set the JavaScript function to be called when any tab is clicked.
        userProfileTabs.TabStripClick = "TabClicked(this)"
        ' Set an ID on the content area so we can easily update it.
        userProfileTabs.TabContent.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "tab-content-area")

        ' 2. Add the tabs.
        userProfileTabs.AddTab("Profile")
        userProfileTabs.AddTab("Account")
        userProfileTabs.AddTab("Settings")

        ' 3. Set the initial content that appears before any tab is clicked.
        userProfileTabs.InitialContent = "<h2>Profile</h2><p>This is the user's profile information.</p>"
        
        ' 4. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userProfileTabs.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function TabClicked() As ApiResponse
        Dim tabName As String = GetDataValue("tabName")
        Dim content As String = ""

        ' Determine which content to load based on the clicked tab's name.
        Select Case tabName
            Case "Profile"
                content = "<h2>Profile</h2><p>This is the user's profile information.</p>"
            Case "Account"
                content = "<h2>Account Details</h2><p>Manage your account settings and password.</p>"
            Case "Settings"
                content = "<h2>Application Settings</h2><p>Configure notification preferences.</p>"
        End Select

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' Update the content of the tab's content area.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("tab-content-area", content)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Crucial Step: Add the required built-in JavaScript file.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TabStripScript);

        // 1. Create the TabStrip instance.
        var userProfileTabs = new TabStrip();
        // Set the JavaScript function to be called when any tab is clicked.
        userProfileTabs.TabStripClick = "TabClicked(this)";
        // Set an ID on the content area so we can easily update it.
        userProfileTabs.TabContent.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "tab-content-area");

        // 2. Add the tabs.
        userProfileTabs.AddTab("Profile");
        userProfileTabs.AddTab("Account");
        userProfileTabs.AddTab("Settings");

        // 3. Set the initial content that appears before any tab is clicked.
        userProfileTabs.InitialContent = "<h2>Profile</h2><p>This is the user's profile information.</p>";

        // 4. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userProfileTabs.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse TabClicked()
    {
        string tabName = GetDataValue("tabName");
        string content = "";

        // Determine which content to load based on the clicked tab's name.
        switch (tabName)
        {
            case "Profile":
                content = "<h2>Profile</h2><p>This is the user's profile information.</p>";
                break;
            case "Account":
                content = "<h2>Account Details</h2><p>Manage your account settings and password.</p>";
                break;
            case "Settings":
                content = "<h2>Application Settings</h2><p>Configure notification preferences.</p>";
                break;
        }

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // Update the content of the tab's content area.
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("tab-content-area", content);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.TextArea

  The skilite.ToolKit.TextArea is a server-side UI control that generates an HTML [textarea] element for multi-line text input. It is a composite control that includes an HtmlTag for the main [textarea] element itself (accessible via the Text property) as well as HtmlTags for an associated label and various wrappers. This structure allows for independent styling and attribute setting on both the label and the input area. The control's Alignment property can be set to arrange the label and textarea either vertically (label above) or horizontally (label beside). TextArea is the standard control for capturing larger blocks of user text, such as comments, descriptions, or messages.

Constructor:

  • toolAlignment (Alignments) (Optional): Specifies if the label and textarea are arranged vertically or horizontally. Defaults to Vertical.
Usage and Example:

The TextArea control is essential for any form that requires free-form text entry. This example demonstrates a simple feedback form where a user can type a comment and submit it. The server-side function then displays the submitted comment.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.

function SubmitFeedback() {
    // Get the content from the textarea.
    var feedbackText = document.getElementById('feedback-textarea').value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'feedback', vlu: feedbackText }
    ];
    
    // Call the server-side function with the feedback.
    $ApiRequest(this, JSON.stringify(data));
}

skylite.ToolKit.Texts

  The skilite.ToolKit.Texts is a server-side UI control that generates an HTML [input] element for single-line text entry, along with an associated [label]. It is one of the most frequently used controls for building forms. The control is a composite object that provides access to the inner [input] tag via the Text property and the [label] tag via the Label property, allowing for granular styling and attribute setting. The type of the input (e.g., text, password, email, date) is specified by passing a TextTypes enum value to the constructor. The control's Alignment property can be set to arrange the label and input field either vertically (label above) or horizontally (label beside), providing layout flexibility.

Constructor:
VB
' Constructors
Public Sub New()
Public Sub New(labelText As String)
Public Sub New(labelText As String, textType As TextTypes, Optional toolAlignment As Alignments = Alignments.Vertical)

C#

public Texts(string labelText, TextTypes textType, Alignments toolAlignment = Alignments.Vertical)
public Texts(string labelText, string id, TextTypes textType, Alignments toolAlignment = Alignments.Vertical)
Parameters:
  • labelText (String): The text for the
  • id (String) (Optional): The id attribute for the "input" element.
  • textType (TextTypes): An enum specifying the type attribute of the input (e.g., text, password, email)
  • toolAlignment (Alignments) (Optional): Specifies if the label and input are arranged vertically or horizontally.
Usage and Example:

The Texts control is the standard way to create input fields in a form. This example demonstrates a simple login form with "User ID" and "Password" fields, created using two instances of the Texts control with different TextTypes.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
function Login() {
    var userId = document.getElementById('user-id').value;
    var password = document.getElementById('user-password').value;
    
    var data = [
        { key: 'userId', vlu: userId },
        { key: 'password', vlu: password }
    ];
    
    $ApiRequest('Login', JSON.stringify(data));
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)

Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the 'User ID' text input.
        Dim userIdInput As New Texts("User ID:", "user-id", TextTypes.text, Alignments.Horizontal)
        userIdInput.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "10px")

        ' 2. Create the 'Password' text input.
        ' Note the use of TextTypes.password, which masks the input.
        Dim passwordInput As New Texts("Password:", "user-password", TextTypes.password, Alignments.Horizontal)

        ' 3. Create a submit button.
        Dim loginButton As New Button("Log In", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        loginButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "Login()")
        loginButton.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "15px")

        ' 4. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userIdInput.HtmlText() & passwordInput.HtmlText() & loginButton.HtmlText()
    End Sub

    Public Function Login() As ApiResponse
        Dim userId As String = GetDataValue("userId")
        Dim password As String = GetDataValue("password")

        Dim message As String = ""
        ' Simulate validation
        If userId = "admin" AndAlso password = "pass" Then
            message = "Login Successful!"
        Else
            message = "Invalid credentials."
        End If

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // 1. Create the 'User ID' text input.
        var userIdInput = new Texts("User ID:", "user-id", TextTypes.text, Alignments.Horizontal);
        userIdInput.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "10px");

        // 2. Create the 'Password' text input.
        // Note the use of TextTypes.password, which masks the input.
        var passwordInput = new Texts("Password:", "user-password", TextTypes.password, Alignments.Horizontal);

        // 3. Create a submit button.
        var loginButton = new Button("Log In", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        loginButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "Login()");
        loginButton.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginTop, "15px");

        // 4. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = userIdInput.HtmlText() + passwordInput.HtmlText() + loginButton.HtmlText();
    }

    public ApiResponse Login()
    {
        string userId = GetDataValue("userId");
        string password = GetDataValue("password");

        string message = "";
        // Simulate validation
        if (userId == "admin" && password == "pass")
        {
            message = "Login Successful!";
        }
        else
        {
            message = "Invalid credentials.";
        }

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.MessageBox(message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Timer

  The skilite.ToolKit.Timer is a server-side control that generates an HTML [div] element, but its primary function is to enable a recurring client-side JavaScript interval. It relies on a built-in JavaScript library, which must be included in the page via HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TimerScript) and initialized with HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$StartTimer('TimerId')"). Once started, this script repeatedly calls a specified JavaScript function at a defined interval (e.g., every few seconds). The Timer control provides a way for the server to initiate and manage client-side polling, dynamic updates, or animations that occur without direct user interaction. It's often used in dashboards to refresh data, in chat applications to check for new messages, or for session timeout warnings.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
C#
// Main constructor
public Timer()
Parameters:
  • ID (String): The ID of the generated
    element. This ID is crucial for starting and stopping the timer from JavaScript.
  • Interval (Integer): The time in milliseconds between each execution of the timer's associated JavaScript function.
  • TargetFunction (String): The name of the JavaScript function to be called at each interval.
Usage and Example:

The Timer control is ideal for automatically refreshing data on a dashboard. This example demonstrates a simple clock that updates automatically every second without the user needing to do anything.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
// This function will be called by the Timer repeatedly.
function UpdateClock() {
    // Call the server to get the latest time.
    $ApiRequest();
}

// Function to stop the timer (called by a button for demonstration).
function StopClock() {
    // Call the built-in SkyLite JavaScript function to stop the timer.
    $StopTimer('myClockTimer');
    document.getElementById('clock-display').innerText += " (Stopped)";
}
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit
Imports System

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' Crucial Step 1: Add the required built-in JavaScript file for the Timer.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TimerScript)

        ' A label to display the current time.
        Dim clockDisplay As New Label("Current Time: --:--:--")
        clockDisplay.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "clock-display")
        clockDisplay.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "24px")

        ' 1. Create the Timer control.
        Dim myTimer As New Timer()
        myTimer.ID = "myClockTimer" ' Unique ID for this timer instance.
        myTimer.Interval = 1000 ' Update every 1000 milliseconds (1 second).
        myTimer.TargetFunction = "UpdateClock" ' The JavaScript function to call.

        ' 2. Create a button to manually stop the timer.
        Dim stopButton As New Button("Stop Clock", Button.ButtonTypes.Button)
        stopButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "StopClock()")

        ' 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = clockDisplay.HtmlText() & stopButton.HtmlText() & myTimer.HtmlText()

        ' Crucial Step 2: Initialize the timer script after the page loads.
        HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$StartTimer('myClockTimer')")
    End Sub

    ' This server-side function is called repeatedly by the client-side Timer.
    Public Function UpdateClock() As ApiResponse
        Dim currentTime As String = DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss")
        Dim message As String = "Current Time: " & currentTime

        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("clock-display", message)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function

    ' This function is called by the Stop Clock button.
    Public Function StopClock() As ApiResponse
        Dim _ApiResponse As New ApiResponse()
        ' (No server-side action needed other than confirming it was called.)
        Return _ApiResponse
    End Function
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)
using skylite;
using skylite.ToolKit;
using System;

public class MyPage : WebPage
{
    public override void OnInitialized()
    {
        // Crucial Step 1: Add the required built-in JavaScript file for the Timer.
        HtmlDoc.AddJsFile(WebEnv.HeaderScripts.TimerScript);

        // A label to display the current time.
        var clockDisplay = new Label("Current Time: --:--:--");
        clockDisplay.Wrap.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.id, "clock-display");
        clockDisplay.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "24px");

        // 1. Create the Timer control.
        var myTimer = new Timer();
        myTimer.ID = "myClockTimer"; // Unique ID for this timer instance.
        myTimer.Interval = 1000; // Update every 1000 milliseconds (1 second).
        myTimer.TargetFunction = "UpdateClock"; // The JavaScript function to call.

        // 2. Create a button to manually stop the timer.
        var stopButton = new Button("Stop Clock", Button.ButtonTypes.Button);
        stopButton.SetAttribute(HtmlEvents.onclick, "StopClock()");

        // 3. Add the controls to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = clockDisplay.HtmlText() + stopButton.HtmlText() + myTimer.HtmlText();

        // Crucial Step 2: Initialize the timer script after the page loads.
        HtmlDoc.InitialScripts.ExecuteScript("$StartTimer('myClockTimer')");
    }

    // This server-side function is called repeatedly by the client-side Timer.
    public ApiResponse UpdateClock()
    {
        string currentTime = DateTime.Now.ToString("HH:mm:ss");
        string message = $"Current Time: {currentTime}";

        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        _ApiResponse.SetElementContents("clock-display", message);
        return _ApiResponse;
    }

    // This function is called by the Stop Clock button.
    public ApiResponse StopClock()
    {
        var _ApiResponse = new ApiResponse();
        // (No server-side action needed other than confirming it was called.)
        return _ApiResponse;
    }
}

skylite.ToolKit.Title

  The skilite.ToolKit.Title is a server-side UI control designed to generate a structured and styled page title or header section. It is a composite control that encapsulates several HtmlTag objects for different parts of the title: a LogoImage, a main Caption (the title text), and a Page element (often for subtitles or breadcrumbs). By providing separate objects for each component, the control allows for detailed and independent styling and attribute setting. The entire component is enclosed in a main Wrap [div], making it easy to position the entire title block on the page. Its primary purpose is to provide a consistent and programmatically manageable way to create branded page headers across an application.

Constructor:
VB
' Main constructor
Public Sub New()
C#
// Main constructor
public Title()
Parameters:
  • Caption As skylite.HtmlTag: The main text of the title.
  • LogoImage As skylite.HtmlTag: An "img" tag for a logo.
  • Page As skylite.HtmlTag: An element for a subtitle or secondary text.
  • Wrap As skylite.HtmlTag: The main container "div" for the entire title section.
Usage and Example:

The Title control is used to create a standardized header for your webpages. This example demonstrates building a page title that includes a company logo, a main caption, and a subtitle for the specific page.

Client-Side HTML (MyPage.html)
There is no separate .html file; the UI is built entirely on the server.
JavaScript (myPage.js):
This example is for display purposes only and does not require JavaScript.
VB.NET Server-Side Example (myPage.vb)
Imports skylite
Imports skylite.ToolKit

Public Class MyPage
    Inherits WebPage

    Public Overrides Sub OnInitialized()
        ' 1. Create the Title control instance.
        Dim pageTitle As New Title()

        ' 2. Configure the components of the title.
        ' Configure the logo.
        pageTitle.LogoImage.SetAttribute(HtmlAttributes.src, "/images/company-logo.png")
        pageTitle.LogoImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.height, "40px")
        pageTitle.LogoImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.verticalAlign, "middle")
        pageTitle.LogoImage.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginRight, "15px")

        ' Configure the main caption.
        pageTitle.Caption.InnerText = "My Application"
        pageTitle.Caption.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "28px")
        pageTitle.Caption.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontWeight, "bold")
        
        ' Configure the subtitle/page name.
        pageTitle.Page.InnerText = "Dashboard"
        pageTitle.Page.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.fontSize, "18px")
        pageTitle.Page.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginLeft, "10px")
        pageTitle.Page.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.color, "#555")

        ' 3. Style the main wrapper for the title section.
        pageTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.padding, "20px")
        pageTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.borderBottom, "2px solid #eee")
        pageTitle.Wrap.SetStyle(HtmlStyles.marginBottom, "20px")

        ' 4. Add the final control to the page.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText = pageTitle.HtmlText()

        ' Add some dummy content below the title.
        HtmlDoc.HtmlBodyText &= "<p>This is the main content of the dashboard page.</p>"
    End Sub
End Class
C# Server-Side Example (myPage.cs)

using skylite.ToolKit;
